The data collected is subsequently preserved or shared with healthcare providers for assessment and recommendation \[[16](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_016)\]. RPM, telemedicine, telehealth services, and mobile health are interconnected terms that pertain to the utilization of information technology for monitoring patients in nonhospital environments. RPM is used to study the signals and behaviors of chronically ill patients who are prone to acute symptoms. This intervention offers patients improved treatment outcomes, increased confidence in monitoring acute health episodes, and decreased reliance on hospital visits. Remote monitoring has numerous advantages, such as real-time and continuous symptom observation, early sickness detection or prevention, decreased healthcare expenses, augmented health data, and improved access to emergency medical services \[[4](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_004)\]. The main reason to implement remote health monitoring is to reduce costs, which is driven by causes such as an aging population, growing demands, restricted resources, and the goal of promoting healthy aging \[[17](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_017)\].

A remote monitoring system generally comprises a data collection system, a data processing unit, a user terminal, and a communication network. The data collection system comprises several sensors and gadgets, including smartphones equipped with the internal sensors and wireless communication functionalities. The information processing system enables the exchange of data between a hospital computer and a smartphone database. A communication network is commonly created to facilitate the connection between users, data processing systems, and healthcare professionals \[[18](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_018)\]. In certain situations, patients collect and utilize data on an ongoing basis, occasionally sharing it with healthcare professionals. In contrast, data is regularly shared with medical personnel for continued assessment in different situations. The outcomes are influenced by factors like condition of the patient, recorded information, and data complexity. The patient should be suggested to get medical care, receive first aid, get the medication as directed, or speak with a medical professional. Remote monitoring systems vary in technology, communication path, and sensor integration ([Figure 3.2](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_fig_002)).

![Figure 3.2: Model for the development of systems for the remote monitoring of patients. (Created with BioRender.com)](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-003_fig_002.png)

Figure 3.2: Model for the development of systems for the remote monitoring of patients. (Created with BioRender.com)

## 3.3 Improving therapeutic outcomes via RPM

RPM has demonstrated enhanced clinical outcomes in diverse applications, supported by research investigations and pilot programs. Simultaneously, numerous studies have documented minimal enhancements in clinical outcomes, even when utilizing identical monitoring tools or treating the same disease. Assessing the efficacy of RPM systems is difficult due to their diverse applications and usage across various disease indications. RPM systems typically focus on specific technologies and diseases, highlighting the segmented nature of this field. In the context of diabetes, current conversations in the field of clinical outcomes are mostly concerned with lowering death, morbidity, and tracking biometric markers like HbA1c. However, essential clinical parameters such as adherence are not adequately considered. Adherence is the extent to which an individual adheres to healthcare recommendations and prescriptions. This concerns the patient’s compliance with approved treatment guidelines and prescription drug regimens. Adherence must be recognized as a critical element in the deployment of patient monitoring to guarantee effective management and improved health outcomes \[[19](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_019)\].

The use of remote monitoring systems such as mHealth apps for monitoring adherence has significantly increased in the past decade. This application has emerged as a prominent utilization of modern applications and services. Adherence-promoting tools include various technologies, such as text message alerts as well as sensor-based surveillance systems, using incorporated medical assistance. Researchers performed an extensive review of data from 107 clinical studies to examine the relationship between adherence and patient outcomes. The research indicated that 56% of the studies showed a significant impact on adherence, while 40% displayed substantial effects on clinical outcomes \[[20](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_020)\]. SMS reminders are commonly employed and have proven effective in enhancing adherence. The efficacy of these reminders relies on patient participation as well as data exchange.

Active patient engagement is essential to maintaining high user retention, which in turn leads to improved adherence to treatment plans and better clinical outcomes. Patients frequently lack motivation to enhance adherence due to the additional effort required and the lack of immediate observable outcomes. Encouraging individuals to adopt a new habit can be challenging, particularly when the habit lacks entertainment value or immediate rewards. For adherence tools to effectively influence society, widespread adoption is essential, going beyond clinical trials and pilot studies.

The current adoption rates for most devices and systems have been relatively low, and there are various factors that contribute to this phenomenon \[[21](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_021)\]. Monitoring systems are typically affixed to the body and frequently possess considerable dimensions, necessitating precise placement to guarantee accurate measurements. Connectivity issues, including complications, loss of data, and network connection problems, are commonly experienced in monitoring systems, resulting in a less-than-optimal user experience. Clinicians may question the precision and dependability of clinical decision support when utilizing a tracking system that has primarily undergone development and testing in controlled parameters and simulations. User engagement and interaction are often insufficient. Users commonly view themselves as active data collectors but face restricted access to the collected data, which can impede their acceptance and utilization \[[22](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_022), [23](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_023)\]. To enhance adoption rates, it is essential to refine monitoring systems by prioritizing the end-user experience and ensuring the product delivers value to users. The recommendation and application of healthcare products are dependent upon user awareness and acceptance among patients and clinicians.

## 3.4 Remote patient monitoring architectures

In hospital settings, healthcare professionals routinely monitor the health status of patients and manually document their records. The collection of patients’ vital signs in hospitals is influenced by many factors, including medical workload, staff members’ hours of work, the patients’ examination, clinical leadership, national guidance, etc. \[[24](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_024)\] However, this practice is often constrained by resource limitations. Patient monitoring typically involves the application of invasive devices that necessitate being in contact with the patient’s skin for measuring their vital signs. Technological advancements in information transmission have profoundly altered the healthcare sector by introducing noninvasive gadgets that enable continuous patient monitoring without physical contact. Innovations have transformed the monitoring of patients’ health, enabling remote monitoring in diverse healthcare settings, including healthcare facilities, patients’ facilities, elderly care facilities, and homes. This section explores the designs of various technology-enabled RPM system.

### 3.4.1 Video-based monitoring

Telehealth monitoring enables patients to engage in remote communication with healthcare providers using smart devices through audio or video calls.

Telehealth has become a widely used method to prioritize the protection of patients and healthcare providers during the COVID-19 pandemic. In telehealth tracking, machine learning and image processing are very important. Different critical signs of patients, like heart rate, respiration, oxygen saturation (SpO[2]), cough analysis, and blood pressure, can be tracked using AI methods. An investigation was conducted by researchers that compared the implementation of AI-enabled telehealth for the tracking of vital signs with traditional methods of vital sign monitoring \[[25](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_025)\]. In order to identify facial characteristics on the patient’s image or video, ML algorithms were implemented to establish a region of interest (ROI). After that, attention was moved to the chosen ROI so that important indicators like heartbeat and breathing rate could be measured. The researchers created a CNN model to identify individuals’ psychological stress levels by analyzing their breathing patterns through video surveillance \[[26](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_026)\]. Auscultation sounds were used to analyze coughs. A 3D ResNet18 neural network model that was previously instructed was used in the study to put sounds into groups that correspond to different types of illness. With 100% sensitivity and 94.11% specificity, the model was able to get an accuracy of 94.57%. Remote photoplethysmography was used to find out the heart rate, blood volume, pulse, and SpO[2] measure \[[27](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_027)\]. This information was obtained from a single video frame captured with a standard smartphone camera. The Viola-Jones algorithm was employed to assess the variation in blood volume pulse and its impact on blood absorption during a heartbeat, with a specific emphasis on the forehead’s ROI. The PPG signal and actual heart rate were fed into a feed-forward neural network model. The blood pressure extraction model attained an accuracy rate of 85%.

Research indicates that telemedicine lowers travel time, clinic visits, and the necessity for prolonged absence from work \[[28](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_028)\]. Nevertheless, telehealth monitoring presents certain challenges despite its advantages. The overuse or improper use of telehealth services has led to an escalation in healthcare expenses for providers \[[29](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_029)\]. The availability of internet and technology has exacerbated the disparities between rural and urban populations in terms of access to telehealth monitoring \[[30](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_030)\]. Telehealth monitoring poses a security barrier for patient data due to the absence of a secure, end-to-end encrypted communication service, hence jeopardizing patients’ health information \[[31](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_031)\]. Telehealth patient monitoring techniques can facilitate the diagnosis of patients’ medical issues. AI-enabled telemedicine tracking is an advanced approach for categorizing and forecasting patients’ vital signs.

### 3.4.2 IoT-enabled devices

Continuous real-time patient monitoring is made possible by the installation of an IoT-dependent RPM system \[[32](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_032)\]. Most IoT technology systems are primarily designed for deployment in hospitals or private residences. Nevertheless, there are instances where a unified system could be easily implemented for both cases. The architectural structure is segmented into three distinct sections \[[33](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_033)\]. Section A demonstrates the utilization of wearable devices for the surveillance of patients’ vital signs, including blood pressure, heart rate, respiratory rate, body temperature, and other significant metrics. Section B will use cloud services to store \[[34](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_034)\] patient data for future analysis. This research will utilize machine learning approaches to predict and categorize the data. Section C of the architecture integrates established threshold values of vital signs to forecast and notify medical workers or healthcare experts of anticipated aberrant occurrences in the near future \[[35](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_035)\]. The IoT can link wearable sensors and reader antennas to the patient’s body, facilitating their integration into a tracking network. Wearable technologies for monitoring vital signs include evaluations including the electrocardiogram (ECG), oxygen saturation in the blood, glucose levels in the blood, skin perspiration, capnography, movement evaluation, and cardiac implanted gadgets \[[3](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_003)\]. Additionally, the gadgets may be worn as adhesive patches, chest straps, or wearable shirts. To treat the patient and reduce any potential problems, medical personnel would use necessary interventions.

There has been research on the advancements and issues surrounding the development of wireless body area networks (WBANs), a subset of the IoT that links wireless sensors on a patient’s body to a network \[[36](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_036)\]. WBANs encounter challenges in spread procedures, information security, and privacy, interoperability, and integration. To enable data transfer via wireless sensors attached to the human body, to local and worldwide networks, it is imperative to employ established data transmission technologies such as ZigBee, LoRa, Wi-Fi, and Bluetooth \[[37](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_037)\]. It is crucial to acknowledge that these networks possess inherent limitations regarding energy consumption as well as transmission range. Research is being performed to investigate issues related to privacy and security in technology-enabled applications, specifically in the context of data transmission and transactions. The incorporation of sensors and remote devices has enhanced sensor connectivity, while the incorporation of diverse information sources has allowed information exchange within wireless sensor networks.

RPM sensors necessitate direct skin contact with patients in order to obtain vital signs, thereby restricting the daily activities of individuals with chronic illnesses. RFID (radio frequency identification) technology implementation resolves accuracy concerns in RPM. RFID technology includes active tags with batteries and passive tags without batteries, which function using the near-field coherent sensing (NCS) principle. Cornell University researchers have developed noninvasive digital technology using NCS to enhance patient comfort and reduce limitations on their everyday lives \[[38](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_038)\]. The NCS technique, created by researchers, allows for the immediate modulation of mechanical motion on and within an object onto multiplexed radio signals. This is incorporated with a distinctive digital identifying scheme. This system uses electromagnetic energy to enter body tissue, pick up internal organ signal reflections, and amplify the signals. Mechanical motions with shorter wavelengths in the body can be transformed into substantial phase variations, thereby increasing sensitivity. NCS mechanisms were utilized to monitor vital signs, assess sleep quality \[[39](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_039)\], and accurately measure heartbeat intervals \[[40](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_040)\].

### 3.4.3 Cloud computing

Cloud computing plays an essential function in the performance of continuing patient monitoring systems. The storing of data produced by IoT devices in RPM systems is necessary for data sharing and trend analysis among various stakeholders \[[41](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_041)\]. Cloud computing is a powerful platform that facilitates the storage of servers, databases, networking, software, and intelligence online, facilitating rapid innovation and flexible resource allocation. Researchers created an IoT-enabled patient monitoring system by leveraging cloud computing capabilities \[[42](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_042)\]. The IoT platform’s data is conveyed to the cloud via the 5G network. The research employed a long short-term memory (LSTM) neural network model to observe patients and categorize their health statuses. The deep learning model surpassed the baseline models, achieving an accuracy rate of 97.13%. Cloud computing employs a centralized data server to efficiently manage substantial volumes of data from many IoT devices. Integrating the IoT platform with cloud computing presents challenges related to latency, real-time response delays, bandwidth utilization, and information security. The emergence of fog computing and edge computing has addressed the challenges of cloud computing by decentralizing or distributing computing processes, bringing cloud services closer to IoT networks \[[43](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_043)\]. The implementation of encryption at the level of IoT devices can effectively address data security concerns in cloud computing. This involves encrypting data prior to its transmission to cloud storage for subsequent analysis. The AES algorithm was implemented by researchers to encrypt patient’s vital signs prior to their transmission to the cloud \[[44](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_044)\]. Access to the data is restricted to authorized medical organization servers, which can only be obtained through a confidential decryption key that is distributed exclusively among the system and the healthcare center. Root-mean-square error values of 0.012, mean absolute error values of 0.009, and mean relative error values of 0.003 all show that the suggested method outperforms existing commercial devices.

### 3.4.4 Fog and edge computing

Fog computing is a kind of cloud computing designed to deliver cloud services nearer to IoT devices. The implementation of IoT and integrated cloud computing for continuous tracking of person’s vital signs and physical activity has elicited concerns regarding security, performance, latency, and network disturbances in cloud computing \[[45](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_045)\]. Fog computing functions as a middleman between IoT devices and the cloud, employing a distributed or decentralized virtual network \[[46](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_046)\]. Researchers investigated the system architectures of IoT-Fog in the healthcare sector \[[47](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_047)\]. Delay and latency challenges in cloud computing are mitigated by fog nodes, which swiftly evaluate data from the IoT platform \[[48](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_048)\]. By keeping private data safe and running computations close to the IoT platform, the fog computing design improves data analysis and security.

Edge computing is a decentralized concept that aims to bring cloud computing services closer to networks or IoT devices. In order for edge computing to work, smart devices like programmable computers need to be able to talk to IoT devices. Edge computing units are placed close to the network in a smart way and can store and process data. Cloud, fog, and edge monitoring architectures provide decentralized real-time surveillance for customized healthcare. Nonetheless, these technologies possess limits.

A system was proposed by researchers that uses wearable sensors as well as an AI-enabled edge instrument to predict the physical activity of patients \[[49](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_049)\]. The edge gadget utilized a graphics processing unit for enhanced computational performance. An LSTM deep learning model was employed at the edge for assessing physical activity. The model exhibited exceptional performance, achieving an accuracy of 99.69%. This surpassed conventional methods such as the hidden Markov model and deep belief network, which attained an average accuracy of 92.01%. The researchers suggested an AI-driven method for detecting falls in elderly patients within a remote health monitoring framework \[[50](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_050)\]. The method involves the processing of binary images of individuals. The proposed methodology consists of five tiers: a sensor layer attached to the elderly patient’s body, an edge gateway, a fog node layer utilizing LoRa connection, a cloud layer, and an application layer for user access. A comparative analysis was performed to assess the effectiveness of a DL CNN model compared to support vector machines (SVM) and ANNs in image processing. The deep learning model was 98% accurate and took 65 dB to process.

### 3.4.5 Blockchain monitoring

There have been reports of security and privacy issues with virtual technologies such as fog and edge computing \[[51](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_051)\]. The researchers suggested a healthcare architecture that utilizes authorized blockchain technology to tackle these challenges \[[52](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_052)\]. A study investigated the potential advantages of blockchain technology in healthcare and the potential for decentralized AI to collaborate with blockchain-based systems. The “blockchain,” a distributed ledger, enables the exchange of transparent and reliable transactions among multiple users. Blockchain technology has the potential to enhance the integrity of data exchanges in RPM applications when it comes to patient interactions with monitoring technologies such as fog, cloud, and edge computing. The researchers constructed a data repository on the Ethereum platform for use with electronic health records (EHRs) and RPM \[[53](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_053)\]. The data repository enabled the secure uploading, storing, analysis, retrieval, and transmission of patient data in accordance with the patient’s instructions. The decentralized blockchain system provides support for both hospitalized patients and outpatients. Researchers propose MedHypChain as a solution to address the challenge of interoperability in cloud computing \[[54](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_054)\]. MedHypChain is a privacy-focused medical data exchange platform. The platform is based on Hyperledger fabric and uses an identity-based broadcast group encryption technique to guarantee the security of all data transactions. The SynCare app, developed by researchers, allow for the safe capture of data and the coordination of RPM \[[55](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_055)\]. The study examined the integration of patients, healthcare professionals, and carers, establishing secure channels for sharing data, and enabling patients to manage their health information. Blockchains are recognized for their strong security characteristics that ensure the immutability of recorded transactions. The architecture is characterized by its high implementation complexity, intricate integration, and significant energy dependence, which are considered as disadvantages.

Technology-enabled RPM systems prioritize data acquisition and secure transmission to relevant groups involved in RPM. The incorporation of AI into RPM architectures improves the monitoring process through the ability to predict and classify patient data. AI modeling can enhance the data analytics step of an RPM architecture.

## 3.5 Application of artificial intelligence in RPM

### 3.5.1 Sensors, smartphones, apps, and devices for RPM

Technology such as social media, sensors, smartphone applications, and location monitoring that incorporates and uses AI and the IoT can help people with chronic conditions with early detection, treatment, and better self-management ([Figure 3.3](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_fig_003)). There are three main types of remote healthcare sensing systems: passive, active, and functional assessment. Smartphones are commonly employed for passive sensing. Smartphones are equipped with built-in sensors, including an accelerometer, gyroscope, and magnetometer. These sensors facilitate physics-based functionalities like geographic localization as well as step counting. Furthermore, they possess the ability to collect data pertaining to atmospheric pressure, voice, ambient light, and touchscreen pressure. Furthermore, the smartphone’s built-in camera can be utilized for various innovative purposes using these sensors. For example, it can be utilized as a fall detector \[[56](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_056)\] or as a spirometer \[[57](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_057)\] using a heart rate monitor or the microphone’s recorded sound as input \[[58](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_058)\].

![Figure 3.3: Wearable technology in healthcare. (Created with BioRender.com)](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-003_fig_003.png)

Figure 3.3: Wearable technology in healthcare. (Created with BioRender.com)

The proliferation of wearable technology has led to the development of sensors that can compete with smartphones in terms of functionality. One example is wrist sensors. They have the capability to detect various types of motion, including smoking-related movements \[[59](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_059)\] and seizure activity \[[60](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_060)\]. Research conducted in 2017 indicated that approximately 17% of American people made use of wearable technology, such as electronic watches or health bands worn on the wrist \[[61](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_061)\]. One common type of wrist sensor is the photoplethysmographic sensor, which measures the rhythmic changes in microvascular blood flow as they occur in response to the heartbeat. This study looked at the characteristics related to the heart’s rhythm \[[62](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_062)\]. Clinicians employ wearable sensors for diverse applications. Wearable patches have the capability to measure muscle activity and posture \[[63](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_063)\]. Pulmonary function can be detected using radiofrequency sensors positioned over clothing \[[64](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_064)\]. Wearable sensors have the capability to evaluate the progress of recovery in different neurological disorders \[[65](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_065)\]. An innovative technology has been created to enhance medication adherence through the use of a small sensor integrated within a pill. The sensor transmits signals to a body-worn patch upon pill ingestion \[[66](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_066)\]. The patient tracking procedure is an example of active sensing, whereas passive sensing merely gathers observable data. Active sensing involves using questionnaires to gather information about a patient’s experiences at regular intervals, allowing for the subjective evaluation of their health condition. The inclusion of patient-reported data in AI models is crucial for ensuring accurate assessment of results, as it holds equal importance to measured data.

The employment of device sensors to gather objective data and the incorporation of subjective patient reporting are both components of functional assessments, which integrate passive and active sensing approaches. Patients must use mobile health technology to evaluate their functional performance through the completion of standardized tasks. Several potential solutions have been proposed. These include utilizing smartphone motion sensors to calculate performance on the 6-min walk test \[[67](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_067)\], using a microphone of a smartphone to evaluate parkinsonian voice vibrations, as well as assessing cognitive abilities such as memories and response time by mobile applications \[[68](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_068)\]. Most cognitive function evaluation applications for mobile devices are brain training applications that utilize mental stimulation. Cognitive abilities including focus, memory, speed, and problem-solving capacity have been demonstrated to be significantly enhanced by a number of mental health treatments. Individuals’ mental health is positively affected by these interventions. Lumosity and Elevate, among the initial apps available, utilize entertaining mini games created by scholars to enhance brain neuroplasticity. The incorporation of AI in these applications offers users an intuitive interface for monitoring timely assessment of their cognitive function. Chatbot coaches are an important implementation of AI in the domain of cognitive and mental health monitoring. A number of mobile health apps use conversational AI chatbots to provide emotional support and track mental health using smartphones. These apps include WoeBot, Wysa, Youper, Replika, and Unmind \[[69](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_069)\]. Chatbots utilize AI-based algorithms trained and evaluated with tools based on cognitive behavioral therapy, which have been created by researchers in the fields of psychology as well as mental health.

### 3.5.2 Clinical decision support-based RPM

Many chronic diseases, including chronic obstructive pulmonary disease, have prompted the development of clinical decision support systems (CDSSs) for remote monitoring of patients at home \[[70](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_070)\]. The CDSS primarily aims to efficiently oversee the patient’s health status through regular monitoring. This allows for the early identification and prevention of any disease recurrence or worsening \[[71](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_071)\]. CDSS employs computable medical research information, specific to the patient data, and integrated AI models, to support clinicians by providing pertinent information and helping with decision-making \[[72](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_072)\]. The primary goal of the most popular and cutting-edge CDSS is to improve medication safety and adherence. CDSSs consist mainly of electronic prescribing systems integrated into clinical decision support frameworks. Complex algorithms for AI are often unnecessary for providing support. The data available is adequate for clinicians to make informed decisions ([Figure 3.4](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_fig_004)). The amount of patient data stored by CDSSs is substantial \[[73](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_073)\], and the advancement of AI technologies is keeping pace with the proliferation of varied data sources. By employing specialized strategies for feature selection, ML algorithms and neural networks are built to efficiently handle large datasets \[[74](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_074)\]. AI allows CDSS to function at multiple levels through the analysis of extensive datasets. The electronic medical records of the hospital can be utilized by the AI algorithms in a CDSS to forecast emergency admissions in a variety of ways. This method, if put into practice, would improve emergency department management’s decision-making techniques, raise the standard for patients’ emergency care, and reduce expenses. In addition, by making it easier to monitor a patient’s health state, these systems can enhance their quality of life by allowing for the early detection and prevention of potential future exacerbations.

![Figure 3.4: A comprehensive presentation of a clinical decision support system. (Created with BioRender.com)](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-003_fig_004.png)

Figure 3.4: A comprehensive presentation of a clinical decision support system. (Created with BioRender.com)

Nevertheless, the utilization of a CDSS by clinical staff cannot be assumed, despite its presence. Recent studies demonstrate that new nurses mostly rely on CDSS. As they accumulate experience, they predominantly depend on their own skills and utilize the CDSS for validation \[[75](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_075)\]. According to certain nurses, these recommendations from the system can hinder their professional judgement. Poorly designed recommendations can create conflicts with their experienced decisions \[[76](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_076)\]. Adherence to strict guidelines is crucial when developing and assessing a CDSS. The research suggests that decision-making should adhere to the five rights rules:

1.  Accurate information (including solid AI algorithms, clinical practice norms, and medical knowledge)
    
2.  Appropriate people (clinicians, multidisciplinary team, and patient-needs data for decision-making)
    
3.  The correct presentation format (including alarms, templates, and order sets, prompts, and data buttons to show decision data)
    
4.  The proper channels (using tools like the EHR, sites, and mobile devices)
    
5.  Appropriate timing (identification of the time-related structure in the data and the proper integration of that understanding into the choices being made) \[[77](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_077)\]
    

Satisfaction among medical staff can be achieved when clinicians receive pertinent information that aligns with their professional judgement and does not require additional time or effort.

## 3.6 Others applications of AI in RPM

Conventional ML and DL methods are frequently used in RPM applications for vital sign detection and prediction and patient activity classification. In their study, researchers discuss various methodologies used in RPM, including both contact-based and noncontact-based approaches \[[4](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_004)\]. Irrespective of the type of monitoring devices employed, both contact and noncontact approaches share the common objective of extracting vital indicators of humans, including heart rate, pulse rate, rate of respiration, and oxygen saturation in the blood. A decline in these essential physiological indicators has a substantial influence on the overall well-being of individuals. In addition to monitoring essential human signs, the researchers conducted a literature review on the detection of activities in patients, such as fall detection and diseases related to mobility. The challenges primarily consist of distinguishing intentional rapid movements from unintentional drops. Fall detection systems utilizing ambiance devices and vision-based approaches were also investigated by the researchers, alongside wearable gadgets. Nevertheless, researchers encountered significant obstacles that continue to exist in the realm of contactless monitoring. This section examines the application of ML and DL approaches within the realm of RPM.

### 3.6.1 Monitoring of vital signs

Smartwatches and other wearable devices are innovative technologies that provide continuous monitoring of individuals’ vital signs. Researchers developed a system for continuous health monitoring of individuals using smartwatches connected to a network \[[78](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_078)\]. Smartwatches can collect vital signs from patients and transmit this data to administrators for the purpose of analysis, thus facilitating informed decision-making. The decision model was constructed by the administrator through the utilization of an SVM model. The physicians receive notification of the patient’s status outcomes. An outstanding recall rate of 99% and a classification accuracy of 90% were both attained by the machine learning model. The technology under consideration demonstrates a 99% success rate in accurately identifying people afflicted with cardiovascular ailments. A team of researchers has devised a system based on the RPM technique for the purpose of detecting ECG signals \[[79](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_079)\]. One study encompasses the application of the CatBoost learning program to train a decision tree ensemble classifier. The classifier was trained on a dataset that contained 31 features using a 20-fold cross-validation technique. The CatBoost model, which had endured training, was employed to extract the value of features. The performance evaluation was conducted using the feature importance ranking methodology, with a particular focus on the identification and analysis of the most significant features. A 30-s ECG data was effectively analyzed by the CatBoost model within a processing time of 0.5 s. With a sensitivity of 99.61%, specificity of 99.64%, and accuracy of 99.62%, atrial fibrillation (AF) was successfully identified. The research conducted also involved the creation of a wearable SmartVest system that employs IoT technology to monitor 12-lead ECG signals. The SVM model was employed to assess the signal-to-noise ratio. The average accuracy of the system was 97.9% for ECG segments, which was classified as acceptable, and 96.4% for ECG segments, which was classified as undesirable. The objective of the model’s efficiency evaluation was to evaluate its ability to precisely identify and select ECG segments of superior quality within the wearable device, while simultaneously rejecting those of inferior quality effectively.

ECG monitoring is a technique employed to observe and document the electrical activity of the heart. A telemetry system was created by investigators to monitor cardiac arrhythmia by processing the ECG signal using SVM modeling \[[80](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_080)\]. This technique was designed to promptly notify a medical professional in the event of a critical situation. The study employed dynamic features, specifically heart rate variability (HRV) numbers, calculated from RR intervals, in conjunction with statistical features of ECG signals, to accurately classify cardiac arrhythmia. The SVM classifier model was trained and evaluated by the researchers using a 10-fold cross-validation strategy. The classification model was effective in terms of statistical features, with a 90.8% correlation for HRV features, a 92.2% correlation for both statistical and HRV features, and an 88.9% correlation for statistical characteristics.

A portable ECG device was integrated into an RPM system by the researchers in their study \[[34](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_034)\]. This method is anticipated to simplify the determination of electrocardiographic issues by transferring the data to a cloud service. The cloud service’s intelligent arrhythmia detector (IDAH-ECG) facilitated the identification of aberrant heartbeats and the notification of physicians when they were detected. The work employed data pretreatment techniques, such as principal component analysis, to decrease dimension and discrete wavelet transformations for feature extraction. The multilayer perceptron (MLP) neural network classifier was programmed using the Gradient Descent optimization technique and the backpropagation algorithm. The research study employed a 10-fold cross-validation methodology in conjunction with the Monte Carlo testing approach. The classifier achieved an average accuracy of 96.48%, with a sensitivity of 96.70% and a specificity of 94.50%. DL methodologies were implemented by the researchers to identify pulses from concise ECG segments \[[81](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_081)\]. The researchers implemented a methodology that integrated a variety of ML techniques, such as the auto-encoder, restricted Boltzmann machine, RNN, and DCNN. The objective of the device’s development was to identify a pulse in individuals who were undergoing resuscitation procedures as a result of cardiac arrest. The issue of pulse detection in this specific scenario remains unresolved, despite the superior performance of DNN architectures in comparison to existing methods. A wireless NLOS bio-radar device was developed by researchers to monitor vital signs, such as respiration and heart rate \[[82](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_082)\]. The device functions in a noncontact manner, is compact, and is immune to interference. In order to forecast physiological attributes, edge nodes implemented a DL LSTM model. By employing real-time data within a restricted time frame, the researchers employed LSTM models to predict the short-term respiration rates and heartbeats of patients in the future. In order to anticipate respiration patterns during routine activities, researchers developed a wearable system that integrated numerous modalities \[[83](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_083)\]. Respiratory and exercise parameters, as well as human activity data, were integrated into the system. The study introduced a novel hybrid hierarchical classification system by integrating a threshold-based approach with an LSTM model. The program’s objective was to categorize nine distinct breathing patterns that occur during fifteen distinct forms of physical activity. In comparison to the multiclass SVM, K-nearest neighbor (KNN), and ANN models, the hybrid model demonstrated superior classification performance, with an accuracy of 97.22%. The model that was recommended demonstrated superior performance in comparison to the bidirectional LSTM (Bi-LSTM), LSTM, and DCNN models, all the while maintaining a low computing time of 0.0094 s.

### 3.6.2 Physical activities monitoring

The researchers in their study developed a fall detection system that was specifically designed for the elderly population \[[33](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_033)\]. The system employed multisensor fusion, a technique that involved the integration of data from a number of three-axis acceleration sensors that were strategically positioned on the midsection. The work employed random forest (RF) and SVM algorithms to analyze a dataset of one hundred young, healthy volunteers. The objective of the investigation was to determine the degree to which different algorithms were able to distinguish between real-life activities and simulated falls in terms of recognition rate and time. The model’s accuracy is contingent upon a substantial quantity of precise information, as per the researchers. In spite of this, SVM seems to be more suitable for this specific application due to its faster training and recognition periods. A novel technique was proposed by researchers to effectively classify various stages of falls \[[84](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_084)\]. In order to accurately and efficiently categorize prefall, freefall, impact, resting, and recovery phases, the suggested system combines a fragment modification algorithm with ML techniques. Adaptive boosting, KNN, decision trees, naive Bayes, SVM, and a rule-based approach to fall detection are all part of the algorithm for fragment modification. In order to correctly identify the five separate phases, several methods are used. The KNN approach outperformed the other four models with respect to accuracy (90.28%), sensitivity (82.17%), precision (85.74%), and Jaccard coefficient (73.51%). The researchers hope to enhance their model through integrating empirical data from real-world sources and broaden the range of falls under consideration, so including a more diverse set of scenarios. A framework for the RPM system was developed by researchers, which utilizes a signal reflection model to detect and assess human activities and movement \[[85](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_085)\]. The employed system leveraged RSSI patterns obtained from an array of RFID tags in order to discern and classify human actions. The method utilized the variation of phase readings to partition the phase values, thus serving as a signal for segmenting activity. The study involved the training of six ML classifiers, specifically decision tree, RF, MLP-based neural network, SVM, Naive Bayes, and quadratic discriminant analysis. The classifiers underwent training to classify a range of activities, encompassing actions such as raising a hand, lowering a hand, standing, walking, seated, falling, turning, rising up, and nonactivity. The experimental findings demonstrate the robustness of TACT across various experimental conditions, yielding an average recognition precision of 93.5%.

Researchers developed a microcontroller with limited resources and placed at the network edge \[[86](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_086)\]. This microcontroller utilizes a wearable accelerometer to address challenges related to latency, excessive power usage, and suboptimal performance in regions with unreliable internet connectivity. The study used a three-tier architecture with a fog layer, a cloud layer, and an edge layer. By utilizing long-range communication technologies, these layers aimed to improve the efficiency of data collection, analysis, and transmission to an IoT gateway. For the goal of fall detection, a study was conducted where five AI models were developed. These models include KNN, LSTM, SVM, and CNN. The LSTM was able to detect falls during everyday activities with a high accuracy of 96.78%. It also showed a high level of specificity (95.21%) and sensitivity (97.87%).

In order to detect mobility, the researchers developed a system that makes use of passive RFID tags. Existing fall detection approaches for seniors either require specialized equipment or disrupt individuals’ regular activities; this system intends to solve these constraints \[[87](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_087)\]. The estimation of RSSI and Doppler frequency values was found to be affected by a range of circumstances, including static conditions, regular physical activity, abrupt changes in movement, movements associated with elderly individuals, and actions related to falls. The signal preprocessing stage employed wavelet transform, while the categorization of events into fall detection or other activities was accomplished by the utilization of the ML technique, SVM. RFID technology enables invisible tracking of the movements and detection of falls among elderly individuals, without disrupting their daily activities. The domain of HCI has witnessed a growing interest in the study and application of gesture recognition and motion detection techniques. These technologies have been widely explored with the aim of enhancing the overall user experience. An application can be utilized in the healthcare industry to identify and analyze patient gestures or motions in various environments, such as homes and hospitals. This application may employ a system that eliminates the need for physical hardware.

## 3.7 AI-based RPM for chronic disease monitoring

### 3.7.1 Diabetes monitoring

Beyond the traditional indicators of glucose, age, body mass index (BMI), and insulin, researchers developed a diabetes prediction model to categorize the disease \[[88](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_088)\]. In order to forecast the occurrence of diabetes, their research made use of various ML techniques. Using a classification report and a confusion matrix, the models were trained and evaluated. The accuracy rate of the logistic regression model in determining if a person has diabetes or not was 96%. The goal is to reliably evaluate sensor data pertaining to numerous elements of diabetes patients, such as blood sugar levels, sleep time, heart rate, food consumption, and exercise. To classify the health risk status into four separate levels – low, medium, high, and extreme – the data was analyzed using neural networks \[[89](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_089)\]. The risk variables for type II diabetic mellitus (T2DM) were determined using ML algorithms in conjunction with a nationwide physical examination. Physical measures and questionnaire data were analyzed using a logistic regression model. Decision trees, AdaBoosts, XGBoosts, and RFs are all examples of tree-based machine learning techniques that were considered while choosing the 14 risk variables. Among the four algorithms considered, XGBoost demonstrated notable performance metrics. The model demonstrated a level of performance characterized by a precision of 91.0%, accuracy of 90.6%, F1 score of 90.6%, recall of 90.2%, and an AUC of 96.8%. The feature-significant scores were computed using the XGBoost method. Many important factors were found to significantly affect health outcomes according to the study’s results. Some of these factors include: race, ethnic background, systolic blood pressure, smoking history, dietary preference for oil-rich meals, alcohol status, nutrition ratio of meat to fruits and vegetables, smoking scale, high blood pressure, the degree of physical activity, and BMI \[[90](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_090)\]. Various ML algorithms have exhibited the capacity to effectively distinguish the distinct states of individuals with diabetes.

### 3.7.2 Cardiac monitoring

Several areas of cardiovascular care, including diagnosis, treatment, and RPM, show promise for AI applications. Two primary areas of research pertain to the applications: the physical and the virtual. Virtual research encompasses DL, ML, and NLP (natural language processing) as they pertain to computerized CDSSs and patient monitoring. The utilization of AI in the physical realm is predominantly centered around the deployment of robotic for surgical operations.

There are three main uses for AI in imaging, namely picture analysis, reconstruction, and interpretation. The utilization of modern AI algorithms on extensive quantities of imaging data has enabled the processing of images by generating intricate structural frameworks for interpreting them \[[91](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_091)\]. Numerous AI-driven methods have been suggested to automate distinct image processing procedures. A study was undertaken by researchers in which deep neural networks were trained using a dataset consisting of 17,800 coronary angiograms obtained from a total of 3,900 patients \[[92](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_092)\]. Subsequently, the efficacy of the trained models was assessed by conducting validation on a dataset consisting of 27,900 angiograms obtained from a cohort of 6,250 patients. The main objective of this study was to find a way to automatically determine the end-diastolic frame and precisely determine the cardiac phase. Certain AI systems employ ML methodologies. An ML methodology has been developed by researchers affiliated with Emory University for the purpose of segmenting intravascular ultrasound pictures. This approach enables the digitalization of lumen area and plaque burden estimations in an online setting. The approach showed favorable results in the task of picture segmentation and garnered favorable feedback from professionals in the field \[[93](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_093)\].

The integration of DL and ML methodologies inside intelligent AI systems is being suggested by researchers as a potential means to enable the automated diagnosis and monitoring of diverse illnesses that rely on imaging techniques. The objective of this method is to emulate the knowledge and proficiency exhibited by human specialists.

In the domain of cardiovascular medicine, CDSSs are advancing toward the development of autonomous learning capabilities. This is accomplished through the application of ML, DL, and NLP methodologies. The objective is to replicate a decision-making process that closely emulates the cognitive capabilities of human experts. The cognitive technology, known as IBM’s Watson for Health, incorporates a wide range of information gathered from several resources, comprising healthcare documents such as EMR, laboratory results, and radiology reports, along with web data sources \[[94](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_094)\]. These recently created AI systems mainly use ML and DL techniques to mimic the way the human brain makes decisions. IBM is currently working on the development of an intelligent assistant for cardiologists and radiologists in the field of cardiovascular medicine, aimed at enhancing the way they make their decisions. The IBM Medical Sieve project encompasses various features for cardiac monitoring, particularly in imaging. One key aspect is the ability to automatically detect coronary stenoses during angiograms \[[95](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_095)\].

AI methodologies in the field of cardiology are frequently employed in tandem with VR and AR techniques. The primary use cases for virtual reality frameworks are in the periprocedural and pre-procedural phases of procedures, such as cardiac interventions and patient stress management before procedures \[[96](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_096)\]. AR systems are employed to assist interventionists in cardiac surgeries by delivering up-to-date information via monitor screens \[[97](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_097)\]. Virtual assistants that respond to voice commands, including Google Assistant, Apple’s Siri, and Amazon’s Alexa, employ advanced AI algorithms to accomplish precise voice recognition capabilities. Voice-activated virtual assistants provide physicians and operators with the ability to efficiently and simply retrieve data from the EMR system or access internet information by utilizing their voice-based signature passwords \[[98](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_098)\].

### 3.7.3 “Big data” for predictive analysis

The complexity of datasets has experienced a substantial increase due to the growing availability of different and useful data sources, including sequencing data, social media information, and cardiology imaging. Consequently, traditional statistical methods are no longer sufficient for their analysis. The analytical and predictive capacities of AI algorithms that use big data technologies have shown encouraging results \[[99](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_099)\]. Deep neural networks have demonstrated considerable efficacy in the analysis of intricate datasets pertaining to cardiovascular disorders. They possess the ability to identify noteworthy risk indicators and provide precise prediction analyses. ML methodologies could be incorporated in patient monitoring to forecast the likelihood of stroke and mortality risks linked to specific cardiac interventions. ML techniques were implemented in the domain of cardiology to predict the mortality rates of individuals suffering from heart failure within a duration of 1 year, along with forecasting the 5-year death rate for patients who were diagnosed with coronary artery disease \[[100](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_100)\].

### 3.7.4 Mental health monitoring

Mental illness is a disorder that is often underestimated in terms of its impact, and it has been found to potentially decrease life expectancy by a range of 10–20 years \[[101](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_101)\]. Patients with mental health issues, such as schizophrenia, bipolar illness, major depressive disorder, or thoughts of suicide, would be extremely challenging to manually monitor. Researchers set out to conduct a comprehensive analysis of the use of ML methods in the diagnosis, evaluation, and treatment of mental health disorders \[[102](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_102)\]. The application of machine learning techniques could speed up the discovery of new ways to understand and assess human behavior patterns, and assist in the detection of mental health-related symptoms and risk factors. Furthermore, they aid in disease progression prediction and in the customization and optimization of therapies. The objective of recent developments in machine learning is to utilize relevant data analysis techniques to anticipate instances of suicide and afterwards provide valuable insights for clinical application. A text-mining approach was presented by researchers to provide support for risk assessment. The field of NLP makes use of latent Dirichlet allocation, a subset of topic modeling \[[103](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_103)\]. The utilization of technology has enabled the efficient handling and management of diverse forms of data, encompassing demographics, progress notes, appointments, complete referrals, assessments, and inpatient stays. The statistically equivalent signatures mechanism is a technique utilized in the process of feature selection. The research used K-fold cross-validation with the following models: support vector regression, RF, and linear ridge regression. The method employed resulted in the attainment of the maximum AUC value, which was determined to be 0.705. By utilizing machine learning technologies, abnormal vital sign measurements can be captured by continuous monitoring, and then analyzed to find underlying patterns that guide appropriate treatment strategies.

### 3.7.5 Dementia monitoring

A demographic change marked by an aging population is seen in Western nations as a result of a notable increase in life expectancy and a corresponding fall in birth rates. Simultaneously, the management of challenges is increasingly difficult due to shortages in medical personnel, particularly doctors, nurses, and carers for the elderly \[[104](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_104)\]. The challenge of providing high-quality nursing care has a significant impact on individuals affected by age-related illnesses, including those with dementia. Currently, there is a global prevalence of 50 million individuals affected by dementia, with an annual incidence of about 10 million new cases \[[105](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_105)\]. Therefore, it is crucial to exert significant efforts in improving nursing care services to guarantee that individuals with dementia receive sufficient care and safeguarding, enabling them to independently pursue a normal way of life. The majority of individuals with dementia receive care in their own homes, with the necessary amount of support, varying according to the stage of the disease. These demands have the potential to be transient, although they can also impose significant challenges when carers lack the essential resources and exertions \[[106](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_106)\]. Dementia management necessitates coordination across multiple disciplines and sectors. In practice, insufficient coordination in care professions can be attributed to delays or a lack of information sharing among participants \[[107](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_107)\]. This encourages the development of comprehensive support networks that incorporate primary healthcare, telemedicine, and informal care given by family members. By implementing a comparable support system, it becomes possible to automatically detect anomalies in the typical behavior of individuals with dementia or urgent situations, facilitating the coordination of necessary interventions. The German clinical study, “SAKI,” serves as an illustrative instance of a comparable support system that specifically targets the complexities associated with dementia and endeavors to fulfil the requirements of patients inside the confines of their own residences.

Most current AI-based dementia monitoring systems focus on keeping checks on how the disease is progressing and giving patients the help they need to continue with their everyday activities \[[108](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_108)\]. “Cognitive Orthosis for Assisting Activities” (COACH) is one example of a home monitoring system developed for people with dementia \[[109](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_109)\]. Helping people with dementia more efficiently do the daily task of washing their hands was the driving force behind the development of this method. The method employs AI algorithms and computer vision techniques to observe and assess the handwashing activity of individuals diagnosed with dementia. It determines the appropriate verbal or visual prompts required for intervention. One concept that emerged from the research is the idea of “zero-effort technologies,” which refers to tools that are essentially effortless to use. This concept has subsequently inspired the development of numerous systems \[[110](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_110)\]. Data collection and analysis are the goals of these technological advancements, which employ a wide range of ICTs, such as computer vision, sensor fusion, AI, and the IoT. The objective is to ensure that these technologies do not intrude on individuals’ privacy. Current research is increasingly focused on incorporating AI models into support systems to analyze individual emotions and identities, aiming to seamlessly integrate these systems into users’ lives \[[111](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_111)\].

The combination of AI and IoT support systems in residential settings has given rise to the concept of smart homes (refer to [Figure 3.5](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_fig_005)). The integration of AI with the IoT has led to notable progress in the development of smart houses. These advancements have introduced innovative support systems that are based on specific activities such as cooking and dressing \[[112](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_112)\]. The Gloucester Smart Home \[[113](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_113)\] represents an initial prototype of smart homes that have been specifically developed must meet the requirements of those diagnosed with dementia. The system comprises many components such as monitors for the bath and oven, an object locator, a digital notice board, and a controlled lighting system designed for the purpose of conveying visual and audible instructions. The concept integrates voice prompts as a means of alerting patients to potentially dangerous situations, such as instances where stoves are left on or when night-time roaming occurs. The study implemented a smart home installation to assess the impact of an autonomous remote surveillance system on the standard of life of people with severe dementia who reside in a care facility. This technology has exhibited promising results in augmenting patient autonomy through the examination of data collected through sensors and questionnaires. The smart home system resulted in improvements for the client, including regaining urinary continence, increasing sleep duration by two hours per night, and reducing night-time wanderings by 50% \[[114](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_114)\].

![Figure 3.5: The diagram illustrating the idea of smart homes for the purpose of RPM. (Created with BioRender.com)](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-003_fig_005.png)

Figure 3.5: The diagram illustrating the idea of smart homes for the purpose of RPM. (Created with BioRender.com)

Currently, AI algorithms have enhanced the intelligence of smart homes, enabling increased user interactivity. The integration of advanced AI algorithms allows smart home devices to gather information regarding user behavior and subsequently modify their operations, thus permitting automated adaptations to cater to individual preferences. The research community is currently focused on developing robots that exhibit human-like behavior, rather than physical appearance. It is anticipated that in the future, artificially intelligent robots will be prevalent in smart homes, assisting individuals in various aspects of their daily routines. Japan has implemented nurse robots in healthcare settings to provide patient care \[[115](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_115)\]. There are now smart robots that can help people with physical tasks and also address their emotional well-being. This holds particular significance for persons who require assistance, such as those diagnosed with dementia. An example of such a robot is the PARO robot, which resembles a seal toy and offers interactive and comforting experiences, similar to animal therapy. This can help reduce stress and provide emotional support to users.

### 3.7.6 Migraine monitoring

Migraine is a persistent neurological condition characterized by repeating episodes of headache, sometimes associated by symptoms such as vomiting, nausea, and increased sensitivity to sound and light \[[116](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_116)\]. On a worldwide scale, it is generally recognized as the third most common disease and the seventh most debilitating issue related to neurological disorders. Migraine is a multifaceted ailment characterized by various contributing elements, which might complicate its accurate diagnosis due to the potential for symptom overlap with other disorders such as seizures and headaches caused by tension. Over the past few years, a substantial amount of study has been dedicated to addressing the challenge of accurately recognizing and diagnosing migraines. In this setting, the flash simulation method has demonstrated encouraging outcomes. The suggested method makes use of a multichannel electroencephalography (EEG) equipment to study patients’ brain responses to flash stimulation at various frequencies. Based on the research, the most accurate results are obtained with a flash frequency of 4 Hz \[[117](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_117)\]. Furthermore, the implementation of support systems has been proposed to aid in the clinical identification of headaches caused by tension and subsequent migraines. The presented findings demonstrated a reasonable level of accuracy, as evidenced by performance indicators such as recall rate, precision rate, *F*\-score, and accuracy \[[118](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_118)\].

Numerous AI initiatives exist that aim to manage migraines through the use of mobile applications or smart devices. AI-based methods in big data analysis have utilized real-time data from individuals suffering from migraines. The identification of migraine signals was accomplished by the utilization of wavelet-based characteristics. Using neural networks, the researchers were able to divide electroencephalogram (EEG) patterns into two groups: those that occurred during times when headaches were not present and those that occurred during actual headache attacks \[[119](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_119)\]. A decision-support system using ML models was suggested for the classification of different types of headaches, according to a recent study \[[120](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_120)\]. The technique successfully distinguished different types of headaches, such as tension headaches and migraines. The field of migraine identification faces numerous challenges, primarily due to the symptomatic nature of the condition. Still, research has shown that when it comes to managing massive volumes of data relevant to migraine sufferers, a mix of AI and IoT technologies can work effectively \[[121](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_121)\].

Currently, researchers in the field of migraine are increasingly prioritizing the development of mobile health applications with predictive capabilities. These applications aim to notify patients in advance of impending migraine episodes. Mobile applications that are powered by AI employ a range of big data algorithms in order to develop precise predictive models. These models exhibit improved precision when comprehensive datasets incorporating several datasets are collected. A collaborative research endeavor combining Mayo Clinic, University of Southern California, and Second Opinion Health has resulted in the creation of a mobile health application known as MigraineAlert. In order to create personalized prediction models for the detection of daily migraine attacks, the app makes use of ML approaches. The AI models utilized inside this application underwent training using an extensive dataset encompassing many aspects such as migraine symptoms, triggers, daily exposures, and physiological data obtained from Fitbit devices. The dataset utilized in the study was obtained from a clinical trial that was undertaken by external partners \[[122](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_122)\].

### 3.7.7 Monitoring in emergency department

The incorporation of ML techniques in the decision-making process for emergency department patients within the context of RPM has the potential to improve existing procedures. In order to predict the in-hospital death rate of sepsis patients, the researchers compared a clinical decision rule with a machine learning method \[[123](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_123)\]. Several variables were extracted from preexisting emergency room clinical records using machine learning approaches in the study. The research set out to estimate patient outcomes and open the way for their incorporation and use in clinical decision support systems. Using a random method, the patients who went to the emergency room were divided into two groups: one was to receive training, and the other was to receive validation. About 80% of the patients were put into the training group and 20% into the validation group. From four hospitals’ real-time clinical data, 500 clinical characteristics were extracted using an RF model. The purpose of this data extraction was to estimate the probability of mortality while hospitalized. The next step was to evaluate the RF model’s efficacy in comparison to logistic and CART regression methods. The AUC of the RF model was statistically significantly different from all other models (*p* < 0.003 for all comparisons). The evidential reasoning approach (RIMER) has been employed by researchers to create and validate a decision-making tool for the prediction of trauma outcomes \[[124](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_124)\]. This method can assist physicians in predicting the probability of mortality during hospitalization and ICU admissions for trauma patients who are evaluated in emergency rooms. In order to evaluate and contrast the predicted accuracy of RIMER, a variety of models were implemented, including logistic regression, ANNs, and SVMs. After conducting a five-fold cross-validation procedure, the researchers obtained the following AUC values: RIMER (0.952), SVM (0.821), logistic regression (0.885), and ANN (0.790). The results indicate that the RIMER model exhibits superior performance. By analyzing the vital signs of emergency department patients, ML techniques have the ability to classify near-term mortality.

The machine learning framework employs HRV to closely monitor critically ailing patients in the emergency room. This involves the assessment of the availability and sufficiency of resuscitation resources, as well as the implementation of prompt intervention strategies. In order to evaluate the efficacy of this strategy, a comparative analysis of metrics, including AUC, specificity, and sensitivity, in relation to the modified early warning score (MEWS), can be conducted. In a study \[[125](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_125)\], HRV values were generated using five-minute ECG recordings. The aforementioned criteria were combined with age and vital signs to determine the ML score for each patient. The AUC for ML scores was 0.781 for the prediction of cardiac arrest within 72 h, while the AUROC for the MEWS was 0.680. The ML score had an AUC of 0.741 when it came to forecasting in-hospital mortality, while the MEWS had an AUC of 0.693. The ML score of 60 or higher was found to be highly accurate in predicting the incidence of cardiac arrests. The negative predictive value was 98.8%, the specificity rate was 72.3%, and the sensitivity rate was 84.1%.

### 3.7.8 Monitoring in the ICU

The research employed logistic regression and LSTM neural network models to predict the likelihood of short-term mortality in cirrhosis patients who were admitted to the hospital. The investigation involved the examination and analysis of medical record data from a sample of 500 individuals who were residing in the ICU. Researchers subsequently conducted a comparison of the efficacy of these two machine learning algorithms \[[126](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_126)\]. During training, the algorithm was fed a set of 20 features, including heart rate, systolic blood pressure, diastolic blood pressure, rate of respiration, and pulse. As a tool for clinical decision-making, the mathematical Chronic Liver Failure (CLIF) score was outperformed by the machine learning models. According to the investigation, the logistic regression model has an area under the curve (AUC) of 0.80. In contrast, the RNN-LSTM model achieved an AUC of 0.77 \[[127](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_127)\]. Also, the CLIF model showed an AUC of 0.72 for the receiver-operating characteristic curve. A customized model possesses the capacity to examine essential physiological indicators by leveraging historical data. The utilization of Gaussian process regression (GPR) was suggested by researchers as a technique for developing flexible and personalized models for patient vital sign time series \[[128](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_128)\]. The study employed a method to develop patient-specific GPR models for predicting robust vital signs. The models are constructed with varying complexity and regularization, utilizing different hyperparameters. The researchers employed a random search algorithm for the purpose of identifying patient-specific parameters. Bayesian optimization techniques were employed to accommodate a wide range of parameterizations within the patient population. Machine learning techniques are utilized at the most advanced level of RPM for optimizing patient-specific parameters. This approach facilitates the creation of individualized models for patients and enables a comprehensive analysis of their health status at the most basic level.

### 3.7.9 Facial and emotional recognition

AI has the capability to classify a patient’s emotions by utilizing facial recognition technology. The proposed study by the researchers introduces a smart, integrated patient monitoring system that includes temperature sensors, face recognition algorithms, and pulse sensors. This system is designed to precisely determine the emotional states of patients and quantify their heartbeat rates \[[129](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_129)\]. In order to offer patients’ affective data, the RPM system implemented facial recognition techniques, feature extraction, image preprocessing, and classification algorithms. The face emotion detection model is capable of detecting seven distinct emotions: rage, sorrow, independence, astonishment, dissatisfaction, and fright. The patients’ vital signs were monitored at 5-s intervals using a temperature sensor and heart rate sensor. Researchers worked through an RPM framework to construct a deep learning-based system for facial emotion recognition. This was done in order to tackle the issues associated with simultaneously optimizing feature extraction and classification \[[130](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_130)\].

This study aims to experimentally identify user emotions among individuals using commercial RFID devices. Researchers developed a respiratory and heartbeat-based feature extraction framework for an RPM system using RFID signals. Using the features that were obtained, a classifier was trained to identify different user emotions. The study used filters to isolate the breathing rate and improved the signal-to-noise ratio to recover the data from the heartbeat by reducing the signal from the respiration rate. The study employs a 2D framework that classifies emotions into four states: joy, pleasure, anger, and sorrow. The SVM model achieved classification accuracies of 80.65%, 61.29%, 83.87%, and 74.19% for the four emotion states \[[131](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_131)\].

### 3.7.10 Role in early detection of patient deterioration

Patients in critical care settings are particularly vulnerable to clinical deterioration, making the rapid identification of changes in vital signs an absolute priority. The conventional approach to patient monitoring is the collection and analysis of specific vital signs in order to assess the actual clinical condition of individuals. In patients entering the emergency room, vital signs including temperature, pulse, respiration rate, and mean arterial pressure are generally recognized as continuous predictors \[[132](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_132)\]. Emerging patient monitoring algorithms possess the capability to analyze diverse physiological signal characteristics. A predictive or prognostic score was created by researchers in order to evaluate a certain crucial health event or physiological instability \[[133](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_133)\]. The capacity of surgical wards to quickly detect patients exhibiting deterioration was examined in a case series study that examined the impact of wireless remote monitoring systems for vital signs \[[134](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_134)\]. According to the findings of the study, the nursing staff indicated that the systems provided a modest level of assistance; however they still required the exercise of clinical judgement for patient assessment. The absence of established standards or guidelines for implementing these systems has been observed, leaving clinicians with the responsibility of determining the most suitable system for their requirements.

Scholars have underscored the importance of healthcare professionals prioritizing the systematic and accurate recording of vital signs \[[135](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_135)\]. A lack of comprehension among specialists regarding the frequency at which vital signs should be recorded was observed by the researchers. They also emphasized the common practice of periodically manually observing vital signs in hospital wards. The significance of ongoing patient monitoring in forecasting the onset of unexpected occurrences was underscored by researchers.
