Currently, clinicians utilize early warning scores (EWSs), which are manually computed screening metrics, to anticipate patient deterioration at an early stage \[[136](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_136)\]. The study conducted by researchers revealed that although EWS exhibit robust predictive capabilities, their intermittent nature presents certain limits \[[137](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_137)\]. In the past, the practice of constantly recording vital signs was primarily restricted to intensive care units, which required a substantial workforce to accommodate patient requirements. Researchers introduced a new framework for predicting patient deterioration in ICUs using LSTM-RNNs \[[138](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_138)\]. The model outperformed the conventional method in terms of classification capability and was capable of accurately predicting the onset of deterioration one hour in advance.

Using historical data retrieved from EMR, the study applied RNNs and LSTM models to predict the development of crucial physiological indicators. The research makes a valuable contribution to the existing body of scholarly literature regarding the forecasting of health outcomes \[[139](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_139)\]. The anticipated physiological indicators were then employed in a clinical prognostic instrument that integrated both laboratory results and vital signs to assist timely identification of declining health conditions. The tool had a precision level of 80%.

### 3.7.11 Personalized monitoring for patient

Traditional medical treatments and diagnoses rely on population averages and fail to account for the unique variations among individual patients \[[140](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_140)\]. The previous sections have discussed the incorporation of IoT-enabled RPM architecture with cloud computing. This integration allows for the aggregation of patients’ data, which can then be utilized for AI modeling purposes. Patient-centric or personalized monitoring is crucial in modern settings, especially for chronic conditions such as mental health disorders and diabetes. Personalized monitoring can be achieved through the utilization of distributed networks such as fog and edge computing. Within this particular context, an edge network fulfils the role of catering to a distinct collection of IoT devices that have been strategically positioned on a patient. The authors of the study proposed a novel edge-fog-cloud framework for the purpose of personalized health monitoring \[[141](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_141)\]. The proposed methodology utilizes predictive models to anticipate patient mobility patterns and then offers recommendations for accessing neighboring healthcare centers during emergency scenarios. Information obtained from an IoT platform must be transmitted from the devices to a centralized cloud server in order to facilitate data analytics. The current situation raises concerns regarding the confidentiality and preservation of patients’ medical information. In addition, it necessitates substantial technological resources and significant power consumption.

Google has created a Federated Learning framework that is specifically designed for AI in order to meet the challenges of training an AI model on decentralized peripheral devices. This allows the model to utilize individual patient data without necessitating data sharing or integration. The weights of the local model were collected and transferred to a cloud server in the subsequent phase. The model weights are combined to train a robust global AI model. In this decentralized system, patients’ data is securely stored on their own devices, thereby guaranteeing its privacy. The accuracy of predictions or classifications on their own local datasets can be enhanced by local models that capitalize on the global model. The scientific community has broadly embraced the concept of IoT applications \[[142](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_142)\].

A notion for a federated transfer learning mechanism in healthcare was given in the context of the Internet of medical things. Researchers looked at different federated learning systems that are used in smart healthcare \[[143](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_143)\]. The contributors went over the benefits, requirements, and uses of federated learning in healthcare environments. They also looked at new trends and figured out what problems might come up when shared learning is used in this area. Researchers came up with the FedHome framework, which is a cloud-based federated learning system, made for health tracking in the home and localized training for models \[[144](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_144)\]. The project’s main goal was to create a generative convolutional autoencoder that could handle uneven and nonidentical distribution data for accurate, personalized health tracking. With a 95.41% success rate for uneven data and 95.87% success rate for balanced data, the proposed method performs better than the baseline models. Using FedHealth for personalized monitoring, researchers did studies to classify physical activity \[[145](#9783111547589_005273.xhtml#b_9783111549729-003_ref_145)\]. The study presents a federated learning framework that uses transfer learning and data collection to create personalized models. Two classification problems were used to test the FedHealth system. They put the physical tasks into groups and got a 99.4% improvement in accuracy compared to the baseline models. The second methodology entails the classification of the droop exhibited by individuals with Parkinson’s disease in their upper limbs, as well as their performance on the postural tremor assessment. The method used in the study was accurate 84.3% of the time when measuring arm droop and 74.9% of the time when classifying postural tremor.

## 3.8 Conclusion

An escalating number of individuals are experiencing chronic ailments necessitating the management of their lifestyle and medication. Consequently, there is a growing inclination towards the adoption of remote monitoring technologies, which can effectively aid in illness management for both patients and healthcare professionals. These monitoring tools enable individuals to gather health data in the comfort of their own homes or while travelling, and afterwards retain or transfer this data to healthcare specialists for clinical assistance. In recent years, data science has experienced substantial advancements, leading to the extensive application of ML methodologies for RPM in several fields. The outcomes of these endeavors have shown promising outcomes. AI-based tools have the capability to process the extensive datasets that are frequently obtained through remote monitoring systems, such as medical devices, wearables, and applications. These tools can offer improved prediction, classification, and decision support functionalities. Numerous instances of enhanced health outcomes have been documented in several domains, such as heart failure, migraine, and diabetes care, through the utilization of AI-driven remote monitoring systems. Therefore, it can be asserted that AI is a highly effective method for accurately monitoring patients.

# Chapter 4 Engaging AI in emergency medicine for better patient care

#### Abstract

As artificial intelligence (AI) has enhanced and grown, individuals are interested in discovering how it can be utilized in healthcare. Researchers are currently looking into how machine learning (ML) technologies could be used to make emergency medicine better. People are becoming more and more interested in emerging medicine because it uses AI and ML in a lot of different ways. A lot of progress has been made in the study of AI in the last few years. This is mostly because of how well modern ML methods like deep learning work, how easy it is to get to big datasets, and how fast computers are getting faster. Medical professionals can do more and more tasks that are as good as or better than what doctors can do. In other words, AI is getting better at helping people get better health. AI could be useful in many areas of the emergency department (ED) including the operating room, running the ED, images, keeping track of patients, and making clinical predictions. These different ways show that AI can be used to make EDs better in many ways. Many scientists believe that over the next few years, AI will be used in medicine more and more.

Keywords:  Emergency department,  artificial intelligence,  deep learning,  patient care,  emergency operation,  

## 4.1 Introduction

Healthcare has used artificial intelligence (AI) for a very long time \[[1](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_001)\]. Also known as AI, this subject has been an important part of computer science for over 60 years. A subject called medical history, on the other hand, has roots that go back thousands of years. Medicine researchers and AI experts have been working together to find new ways to improve care for patients and make healthcare systems run more smoothly \[[2](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_002), [3](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_003)\]. There are two main types of partnerships between the two fields: those that are driven by technology and those that are driven by problems. When it comes to technology-based study, the main focus is on making and testing new AI algorithms that are meant to solve specific therapeutic problems. The main goal of these algorithms is to be flexible, not to prioritize optimization for handling real-world problems. AI algorithms are tailored to successfully address specific clinical issues within the framework of problem-driven research, resulting in the most effective solutions. Nevertheless, it is crucial to acknowledge that these customized algorithms may not exhibit a high degree of generalizability to other problem domains. The gap between technology-driven and problem-driven study has been narrowing over the past few years.

In the field of medicine, the development of AI has been greatly helped by the creation of an effective information technology system. More and more computers and storage space have made it easier to use advanced AI algorithms to handle huge amounts of healthcare data. Full picture analysis and fast processing of real-time patient monitoring data have been put into place and are now working well. These days, using powerful graphics processing units has made many types of computing much better including deep learning (DL) \[[4](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_004)\]. These methods are now widely known and used to effectively look at complicated clinical data like medical images \[[5](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_005), [6](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_006)\] and electronic health records (EHRs) \[[7](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_007)\]. Throughout history, AI technologies have been widely known and used in the fields of intensive care \[[8](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_008)\] and heart \[[9](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_009)\]. Researchers have written an in-depth analysis of the latest progress in AI in the medical field as well as predictions about where it might go in the future \[[10](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_010)\]. Still, because both medicine and AI are very broad fields, it is not possible to give a complete list of all the progress being made at the intersection of AI and medicine.

## 4.2 What is AI?

During the 1950s, the phrase “artificial intelligence” was introduced by Professor John McCarthy of Stanford University, who defined it as the field encompassing the scientific and engineering principles behind the creation of intelligent computers. AI makes it easier for machines to copy human cognitive skills like learning and solving problems. Machine learning (ML) is a branch of AI that focuses on using data to create computer systems that can learn new things and get better at what they do without being explicitly programmed \[[11](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_011)\]. Statistical techniques and algorithms are utilized to detect patterns and establish correlations within datasets, hence enabling the development of predictive or decision-making models. The three primary categories into which ML algorithms can be classified are supervised learning, unsupervised learning, and reinforcement learning. According to SML, supervised ML uses labeled data to learn about the connection between input factors and a result variable like a positive or negative diagnostic. Development of an SML entails utilization of three distinct datasets. The method initially undergoes a learning process on the training dataset, wherein it iteratively adjusts the weights to minimize a loss function. The loss function is responsible for quantifying the discrepancy between the predicted and observed results for a specific data point. Utilizing a test set, small changes are made to the model parameters to get the best outcomes after the model has been created. A validation set is needed to find overfitting, which is when there is a big difference in how well the model does in learning from the validation set compared to the training set. In essence, the purpose of utilizing a test set is to evaluate the model’s capacity to apply its obtained knowledge to new data.

Unsupervised learning, on the other hand, includes methods that use unlabeled data to find natural trends or groups. The purpose of looking at these groups is to find data points that are alike and those that are different as well as to learn how the data is separated in the feature space. Reinforcement learning lets computers learn how to reach their goals in a social setting by making mistakes and fixing them over and over again. Reinforcement learning works by using rewards or penalties as feedback based on what the agent does, while supervised learning focuses on having labeled data to give feedback to the model. Over a temporal duration, the agent assimilates information on sequences of actions that maximize the cumulative result.

In recent years, the healthcare industry has recognized and expanded its use of DL, a subfield of ML. This can be attributed to its remarkable achievements in effectively addressing a wide range of intricate categorization issues \[[12](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_012)\]. The aforementioned phenomenon can be partially ascribed to advancements in computer capacity and the availability of increasingly vast quantities of data. Neural network architecture and function are based on the cerebral cortex anatomy and function; this is the foundation of DL algorithms. The neural network architecture consists of interconnected nodes organized in layers, resembling neurons (refer to [Figure 4.1](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_fig_001)). As part of forward propagation, the input data is sent to an input layer and then through a network of hidden nodes that are all linked to each other and have different weights assigned to them. The computation of the input to each node involves taking the weighted total of the outputs from the previous layer. This computed input then undergoes a nonlinear modification to generate the output of the node. The difference between the expected value from the output layer and the ground truth value is measured by the loss function. Backpropagation is an optimization technique that uses the prediction error to change the weights over and over again in order to learn more about the structure of the training data. This makes the model more accurate over time.

![Figure 4.1: The artificial neural network serves as the fundamental framework for deep learning algorithms.](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-004_fig_001.png)

Figure 4.1: The artificial neural network serves as the fundamental framework for deep learning algorithms.

## 4.3 Difference between ML and DL

A basic differentiation between ML and DL techniques may be observed in their distinct learning processes. DL algorithms could independently acquire feature hierarchies, which set them apart from traditional ML approaches that need manual extraction and programming of features. Furthermore, DL frequently necessitates a substantial amount of data in order to make correct predictions, whereas conventional ML techniques may reach a point when the performance of the model no longer improves in proportion to the quantity of data. Another significant distinction between the two methodologies lies in their respective execution times. The training process of DL methods sometimes necessitates a significant duration of time due to the vast number of parameters that must be acquired. DL methods typically need a substantial amount of matrix multiplication operations, leading to a significant reliance on high-performance computing systems.

## 4.4 Machine learning algorithms in clinical research

The decision tree is a well-known ML model that is used in clinical study and is easy to understand. A guided learning method called the decision tree is organized in a way that looks like a flowchart. The system can do both jobs related to classification and tasks related to regression. The study’s goal was to create a prediction model that can correctly guess the target variable for future events. This was done with the help of a set of rules for making decisions. Utilizing a recursive approach, the method divided the data into subsets, or decision nodes by checking the feature value that leads to the greatest reduction in disorder within the created subsets \[[13](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_013)\]. There is only one “pure” node if all of its data points belong to the same class in the graph. When a node exhibits an identical distribution of percentages for each class, it results in the highest level of impurity. [Figure 4.2](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_fig_002) depicts an exemplar decision tree employed in the context of predicting medication dosage. The initial division is predicated upon the body mass index (BMI), suggesting that BMI serves as the most reliable indicator for determining the appropriate dosage amount. Class membership probabilities are assigned to each data point in the final subsets, also known as leaf nodes.

![Figure 4.2: The utilization of a classification and regression tree algorithm for the purpose of predicting medicine dose.](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-004_fig_002.png)

Figure 4.2: The utilization of a classification and regression tree algorithm for the purpose of predicting medicine dose.

Ensemble learning is a ML method that combines many techniques to make predictions more accurate and less variable. A common ensemble model is the random forest, which takes the predictions of many decision trees and puts them together to make a single forecast. Generally, it is often observed that ensembles possess more predictive capability compared to their individual parts. Linear regression, k-nearest neighbors, support vector machines, naive Bayes, and a number of ensemble methods, such as gradient and adaptive boosting, are also commonly used in the field.

Researchers are very interested in the convolutional neural network (CNN) because it works so well in computer vision applications. This makes it a major step forward in the area of DL. Additionally, a CNN is a specialized type of a DNN that can obtain features in a flexible way by using backpropagation, which eliminates the need for human feature extraction. Over the past few years, several studies have shown that DL can be useful for medical imaging tasks \[[14](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_014)\]. Researchers have used a CNN to categorize brain tumors into different types, figure out what causes COVID from chest X-rays, and spot diabetic retinopathy \[[15](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_015)\]. Doctors support neural networks a lot because they can learn feature sets on their own, which is easier now that more advanced DL models have been made. CNN have demonstrated their ability to surpass the constraints of traditional ML models in specific categorization tasks.

The subject of natural language processing (NLP), which focuses on developing machines that can comprehend and analyze human language, has been greatly affected by the advancements in DL \[[16](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_016)\]. EHRs hold a lot of written information. One way to make good use of all that information is to use NLP in emergency care. A lot of different neural networks have been carefully designed to get useful data from unstructured text data like medical reports and doctor’s notes that are kept in electronic medical records \[[17](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_017), [18](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_018)\]. Using NLP on medical data has shown promise for many medical uses like finding cases of sepsis, appendicitis, and flu \[[19](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_019), [20](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_020), [21](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_021), [22](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_022)\]. A lot of focus has been paid to recurrent neural networks, CNN, and deep generative models in NLP when it comes to DL. Additionally, it has been utilized in NLP to employ both independent learning and reinforcement learning.

In the area of predictive research, supervised ML algorithms have quickly taken the place of older methods. These algorithms are used to predict the probability of consequences by looking at patient factors that are set up before these events happen. The adaptability of these models makes it possible to find links between attributes (predictors) and results. This should lead to better prediction performance than with basic scoring systems. In conjunction with the conventional application of predictive analyses, causal inference studies have implemented supervised ML algorithms to look into the influence of interventions on desired outcomes. Models are commonly used in observational study for estimating treatment effects, which is necessary for inferring causal links. With the implementation of sophisticated ML models, it is plausible to anticipate a decrease in bias.

## 4.5 Role of AI in emergency medicine

### 4.5.1 Role in triage and disposition

Triage is a structured procedure that is implemented in the ED to evaluate patients upon their arrival and assign them a priority based on the severity and urgency of their medical conditions. In normal practice, a triage nurse evaluates patients using vital signs, demographic information, and prescribed diagnostic methods. For someone to become a master in triage, they need to put in a lot of time and practice. Emergency severity index level 3 is frequently selected as the default option when situations occur where immediate, life-threatening problems cannot be identified. On the other hand, this choice could mean that a lot of patients have to wait a long time before they can get medical help and an assessment \[[23](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_023)\]. When used in the ED, ML models could make the triage process better by making it more accurate and faster. Computer programs that learn from data that the triage staff collects every day can be used to make this change. Aside from being able to predict how bad medical problems will be, ML techniques can also be used to make screening tools that can predict disease-specific weaknesses.

### 4.5.2 Role in ED operations

A hospital ED needs to plan its resources and keep an eye on its patients all the time. Sun et al. \[[24](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_024)\] used the autoregressive integrated moving average method to look at time series data and make models that could predict how busy the ED would be. The study shows that predicting models can help with planning and making the best use of staffing resources in the real world. The researchers \[[25](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_025)\] looked into the problem of overcrowding in EDs by looking at what happens when different plans for staffing doctors are made. To do this, they made a simulation tool built on agents. This tool’s usefulness has been tested in a single hospital ED.

Diagnose-choice tools are very important in ED because they make screening and categorizing patients easier. In their research, the researchers \[[26](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_026)\] introduced a diagnostic modeling system that has the capacity to automate the development of diagnostic decision support systems. The system was tested in the ED, and it appeared to be scalable enough to be used in other areas. In the same way, researchers \[[27](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_027)\] used data mining methods to help doctors make diagnoses in a pediatric ED.

AI and ML techniques have been utilized for several operational functions inside the ED. Researchers \[[28](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_028)\] did a study where they used different data mining methods, like the naive Bayes and C4.5 algorithms, to determine the seriousness of ED patients so that they could be sent to the right room. In a similar manner, researchers \[[29](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_029)\] used a lot of ML techniques to guess how people with asthma and chronic obstructive lung disease (COPD) would feel so that the triage process could work better. Researchers \[[30](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_030), [31](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_031)\] came up with a way to automatically sort computed tomography (CT) reports for both adult and pediatric patients in the ED. The researchers used a method that involved making mixed systems that combined ML algorithms with NLP methods. It was found that NLP had a big effect in a different study \[[32](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_032)\]. The researchers used an automated system for ED reports called the subjective, objective, assessment, and plan (SOAP).

### 4.5.3 Role in emergency department operations and management

It is hard to accurately predict future service needs in ED operations because many of the factors involved are random. Some of these factors are the schedules of patients’ arrivals, the different types of medical care and diagnostic tests they need, and the lengths of time they need these services and tests. The use of AI could greatly improve the way EDs work and how hospitals are run at all stages of patient care, from admission to exit. The use of ML could make EDs run more smoothly by figuring out the best way to use resources based on what each patient needs. This, in turn, can lead to a reduction in expenditures and an improvement in patient outcomes. The problem of overcrowding in EDs is a growing concern within the healthcare sector, which can potentially lead to adverse effects on the provision of high-quality patient care \[[33](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_033)\]. The application of predictive models in the prediction of ED flow has the capacity to support the creation of staffing models and improve readiness for surge and emergencies. Additionally, other confounding factors contribute to the challenge of estimating ED duration for waiting. ML algorithms, possessing capability to discern patterns within intricate collections of features, hold the potential to generate ED wait times of higher accuracy. On a broader scope, in the event that several hospitals in close proximity provide precise wait time information, patients with low acuity conditions would be able to make a selection for ED services based on both distance to be traveled and duration for waiting.

Not all uses of AI will endure and gain the confidence of medical professionals and patients. According to existing literature, there is a higher likelihood of triage and radiology models being implemented at a faster rate compared to other applications in the context of ED  operations \[[34](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_034)\].

### 4.5.4 Role in medical imaging

The timely access to radiology interpretation is not always available to the ED practitioner. The prompt and efficient availability of medical imaging results is of utmost importance in the proper detection of fracture in X-rays or a stroke in magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scans, as any delays in diagnosis and treatment could potentially lead to misdiagnosis and hindered medical interventions. This aspect becomes particularly crucial while operating within a smaller ED that has restricted availability of specialized medical professional. DL models specifically developed for medical imaging exhibit an exceptional capacity to identify critical medical disorders, hence facilitating expedited diagnosis by healthcare professionals. Recent studies have indicated that the quality of AI interpretation is on a level with that of a skilled radiologist \[[35](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_035)\].

### 4.5.5 Role in clinical image analysis

The progress made in the field of picture recognition holds significant relevance for several domains within the realm of clinical image analysis. Presence of extensive labeled image databases has made image analysis a highly productive area of research. Applying DL techniques has shown a high level of accuracy in finding pneumonia on chest X-rays and 3D segmentation of subdural hematomas on brain CT, figuring out the probability that it is cerebral aneurysm ruptures and scoring CT scans of people who may have had an acute ischemic stroke. These achievements have been found to be comparable in accuracy to that of stroke specialists \[[36](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_036), [37](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_037)\]. DL has been utilized in the area of brain MRI to differentiate individuals with a first episode psychosis from a control group as well as to forecast their lifetime consumption of alcohol \[[38](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_038), [39](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_039)\]. DL has been employed in the domain of ultrasonography (USG) as well. It has shown a high level of accuracy in finding free fluid in the abdomen on focused assessment with sonography for trauma (FAST) scans, categorizing abdominal USG pictures and automatically analyzing ejection fraction on echocardiography \[[40](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_040), [41](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_041)\]. DL has also helped new technologies get better. For example, a microwave-based scanning model can correctly tell the difference between an ischemic and a hemorrhagic stroke before the patient gets to the hospital \[[42](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_042)\].

### 4.5.6 Role in predictive modeling

Predictive modeling stands as a prominent and inherent use of AI within the domain of medicine. A multitude of AI systems has been created with the purpose of forecasting the likelihood of sickness and its consequences. Significantly, within the field of emergency medicine, there has been extensive exploration of AI in development of prediction models. In their study, researchers \[[43](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_043)\] employed AI techniques and data mining tools to forecast the admission to the hospital. The researchers \[[44](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_044)\] employed patient data collected in the ED and employed decision trees, logistic regression, and gradient-boosted machines as components of their methodology. In their study, researchers made a web-based tool that uses a number of data mining and ML methods to instantly figure out the likelihood of someone needing to go to the ED again. The study showed that it is possible to do population-based risk assessment better by combining data from outpatient centers with data from hospitals or EDs. Smart computers, or AI, play a big role in this process. The researchers also made a clinical decision tool that tells how likely it is that a patient will need to go back to the ED in the next 72 h \[[45](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_045)\]. The tool lets the ED staff and managers use patient-specific data to figure out how likely it is that a patient will return to the ED within 72 h. This could give them more power. This is a chance to give more information and improve patient care so that fewer people have to go back to the ED.

The artificial neural network is one of the most useful and well-known of the many AI tools. One study \[[46](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_046)\] showed that groups of artificial neural networks can be used to guess how babies and kids with bronchiolitis will be treated in the ED. Nevertheless, it was determined that the accuracy of the stay prediction was low. The researchers \[[47](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_047)\] used methods from artificial neural networks to make a model that can tell when the craniocervical joint will become damaged in a trauma patient. A study \[[48](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_048)\] was done that used artificial neural network technology and clinical data gathered during the initial evaluation of patients at the ED to create a predictive model for acute coronary syndromes. In their study, researchers \[[49](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_049)\] conducted an assessment of the prediction of mortality and acute morbidity using routinely collected variables. This assessment involved the construction of models utilizing a total of 17 statistical and AI methodologies including the implementation of neural networks. A study \[[50](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_050)\] used artificial neural networks and genetic algorithms, a metaheuristic approach inspired by natural selection, to predict renal colic in emergency medical settings.

Utilization of extensive and intricate datasets for predictive modeling is made possible by the vast quantity of data accessible through EHRs. It is not possible to use classical logistic regression when there are too many independent factors compared to observations. In order to tackle this matter, the utilization of variable selection proves to be both successful and efficient. The researchers \[[51](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_051)\] did a study in which they came up with a ranking method to find a subset of variables that might be able to predict as well as the whole series of variables. The objective was to evaluate the probability of serious cardiovascular events in ED patients who presented with chest pain. In a study, researchers employed a well-established random forest algorithm to assess the relative relevance of factors and showcased the effectiveness of AI and ML techniques in forcasting in-patient mortality for ED sepsis patients, surpassing the predictive capabilities of clinical scores \[[52](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_052)\].

### 4.5.7 Role in clinical monitoring

The implementation of AI-based clinical monitoring systems has the potential to facilitate the timely detection of patients experiencing a decline in their health status. The compensatory reserve index (CRI) uses a number of ML methods to find small changes in the arterial blood pressure (BP) pattern of a finger in order to predict the development of cardiovascular disease in the future \[[53](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_053)\]. The CRI exhibits a linear reduction after voluntary bleeding. Furthermore, the inclusion of CRI on the monitor screen has been linked to the early detection of imminent instability \[[54](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_054), [55](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_055)\]. A ML model has been developed and trained to evaluate vital signs and detect instances of cardiovascular instability in patients transitioning from the intensive care unit to step down units. On average, this model provides alerts approximately 17 min and 51 s prior to the occurrence of a cardiovascular instability event \[[56](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_056)\].

The timely identification and intervention of sepsis lead to substantial enhancements in patient outcomes. When applied to the study of changing heart rate and BP, ML algorithms have shown that they can independently identify the onset of sepsis up to 4 h before the first clinical symptoms show up \[[57](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_057)\]. In a similar vein, InSight is a precise ML-based system for predicting sepsis. It demonstrates resilience in the face of missing data and leverages the temporal evolution of vital signs to effectively forecast the occurrence of sepsis and severe sepsis many hours in advance \[[58](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_058)\]. The utilization of a DL system to the analysis of electrocardiogram (ECG) data has demonstrated superior accuracy in detecting 15 distinct arrhythmias, sinus rhythm, and noise, surpassing the performance of individual cardiologists \[[59](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_059)\].

The problem of false alarms, which is now prevalent in “intelligent” monitors, poses a challenge for any system aiming to provide alerts. Back in 2015, there was a worldwide study competition whose main goal was to find ways to use ML to reduce the number of false alarms in critical care units. Plesinger et al. \[[60](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_060)\] found that the successful model had an 80% success rate in removing fake alarms while only 1% success rate in removing real alarms. It is anticipated that forthcoming monitoring devices would exhibit reduced bulkiness in comparison to existing systems. New methods for measuring heart rate and breathing rate have been created using ML techniques made possible by recent advances in technology. These methods are the only ones that can correctly measure these physiological parameters through video analysis alone, without having to touch the patient directly \[[61](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_061)\]. ML has been used to learn how to use a single arm band to measure BP without a cuff. Additionally, ML has been employed to effectively forecast BP by utilizing ECG recordings \[[62](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_062), [63](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_063)\].

### 4.5.8 Role in patient monitoring

With the advent of modern sensor technologies and the increasing computational capabilities, the continuous collection and analysis of extensive physiological data from patients is no longer a mere concept. The researchers \[[64](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_064)\] presented a comprehensive wireless network that was specifically developed to monitor unchecked individuals in the ED. The study included trying a prototype for a scalable medical alert response technology (SMART) system for the first time. The system was then put into use on a small scale. The authors of a recent study conducted by researchers \[[65](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_065)\] created and validated an integrated patient monitoring system inside a large-scale ED setting. The system was connected to a peer-to-peer network that had monitors at the bedsides and handheld PDAs. The researchers performed a comparative analysis on two early warning systems, one utilizing an integrated system and the other employing a traditional manual technique. The aforementioned extensive clinical research also examined automated approaches for evaluating patients using EHRs enhanced with AI methodologies. Typically, the integration of patient monitoring systems with AI components is a prevalent practice aimed at enhancing assistive decision-making processes. Earlier research has looked at how AI can be used to manage physiological data, especially electrocardiography, in emergency medical situations \[[66](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_066), [67](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_067)\].

### 4.5.9 Role in home monitoring of patient

In the future, it is plausible that patients will arrive to the ED at an earlier stage due to the influence of algorithms, and they will possess a greater amount of information. ML has been used to look at telemonitored breathing sounds in order to predict sudden worsenings of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease. This approach successfully identified 75.8% of exacerbations in advance, on average 5 days prior to the patient seeking medical assistance \[[68](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_068)\]. In the same way, earlier research has shown that a ML model might be able to predict when asthma control will get worse in children, up to 1 week before the first noticeable symptoms show up \[[69](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_069)\]. Wrist-worn accelerometers possess the capacity to identify seizures, whereas “smart carpets” employ floor-based detection systems and utilize ML or smartphone audio systems to determine instances of falls among the elderly \[[70](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_070), [71](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_071)\].

### 4.5.10 Role in causal inference, risk, prediction, and metrics

According to researchers, there are three main types of data science methods used in medical and epidemiological study. These are description, prediction, and causal inference (also called counterfactual prediction) \[[72](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_072)\]. Despite the significance of descriptive and predictive studies in comprehending the occurrence, causes, and prognosis of diseases or maladies, physicians generally find it insufficient to rely solely on these research methodologies when attempting to enhance patient outcomes for interventions. Causal inference is a research methodology that attracts considerable attention in the world of medical research. It includes comparing hypothetical prospective outcomes by applying two or more distinct therapies within a certain population.

ML models have the potential to enhance causal inference through many means. Initially, it should be noted that unsupervised learning models possess the capability to discern clusters of patients who exhibit distinct characteristics. According to a recent scholarly work, a comprehensive analysis of data from many observational studies and randomized control trials has revealed the existence of four distinct phenotypes of sepsis that hold clinical significance \[[73](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_073)\]. The research employed a consensus clustering approach that was based on the k-means algorithm to simplify the classification process. Despite the lack of direct causal inference in this study, the researchers postulated that the phenotypes studied might respond differently to early goal-directed therapy.

Furthermore, it has been observed that supervised learning models have the ability to provide predictions that can identify high-risk patients with greater accuracy compared to previous methodologies. An example of a previously based study used baseline demographic information, chief complaints, vital signs, and patient complications to predict the likelihood of needing critical care, such as being admitted to the intensive care unit or dying in the hospital, as well as the likelihood of being hospitalized for people who come to the ED \[[74](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_074)\]. The researchers saw that the predictor model using logistic regression did not do as well as the other four ML models (lasso regression, gradient boosting, random forest, and DNN). These models had different levels of accuracy. While the study did not include demonstrating causal linkages, it is worth noting that the early detection of persons at high risk could potentially aid in identifying a particular subgroup that could greatly benefit from timely and intensive interventions.

Finally, ML models could be directly used in causal inference to make a treatment model work better so that a propensity score, an outcome model, or both can be made. It is possible to use more complex methods for this purpose, even though logistic regression is often used to estimate propensity scores. An example of this was a research study that evaluated the effect of transthoracic echocardiography on the mortality rate within 28 days in patients with sepsis in the intensive care unit \[[75](#9783111547589_007615.xhtml#b_9783111549729-004_ref_075)\]. The researchers choose to employ gradient boosting, as opposed to logistic regression, as the statistical technique for constructing the treatment model in order to predict propensity scores for the receipt of transthoracic echocardiography. In the same vein, ML models can be implemented to estimate treatment effects in outcome models, thereby simplifying the process of drawing causal inferences.

## 4.6 Conclusion

Despite certain limits, contemporary AI systems have significant proficiency in effectively addressing clearly defined challenges within a diverse array of therapeutic areas. These devices possess the capability to enhance various facets of emergency patient care. It is quite probable that the capabilities of AI technology will enhance progressively, and the incorporation of these solutions into practical settings holds the potential to yield advantages for patients, physicians, and the general population by facilitating the more effective and precise provision of healthcare services of superior quality.
