# Chapter 5 Application of AI in ENT (otorhinolaryngology) care

#### Abstract

The concept of artificial intelligence (AI) has become widely discussed in both popular culture and academic research. In recent years, there have been substantial discussions over its potential to profoundly alter health care delivery. Nevertheless, to now, it has rendered minimal contributions to the domain of medical practice or the enhancement of patient care. The potential integration of AI technologies into the field of otolaryngology may encounter obstacles due to prevailing misconceptions surrounding the nature of AI and concerns over potential machine faults that could impact patient treatment. It is essential for otolaryngologists to have a thorough understanding of AI ideas and implications because of its potential therapeutic and economic benefits. AI is likely to significantly impact biomedical research and the application of medical practices in the near future. Otolaryngologists are crucial contributors to the development and effective application of important AI technologies designed to improve patient care. The acquisition of high-caliber data holds an utmost importance for the advancement of AI technologies. Consequently, otolaryngologists have to actively pursue collaborations with data scientists, with the aim of providing guidance in identifying clinically significant inquiries that can yield substantial impact. AI has significant implications in various domains of otorhinolaryngology. These include the automated diagnosis of hearing loss, the enhancement of hearing aid performance, the restoration of speech in individuals with paralysis, the prediction of speech and language outcomes in candidates for cochlear implants, the diagnosis of various otology conditions through the analysis of otoscopic images, the application of virtual reality simulators for education in otological surgeries, the categorization and measurement of opacification in computed tomography scans of paranasal sinuses, the differentiation of different laryngeal pathologies depending on laryngoscopic images, the automatic segmentation of anatomical structures to expedite radiotherapy planning, and the assistance of pathologists in reporting thyroid cytopathology.

Keywords:  Otolaryngology practice,  laryngeal pathology,  machine learning,  artificial intelligence,  

## 5.1 Introduction

The term “computational intelligence,” also referred to as artificial intelligence (AI), encompasses the use of technology to enable computer systems to perform tasks that generally necessitate human intelligence. These activities involve word recognition and identifying objects, visual perception, and intricate decision-making. In the domain of computational learning, algorithms are specifically crafted to collect data from input sources and employ this learned information to generate predictions for unanalyzed data. In medicine, there has been a significant technological shift and a remarkable enhancement in computing capabilities in recent decades \[[1](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_001)\].

Machine learning (ML) is a domain of AI encompassing a collection of algorithms and methodologies designed to enhance efficiency and precision through the iterative identification of patterns. In ML, computers are trained to recognize patterns in historical data and then apply this learning to forecast the behavior of newly supplied data. There are various classifications of ML. A particular category of ML algorithms is known as artificial neural networks (ANNs). ANNs are engineered to emulate the functional attributes of synapses found in the human brain. ANNs consist of multiple interconnected layers of nodes. Each node performs a sequence of computations based on the inputs and outputs it receives. Furthermore, each node in the network establishes connections with other nodes. Information flows through numerous hidden levels of an ANN, beginning at the input layer and finishing at the output layer. ANNs with multiple hidden layers are commonly termed “deep neural networks.” These networks have been designed to address complex problems, such as voice and picture recognition acquisition \[[2](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_002)\]. In the domain of ML, commonly referred to as “deep learning (DL),” the algorithm is instructed to identify patterns within extensive datasets \[[3](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_003)\].

The use of computational intelligence in otorhinolaryngology is still in its early stage, but it offers a lot of promise. The creation of datasets encompassing diverse patient features, disorders, and associated outcomes is a crucial requirement in the domain of AI applications. Considering the patient’s present symptoms, the subsequent step is to recommend particular diagnostic examinations and potential therapeutic interventions. The ML system assesses the precision of its predictions by leveraging its database, therefore guaranteeing that the patient receives the most appropriate treatment.

## 5.2 Machine learning and deep learning

The area of AI, especially ML and DL, has received a lot of attention from medical professionals and academics since it started in 1990 and 2010. AI, ML, and DL are all strongly linked to each other. It’s not clear what the exact meaning of AI is when it comes to automating jobs that normally need human intelligence \[[4](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_004)\]. Visual comprehension, object identification and recognition, and making decisions are all part of these tasks. ML and DL are often combined in AI, along with other methods that are hard to use with traditional learning models. ML is an analytical technique that combines concepts from computer science, statistics, optimization, and probability \[[5](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_005)\]. Several important parts must be present for a ML program to work properly. The following are part of it: (1) the supply of raw data, (2) the availability of accurate prediction instances, and (3) an approach to test and confirm the extent to which the algorithm works. ML uses input data to build a model that shows a clear pattern, which lets people draw rational conclusions \[[6](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_006), [7](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_007)\]. DL is a clear subfield of the larger discipline of ML. DL involves the training of neural networks to acquire a multiplicity of layers, which typically range from tens to hundreds, in order to identify and represent complex patterns and relationships in data. A neural network is a computer model made up of neurons that are all linked to each other. The network can make nonlinear predictions when it is paired with an activation function. On the other hand, traditional ML techniques focus on extracting features, while DL methods use raw data to find good representations for classification \[[8](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_008)\]. DL has been used in a lot of different AI systems, some of which are meant to be used in medicine \[[9](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_009)\]. DL can be used for many things, such as organizing pictures into groups, letting cars drive themselves, recognizing speech, and turning text into music. In many situations, studies have shown that DL models work just as well as or better than people. Since ML and DL have made such big steps forward in the medical field, it is very important for doctors to fully understand the pros and cons of data-driven analysis tools.

## 5.3 Role of AI in audiology

ML advances have caused a big change in the area of auditory sciences. AI-based method makes it easier to diagnose hearing loss by letting auditory brainstem reactions be automatically put into groups and audiometric limits be calculated \[[10](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_010), [11](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_011)\]. In cases where traditional audiometry methods are not available, computational intelligence makes it easier to do hearing tests online. In the study, computational intelligence methods were used to guess how people who were diagnosed with sudden sensorineural hearing loss (SSNHL) would do with their hearing care \[[12](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_012)\]. Utilizing online testing, especially for people with SSNHL, can be seen as a practical way to begin treatment in areas on the edges where audiology services are scarce or not available at all. Utilizing AI is essential for making telemapping and troubleshooting for cochlear implants possible, which eliminates the need for people to travel to specialist centers. Furthermore, these services provide individuals with the chance to utilize specialized providers, thereby alleviating the monetary stresses associated with wage loss and transportation expenses \[[13](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_013)\]. This makes it easier for habilitationists, audiologists, and doctors to do effective follow-ups and urges family members to be involved in rehabilitation programs.

Auditory aid users often complain about how tough it is to understand speech in busy places. This issue is sometimes called the “cocktail party” issue. New advances in AI might be able to help solve this issue. Tracking and evaluating soundscapes is how AI can change the settings of a device based on what the listener wants in different situations. The integration of DL algorithms into the system has the potential to improve the efficacy of AI by increasing the speech-to-noise ratio at the listener’s tympanic membrane. This technological development successfully changes the sound experience from having background noise to having a calm atmosphere. AI can help people with preference listening, especially when they are listening to speakers they do not know or who sound different, by making it easier for them to change their attention. By looking at the listener’s electroencephalogram skills, the goal of the discussion can be reached by decoding the sound input that is meant for the receiver and then sending it as a separate signal. “Source separation” and “cognitive control” are two tools that are used to make this possible. The application of DL-based speech enhancement approaches can lead to improved programming for hearing aid users, resulting in higher satisfaction and increased speech intelligibility \[[14](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_014)\]. Within the context of those who heavily depend on lip-reading as their major means of communication, the incorporation of digital flashcards and speech-to-text software can prove to be invaluable tools, particularly in the context of the COVID-19 epidemic where face masks are commonly worn by those in their vicinity.

The utilization of DL in advanced AI systems has facilitated the development of methodologies that employ end-to-end decoding, employing neural networks with multiple layers. These systems can immediately convert acoustic waves into text, eliminating the necessity for a hidden Markov model or a distinct language model. This is in contrast to traditional approaches in automatic speech recognition. The application of automatic speech identification and AI allows for the interpretation of spoken language directly from brain cortex activity, resulting in either written output or generated voice waves. With the new developments, researchers have successfully made illustrations of direct neuroprosthesis systems that can read words and sentences right away by observing brain activity. A disease called anarthria makes it hard for people to control their vocal tract while speaking. It can be caused by cerebrovascular accidents or amyotrophic lateral sclerosis. Such devices are intended to assist people with this disease. Progress in speech neurosciences and AI could help people who are severely paralyzed regain their speaking skills. This would help them avoid the problems that come with natural conversation. Speech and language tests could be made better with AI, which would completely change how children who have hearing loss are restored. Several studies are currently looking into how AI can be used to predict what will happen with speech and language. To do this, brain scans of young children who are getting a cochlear implant can be looked at before the surgery. To achieve this goal, brain imaging techniques like T1 and diffusion tensor imaging scans are used. These are both types of magnetic resonance imaging (MRI). Following the implantation of a cochlear device, variations in language outcomes may occur among individuals. This is because the auditory system on the outside and the nerve system inside work together. This is because the appropriate functioning of both systems is essential for spoken language. Also, not being able to hear something changes the structure of the brain, which in turn changes the way understand and perceive spoken language. Being able to accurately guess what will happen can help make language therapy and listening training more personalized \[[15](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_015)\].

## 5.4 Role of AI in otology

The integration of AI is presently underway in the Otolaryngology-Head & Neck Surgery Department at The Ohio State University Wexner Medical Centre in Columbus. Specifically, researchers are focusing on the development of an innovative computer-assisted image processing software known as the “Auto-Scope.” The “Auto-Scope” is a classification system that categorizes tympanic membrane images into two groups: normal and pathological. This intervention has the potential to enhance diagnostic accuracy in primary care settings and mitigate the excessive treatment of ear diseases, thereby contributing to the reduction of antibiotic resistance. AI also contributes to the process of triage and the development of innovative and promising approaches in diagnosing dizziness. Additionally, AI assists in the conventional decision-making process for patients with vertigo \[[16](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_016)\]. [Figure 5.1](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_fig_001) presents an illustrative depiction of AI-based mechanism in the understanding of disease on the basis of the data generated with otoscopy, and then the suggestion in the diagnosis and treatment of the disease \[[17](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_017), [18](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_018)\].

![Figure 5.1: Representation of otoendoscopic image-based treatment intervention. (Created with BioRender.com)](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-005_fig_001.png)

Figure 5.1: Representation of otoendoscopic image-based treatment intervention. (Created with BioRender.com)

## 5.5 AI-based surgical and training applications in otology

The utilization of the virtual reality simulator, known as “Cardinal Sim,” enables the teaching of temporal bone dissection without the requirement of a human or cadaveric temporal bone specimen. The utilization of DL methodologies in this context facilitates the automated recognition of diverse crucial neurovascular components within temporal bone scan pictures, hence facilitating the application of patient-specific virtual reality otologic surgery. As per the findings of the researchers, it has been observed that DL algorithms exhibit the ability to effectively and precisely recognize segmental intracochlear anatomy \[[19](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_019)\]. This facilitates the selection of suitable cochlear implant electrodes and personalized cochlear implant programming.

Temporal bones can be produced in a three-dimensional (3D) format by the utilization of high-resolution CT scans of temporal bones, followed by the application of 3D printing technology for further processing. This tool possesses the capability to function as a valuable asset for surgeons to engage in preoperative surgical dissection practice. Moreover, previous studies have provided evidence to support the notion that this instrument can be utilized as a very efficient teaching aid \[[20](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_020)\].

## 5.6 Role of AI in rhinology

A diverse range of ML techniques are utilized within the domain of rhinology. One example of a supervised ML system is commonly referred to as “classification.” In this system, the ML algorithm makes predictions regarding the category or class to which an object belongs. When utilized, this technique could distinguish between sinuses that are opacified and sinuses that are not opacified on computed tomography (CT) pictures.

Another ML technique commonly employed is known as “clustering.” The methodology employed involves the utilization of an unsupervised ML algorithm for the purpose of categorizing things that demonstrate commonalities, principally relying on their intrinsic characteristics. Clustering is utilized to discern and automatically classify images obtained from endoscopic sinus surgery. The application of convolutional neural network (CNN) enables the precise identification of the anatomical structures within the nasal cavities, as well as the quantification of sinus opacification \[[21](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_021), [22](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_022)\].

The convolutional neural network, known as ‘Inception,’ excels in reliably classifying the integrity of the osteomeatal complex on CT scans of the paranasal sinuses. This achievement is reflected by an accuracy rate of 85% \[[23](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_023)\]. The ‘random forest’ model, a relatively new ML approach, has provided evidence that the prediction of baseline olfactory function in individuals with chronic sinusitis can be achieved by considering the levels of mucus cytokine interleukins 5 and 13.

## 5.7 Role of AI in laryngology

Computational learning methodologies have been utilized in voice recognition software to assist in the identification and assessment of diverse laryngeal disorders. It has been observed that ML classifiers outperform rule-based algorithms in this context \[[24](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_024)\]. It was almost 100% accurate to diagnose T1a glottis cancers and other vocal cord lesions when speech recognition and videostroboscopy pictures were used with AI tools \[[25](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_025)\]. Researchers looked into how to make pictures taken with a larynx instantly recognizable. A DL method was used to tell the difference between the different types of laryngeal cancers. A total of 24,667 laryngoscopy pictures were used for the study. These images showed both healthy people and people with vocal cord polyps, vocal cord nodules, leukoplakia, and laryngeal cancer.

A CNN-based predictor was created, and then it was tested with clinical visual assessments (CVAs). Images of the larynx were put into groups using a transfer learning method called the “ResNet 101 model.” The researchers found that the average accuracy rate was 96.24%. When it came to most laryngeal situations, the CNN classifier performed better than the computer-aided vocal analysis method. Despite the large size of this dataset, the current model is not suitable for diagnosing laryngeal papillomas. This was due to the highly unpredictable distribution of these lesions throughout the larynx, which lacks a consistent location for the CNN to effectively concentrate on and accurately categorize the lesion. AI-based diagnostic algorithms play a crucial role in enhancing physicians’ diagnosis accuracy, especially when dealing with early-stage malignancies or precancerous lesions. The purpose of a diagnostic system, which utilizes AI, is to complement rather than substitute the clinical evaluation conducted by a clinician. This technology facilitates expedited decision-making and provides guidance to healthcare professionals in determining the need for additional investigations, such as a biopsy, when presented with a potentially concerning lesion. Telemedicine can also provide advantages to patients residing in rural and remote regions \[[26](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_026)\].

## 5.8 Role of AI in head-and-neck oncology

DL networks are now utilized in head and neck cancer to enhance the classification of anatomical features, hence expediting the radiotherapy planning procedure. The utilization of this method can be implemented in order to safeguard crucial structures over the course of radiation therapy \[[27](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_027)\]. The researchers utilized ML methods in their investigation to predict the occurrence of nodal disease. This was achieved by the analysis of patterns of gene expression in primary oropharyngeal carcinomas in squamous cells that are linked to human papillomavirus (HPV). The researchers utilized a 40-gene profile \[[28](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_028)\]. The most dependable prognostic indicator in head and neck squamous cell carcinoma is the assessment of HPV status. The researchers in a study used AI methods to build a proteomic signature that can be used to predict whether oral cancer patients will develop lymph node metastases \[[29](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_029)\]. The investigators at Stanford University did a study wherein they utilized an ML algorithm for training it with the specific goal of visually detecting oropharyngeal tumors. The accomplishment was attained through white light as well as narrow-band imaging methodologies. The principal objective was to establish an automated detection technique for oropharyngeal cancers to enhance early diagnosis \[[30](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_030)\]. The objective of the research conducted by investigators \[[31](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_031)\] was to train a neural network to identify nodal metastases and extranodal extension in patients diagnosed with head and neck cancer utilizing preoperative CT imaging.

A novel form of tumor marker, known as “radiomics,” has been developed by the examination of quantitative imaging data. The progress of radiomics is driven by computational innovations that facilitate the examination of extensive amounts of imaging data, encompassing CT, positron emission tomography scans, MRI, and functional imaging research. The field of radiomics has been created as a means of addressing the constraints associated with tumor markers obtained from tissue and blood samples. This is due to the fact that tissue markers obtained from tumor tissue biopsies only capture a limited portion of the tumor at a certain moment in time, and frequently fail to accurately reflect the tumor’s overall biology or any changes in biology that may occur during and after therapy. Radiomics is widely regarded as a highly appropriate methodology for the needs of therapy monitoring and assessing treatment response. Liquid biopsies provide a comprehensive representation of tumor-secreted components, although they do not offer any spatial resolution of the tumor. In the field of radiomics, the characterization of tumor tissue encompasses a comprehensive analysis of various aspects, including conventional radiological features. This entails a thorough examination of the whole 3D information pertaining to the composition, shape, voxel intensity distribution, and texture of the tumor within the occupied volume. Furthermore, radiomics enables the iterative and noninvasive processing of follow-up images. The utilization of radiomics enables the examination of both tumor tissue and healthy tissue, facilitating the prediction of treatment-related adverse effects through the implementation of a specialized resolution. A constraint that arises when utilizing radiomics in the area of the head and neck is the existence of artifacts produced by metallic implants, like dental fillings \[[32](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_032)\].

The application of AI in conjunction with hyperspectral imaging has been employed to discern between thyroid cancers and normal head and neck tissue. The previously indicated methodology has yielded promising results, with a sensitivity rate of 97%, a specificity rate of 96%, and a total accuracy rate of 96%. This capability allows surgeons to establish more precise margins during surgical excision \[[33](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_033)\]. The Bethesda method for characterizing thyroid cytopathology provides a clear clinical decision-making framework for lesions classified as Bethesda II (benign) and Bethesda VI (malignant). The Bethesda III‒V classification encompasses indeterminate categories that present a clinical challenge, requiring physicians to consider various treatment alternatives such as repeat fine-needle aspiration cytology, observation, molecular testing, and surgical intervention to achieve a definitive histological diagnosis. The utilization of AI for identifying thyroid cancers via ultrasound pictures and the creation of ML algorithms for analyzing digital cytopathology slides have demonstrated promising progress. The aforementioned improvements possess the capacity to effectively diagnose the existence or nonexistence of thyroid cancer, hence facilitating the prevention of superfluous surgical interventions. ML algorithms play a crucial duty as a supplementary tool in aiding clinicians to decrease the number of uncertain cases.

## 5.9 Role of AI in augmenting tele-otoscopy

AI is an extensive field dedicated to the creation of computer systems that replicate human intelligence \[[1](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_001)\]. ML is a specific subset of AI that utilizes algorithms to generate predictions from data, independent of direct instructions from human programmers \[[34](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_034)\]. Although healthcare providers have the diagnosis, the accuracy of diagnosing otitis media is still quite poor. The research conducted by investigators indicated that the diagnostic precision in differentiating otitis media with effusion, acute otitis media (AOM), and a normal tympanic membrane by filmed pneumatic otoscopy was found to be 50% for pediatricians and 73% for otolaryngologists. The aforementioned issue is of significant concern because of the substantial disease burden associated with otitis media. This condition ranks as the second most prevalent contributor to hearing impairment globally, surpassed solely by age-related hearing loss and other etiologies \[[35](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_035)\]. It is recommended that primary care physicians adopt a strategy wherein the utilization of otoscopy is restricted to instances where there exists a substantial likelihood of AOM prior to conducting the test. The objective is to mitigate patient discomfort and anxiety, while concurrently lowering the quantity of antibiotic prescriptions \[[36](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_036)\]. Nevertheless, this methodology fails to tackle the fundamental problem of inadequate diagnostic precision and could potentially result in further undetected diagnoses, exacerbating the diminishing training and expertise necessary for effective otoscopy.

The emergence of AI and ML technologies has shown a significant potential for enhancing both in-person and telemedicine services through the visual diagnosis of ear disorders. Comparable advancements are occurring in various other medical disciplines. An exemplary example pertains to the utilization of ML methodologies for distinguishing among melanoma as well as carcinoma in contrast to benign skin lesions \[[37](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_037)\]. Substantial advancements have occurred in ophthalmology, enhancing the detection and assessment of several ocular illnesses, including glaucoma, age-related macular degeneration, diabetic retinopathy, retinopathy of prematurity, and related conditions. The use of digital fundus pictures and visual fields has been associated with these advancements \[[38](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_038)\]. In the field of otolaryngology, there are current efforts being made to enhance the incorporation of ML methodologies within the dental profession. The objective is to enable dentists to effectively identify cases of oral and oropharyngeal carcinomas of squamous cells by the use of these advanced computational methods. This task presents a nontrivial challenge, particularly in light of the fact that a significant proportion of the American adult population, approximately 64.9%, seeks dental care on an annual basis.

The utilization of ML techniques for image categorization necessitates multiple layers of processing due to the intricate nature of clinical images. Consequently, these networks are occasionally designated as CNNs because of their structural resemblance to biological neural networks. CNNs represent a category of DL methodologies. This process involves supplying a specific input, such as an otoscopic image, to the neural network. Subsequently, the network classifies this image into one of multiple categories, such as associating it with a probable illness state. The following studies have utilized examination results, specifically labeled by otolaryngologists, as the “training set” to train CNNs in order to subsequently label new data that is unfamiliar to the algorithm, referred to as the “test set.” The objective is for the network to accurately replicate or potentially surpass the labeling accuracy that can be attained by a human.

Researchers developed a training dataset by combining otoscopic examination images from Google Images and clinically obtained images. Participants meticulously assigned distinct labels to all 1,366 pictures in the training set, categorizing them into one of 14 potential otologic diagnoses. The diagnoses encompassed normal ear conditions, cholesteatoma, AOM, extruded tympanostomy tube (TT), and appropriately positioned TT, among others. The researchers subsequently employed the Google Cloud Vision AutoML system to assess a test set of 89 photos, achieving a diagnosis accuracy of 88.7%. Subsequently, an assessment was conducted to compare the algorithm’s performance with that of healthcare practitioners who specialize in otolaryngology, pediatrics, emergency medicine, and family medicine. The study found that the algorithm achieved an aggregate physician performance of 58.9%, indicating a considerable improvement compared to the performance of the physician group \[[39](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_039)\]. Prior studies have utilized datasets of varied magnitudes, incorporating various diagnostic classifications, and utilizing a range of underlying computational methodologies. Researchers utilized a training dataset consisting of 10,544 otoendoscopic images, with each image assigned to one of six distinct diagnostic categories. The algorithm devised by the authors attained a mean diagnosis accuracy of 93.67%. However, the study did not include a direct comparison of physician accuracy. Researchers proposed an alternate way utilizing video-otoscopic data obtained from an Android smartphone. The visual data captured by the smartphone application is subsequently communicated to an algorithm hosted on a cloud platform, which subsequently delivers the results to the user of the application. The outcomes can be classified into five particular categories: normal, obstructed by wax or foreign material, otitis media with effusion, AOM, and chronic suppurative otitis media. The system’s accuracy ranges from 81.58% to 86.86%, depending on its unique properties. The authors propose that the utilization of this platform could be particularly advantageous in developing nations, given the widespread and swift integration of smartphone technology on a global scale \[[40](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_040)\]. Each of these cases illustrates the capability of AI in facilitating the connection between primary and secondary or tertiary healthcare, leading to faster assessment and referral of conditions that go beyond the expertise of general practitioners. This is especially advantageous in areas with restricted access to expert medical practitioners.

There exist some unresolved issues regarding the application of ML technology in the context of automated otoscopic diagnoses, namely within the field of telehealth. Initially, it is important to acquire images of superior quality, ensuring low heterogeneity. The presence of heterogeneity within a dataset has the potential to introduce bias into the algorithm \[[41](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_041)\]. When an algorithm is exposed to a combination of otomicroscopic and hand-held otoscopic pictures, there is a possibility for bias to emerge, preferring the identification of more severe conditions linked to the otomicroscope modality. Nonetheless, it is crucial to acknowledge that this bias would solely result from the composition of the training set and should be regarded as a mere artifact. Additionally, the inclusion of high-resolution photographs is necessary, and the proficiency and area of expertise of the supplier may impact the precision of the produced results. Lastly, it is worth noting that the algorithms outlined above mostly depend on visual input. Nevertheless, ongoing research is being undertaken to include supplementary attributes, such as intraoperative myringotomy observations, into these algorithms with the aim of augmenting their diagnostic precision. Despite the above-described obstacles, it is unequivocal that AI will profoundly impact otologic care in the forthcoming years, aiming to enhance the timeliness of specialist treatment for patients.

## 5.10 Operation-based use of otoscopes in the telehealth setting

### 5.10.1 Guardian-operated

The CellScope, a device developed by CellScope, Inc. located in San Francisco, CA, has been extensively studied and explored. The composition of this device includes a structural component resembling an iPhone cover, a specialized otoscope head designed to facilitate the alignment of the backward-facing camera’s flashlight and an ear speculum. The iPhone has the capability to download and install the CellScope program, which enables users to acquire and store videos.

The study conducted by researchers, involved the participation of parents of 40 patients ranging from 3 months to 17 years of age who sought medical attention at the pediatric otolaryngology clinic. These parents were given a CellScope box along with an instructional video in the English language \[[42](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_042)\]. The participants received guidance regarding the process of imaging the middle ear; however, they did not receive any additional support or aid from the healthcare professionals. Subsequently, a second-year resident specializing in otolaryngology employed the aforementioned instrument, after which the patient had examination by the attending otolaryngologist, who conducted an assessment via otoscope. Without knowing where the photos came from, an otolaryngologist examined the pictures that the parents and resident doctors had taken. The otolaryngologist evaluated the visibility of anatomy markers and attempted to establish a diagnosis solely on the basis of the provided photographs. The findings of the study revealed a relatively low level of agreement among raters (*k* = 0.42) when diagnosing based on parent video in comparison to pneumatic otoscopy. Conversely, a high level of agreement (*k* = 0.71) was observed between resident diagnoses and pneumatic otoscopy. The agreement between resident physician and parent videos in terms of identified landmarks was found to be quite low (*k* = 0.087). Therefore, the study ultimately determined that the utilization of CellScope by parents for remote otoscopy was deemed unreliable.

Researchers conducted an additional study in Finland to investigate the utilization of CellScope by parents. The study involved a sample of 41 participants, aged between 10 and 34 months, who were randomly assigned to either the direct instruction group or a delayed teaching group \[[43](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_043)\]. The parents within the teaching group were presented with an explanation regarding the anatomical features of the middle and inner ear, which was afterward followed by an instructional session on the procedure for taking videos utilizing CellScope. In the delayed intervention condition, parents received instructions to use the CellScope equipment for video recording, without any formal training until 1 week later. The study’s findings reveal that the immediate teaching group displayed a 62% rate of videos with superior technical quality, but the delayed instruction group exhibited a much lower rate of 22%. Following the instructional period of 1 week, it was observed that 64% of the movies in the delayed group exhibited satisfactory quality. Following the distribution of instructions to both groups, it was possible to determine the normal functioning of the ear, the existence of otitis media with effusion, or the emergence of AOM in 40% of cases. The research showed that parents need more thorough training to make diagnostic videos better and more useful. The data showed that in both groups, AOM can be confirmed or ruled out in 87% of cases where patients have severe symptoms.

Remote otoscopy can be used for more than just diagnosing AOM. Researchers looked into how CellScope could be used to keep an eye on the insertion of a TT \[[44](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_044)\]. Twenty-one kids between the ages of 6 months and 15 years old who had bilateral TT implanted were watched for an average of 17 months. Two separate otolaryngologists looked at the videos that the parents took every month. The tubes’ status was assessed by these specialists, who specifically determined whether they were open, extruding, or occluded. The researchers also listened to the records to see if there were any signs of otorrhea, middle ear effusion, granulation tissue, tympanic membrane perforation, or AOM. The first results showed that the CellScope videos and in-office otoscopy were about 80% accurate at finding a tube that was blocked, partially extruded, or completely extruded. Therefore, the results show that CellScope can be used for more than just AOM. The strong link between the usual office method and CellScope shows how well it works for remote tracking of the TT.

### 5.10.2 Healthcare-provider-operated

A thorough investigation has been carried out into how otoscopes are used by medical professionals. Researchers did a study to see how well CellScope worked as a tool for diagnosing AOM when used by emergency care doctors. The study used a crossover randomized controlled trial design and took place in a single tertiary pediatric emergency room \[[45](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_045)\]. Two doctors looked over a group of 96 patients, aged 1–4 years. The check was done by doctors using either the CellScope or a regular otoscope. The doctors were randomly given each piece of equipment. The results were compared to those of a binocular exam done by a pediatric otolaryngology expert. The result showed that the doctors’ ratings were more accurate at diagnosing than the visiting pediatric otolaryngologist’s binocular exam. The accuracy was 0.74 for CellScope and 0.69 for a traditional otoscope (*p* < 0.05).

A cross-sectional study was done in a rural area of Greenland to find out how common AOM is there. Individuals who are not medical experts filmed the ear canal using a Cupris TYM, an otoscope that is easily attached to smartphones. After that, the tapes were sent to otolaryngologists for the examination \[[46](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_046)\]. Four hundred and eighty video records were made by 84 children between the ages of 1 and 6. After that, the tapes were sent to three otologists, who each did their own evaluations. The job of otolaryngologists was to judge how well the movies showed the tympanic membrane so that AOM could be correctly diagnosed. The results indicate that the average percentage of valuable movies assessed by the three otologists was 18.1%, with a modified interrater agreement coefficient of 0.67. The panel determined that the Cupris gadget offered no significant advantages in this context. Moreover, they underscored the necessity for supplementary training for individuals lacking specific skills to enhance the device’s broader application.

In order to determine the occurrence rate of chronic suppurative otitis media in rural Malawi, researchers employed the use of Dino-Lite, a portable otoscope with a smartphone-compatible external connector, to conduct a structural evaluation of 281 children aged 4–6 years \[[47](#9783111547589_008896.xhtml#b_9783111549729-005_ref_047)\]. The data acquired was transmitted to two otolaryngologists located remotely by healthcare staff who do not specialize in this field. When there was suspicion of pathology, the children were directed to a specialized otolaryngology center at the tertiary level. As per the assessment conducted by the independent otolaryngologists, the quality of the images was classified as extraordinary in 15.3% of cases, acceptable in 72.4% of the cases, and insufficient in 3.2% of situations.

## 5.11 Conclusion

AI is significantly transforming the healthcare sector by improving treatment quality, lowering expenses, and augmenting access to medical services. A significant number of these applications necessitate access to substantial resource centers and environments. Nevertheless, this technology possesses distinctive qualities that enable its utilization even in the most disadvantaged regions, as it assumes the diagnostic responsibilities often performed by healthcare professionals. Nevertheless, AI functions solely as a supplementary tool for healthcare professionals and should not be regarded as a replacement for human medical practitioners. Its primary role is to offer significant assistance to physicians when faced with complex scenarios, relying on patient data to provide valuable suggestions. The acquisition of unparalleled knowledge in the areas of diagnosis, patient care, and treatment outcomes promises a new approach characterized by enhanced quality and efficacy in medical care. AI possesses the capability to revolutionize the field of otorhinolaryngology, indicating a promising outlook for the future.
