# Chapter 7 AI in dentistry: role and application

#### Abstract

The incorporation of artificial intelligence (AI) into daily activities has accelerated, particularly during the past decade, with significant progress in the field of medicine. AI, defined as machines doing tasks that usually necessitate human intellect, originated in 1950 but became feasible approximately 20 years ago. The swift advancement of three critical elements – extensive data from digital devices, improved processing power, and advanced AI algorithms – has enabled the rise of applications that augment daily assistance. AI is progressively employed in dentistry across multiple specialties, such as operative dentistry, orthodontics, periodontics, oral and maxillofacial surgery, and prosthodontics, mostly concentrating on diagnostic functions related to radiographic and optical imaging. This focus on image-based analysis arises from the comparative simplicity of implementing AI in this domain, as opposed to other dental tasks that encounter difficulties concerning data accessibility, consistency, and the computer resources required for processing three-dimensional data. Evidence-based dentistry (EBD) is acknowledged as the superior paradigm for informed decision-making among dental practitioners. Simultaneously, AI and machine learning (ML) models acquire knowledge from human expertise, providing a significant resource to assist dental practitioners in many clinical situations. The collaboration between EBD and AI/ML improves care quality, indicating a potential future for AI technology integration in dental practice. This chapter elucidates the function and utilization of AI in dentistry.

Keywords:  Artificial intelligence,  machine learning,  neural network,  dentistry,  evidence-based dentistry,  orthognathic surgery,  orthodontics,  

## 7.1 Introduction

Artificial intelligence (AI) has been substantially incorporated into various areas, including robotics, automotive, smart city infrastructure, and financial analysis, among others. Furthermore, the application of AI has been noted in the fields of medical and dentistry. The applications include medical and dental imaging diagnostics, precision and digital medicine, decision support systems, wearable technology, drug research, hospital monitoring, and the development of robotic and virtual assistants. AI is often perceived as a beneficial instrument that can assist dentists and clinicians in alleviating their workload. In addition to utilizing a singular information source tailored to a certain ailment for diagnostic purposes, AI has the capacity to acquire knowledge from numerous information sources, known as multi-modal data, in order to surpass human skills in the field of diagnosis. Researchers have used fundus photos along with other relevant health information, like body mass index (BMI), age, smoking habit, gender, blood pressure levels, and the chance of having diabetes, to guess the occurrence of heart disease \[[1](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_001)\]. Therefore, AI can tell the difference between a lot of different medical conditions, such as diabetic retinopathy and heart disease, by taking fundus photograph. Image-based research that uses AI has shown that it is reliable and has had a lot of progress. The things listed above depend on how quickly computer hardware gets better, how quickly algorithmic study gets done on software, and how readily available large databases are as input data. There is a lot of promise and possibility for using AI in dentistry and medicine.

A number of studies are being done or have already been done to look into how AI could be used in dentistry. These efforts are mostly aimed at different areas, such as evaluation, planning treatments, making decisions, predicting how treatments will work, and figuring out how long a disease will last.

## 7.2 AI in dentistry

In recent years, there has been a big rise in the use of AI in dentistry, which is similar to what has happened in other fields. In the dentistry field, AI is used in a number of different areas, such as diagnosis, treatment planning, decision-making, and predicting how well a treatment will work. In the area of dentistry, diagnosis is where AI is most often used. The use of AI in dentistry could improve the accuracy and usefulness of diagnoses, making work easier for dentists. Consider the fact that dentists are becoming more and more reliant on computer programs to make decisions \[[2](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_002), [3](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_003)\]. On the other hand, computer programs made for dentists are becoming more and more intelligent, accurate, and reliable. An increasing amount of study into AI has spread to many areas of dentistry \[[4](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_004)\].

## 7.3 AI in operative dentistry

Historically, dental practitioners have employed visual and tactile assessment, as well as radiographic examination, to diagnose caries based on a comprehensive set of criteria. There are, however, tight interproximal contacts, deep cracks, and secondary lesions that make it hard to find early-stage lesions. In due course, a considerable number of dental caries lesions are identified solely during the later phases, necessitating more intricate treatment options such as dental crowns, root canal therapy, or potentially also dental implants. Dental radiography, encompassing periapical, panoramic, and bitewing views, as well as the use of explorers or dental probes, has gained significant recognition as dependable diagnostic methods for detecting dental caries. Nonetheless, it is important to acknowledge that a significant aspect of the screening and final diagnosis continues to depend largely on the proficiency and experience of dentists.

A multitude of studies has been conducted in operative dentistry to investigate several aspects, including the identification of dental caries, apical lesions, vertical root fractures (VRFs), measurement of pulp space volume, and evaluation of tooth wear \[[5](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_005), [6](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_006)\]. The grayscale image of a two-dimensional (2D) radiograph is composed of pixels, with each pixel having an intensity value that correlates with the density of the item being imaged. By acquiring knowledge from the aforementioned attributes, an AI program can assimilate the discernible patterns and generate predictions for the purpose of segmenting teeth, detecting caries, and other related tasks. This is seen in the research undertaken by scholars \[[7](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_007)\], who developed a convolutional neural network (CNN) algorithm for detecting dental cavities in periapical radiographs. In another study researchers \[[8](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_008)\] introduced a CNN algorithm for identifying caries in intraoral pictures. In a distinct study, researchers \[[9](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_009)\] performed a comparison analysis to evaluate the cost-effectiveness of AI in identifying proximal caries, alongside the diagnostic accuracy of dentists. The study’s findings revealed that AI exhibited greater efficacy and reduced expenses in this environment.

Numerous studies previously mentioned have shown that AI has excellent outcomes in the early identification of lesions, exhibiting accuracy comparable to or even surpassing that of dental specialists. The successful attainment of this milestone necessitates the collaborative efforts of computer scientists and doctors from several disciplines. The radiographic pictures are manually annotated by physicians to indicate the specific location of caries, while computer scientists are responsible for preparing the dataset and developing the machine learning (ML) algorithm. Ultimately, the collaborative effort between physicians and computer scientists is employed to thoroughly examine and validate the training outcomes in terms of their correctness and precision \[[10](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_010)\].

## 7.4 AI in periodontics

Periodontitis is a common illness that impacts a substantial population. Dental issues impose a considerable strain on many individuals, and if neglected, they can lead to tooth mobility and eventual loss of teeth \[[11](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_011)\]. In order to mitigate the development of severe periodontitis, it is imperative to promptly identify and address the condition by early identification and intervention. The diagnosis of periodontal disease in clinical practice depends on assessing pocket probing depths and gingival recession. The application of the PSI (periodontal screening index) is a common practice in the quantification of clinical attachment loss. Nonetheless, the clinical assessment in question exhibits limited reliability due to its dependence on the subjective judgment of dentists, who may overlook instances of localized periodontal tissue loss \[[12](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_012)\].

AI is primarily used in periodontics for the purpose of diagnosing periodontitis and categorizing various forms of periodontal diseases \[[13](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_013), [14](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_014)\]. Furthermore, researchers employed CNN for detecting PBL (periodontal bone loss) in panoramic radiographs. In a different study, researchers \[[15](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_015)\] conducted an evaluation of proposed CNN algorithm with the aim of assessing its potential utility and precision in the automatic detection of periodontally damaged teeth. Another study indicated that \[[16](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_016)\] a CNN-based algorithm may be applied to evaluate periodontal infections. They used data pertaining to the health system as a whole to build this algorithm.

## 7.5 AI in orthodontics

The way an orthodontist treats a patient is usually based on what they know and their own personal opinions. In the field of orthodontics, the development of treatment programs necessitates a collaborative effort between orthodontists and patients, in which the distinctive characteristics and needs of each individual are appropriately assessed. Determining malocclusion has traditionally been a very difficult task in the field of orthodontics. This is because there are so many factors that need to be carefully thought through during cephalometric study. Because of this, it is very hard to come up with the right treatment plan and exactly predict what will happen \[[17](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_017)\]. AI is an extremely beneficial technology for addressing orthodontic issues. Within the domain of orthodontics, AI exhibits several uses. These applications mostly involve treatment planning and the ability to predict treatment outcomes. Notably, AI can simulate the anticipated alterations in the visual aspects of face images prior to and subsequent to orthodontic therapy. Incorporating AI algorithms into lateral cephalograms improves patient-dentist communication by providing a more accurate depiction of the orthodontic treatment’s effects on skeletal patterns and anatomical landmarks \[[18](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_018)\].

Researchers \[[19](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_019)\] developed a decision support system utilizing Bayesian principle to identify the necessity of orthodontic therapy. The system receives orthodontics-related data as input for the purpose of analysis. In a previous study, a researcher \[[20](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_020)\] proposed the utilization of an ANN model to evaluate the need for extractions based on lateral cephalometric radiographs. Researchers also established an equivalent evaluation framework \[[21](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_021)\]. AI has been employed not only for the purpose of predicting the required extractions in orthodontics, but also for the purpose of recognizing cephalometric features. The study conducted by researchers \[[22](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_022), [23](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_023)\] showcased a deep learning algorithm that effectively and accurately identifies cephalometric landmarks on radiographs. Bulatova et al. \[[24](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_024)\] and Kunz et al. \[[25](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_025)\] independently devised AI systems that exhibited equivalent accuracies to human examiners in the identification of those landmarks. Additionally, a method for the automated categorization of skeletal components using lateral cephalometric radiographs was suggested by researchers \[[26](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_026)\].

Furthermore, the utilization of AI algorithms has been observed in the domain of orthodontic treatment planning, alongside the recognition of diverse cephalometric markers and classification. Based on the study of lateral cephalometric images, researchers \[[27](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_027)\] created an AI model that can decide if surgery is needed. Marker detection and treatment planning, two tasks that take a lot of time for orthodontic professionals, seem to be the main topics of most orthodontic apps. An important part of planning orthodontic treatment is dividing and sorting teeth into groups. Radiography and full-arch three-dimensional (3D) digital visual scans are two examples where AI has been used. A group of AI methods were used by the researchers to automatically separate the teeth on a digital model of teeth that was made using a 3D intraoral scanner \[[28](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_028)\] and cone-beam computed tomography (CBCT) images \[[29](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_029)\]. Researchers did more than just segmentation of teeth; they also did segmentation of the alveolar bone. This segmentation method worked 500 times faster than the doctors’ and was 300 times more accurate. The experts say that the system works well in various difficult situations involving different dental problems \[[30](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_030)\].

In recent years, using AI in personalized orthodontic care has become a very critical and in-depth topic of study. Using AI has made it possible to plan, diagnose dental problems, and keep an eye on treatment \[[31](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_031)\]. Radiographs and pictures taken by intraoral scanners and cameras have a lot of promise for use in diagnosis and planning treatment. This method gets results that are often more accurate than those obtained through human perception \[[32](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_032)\], so it gets rid of the need for many lab tests and communicating to patients to get their opinions. By using accurate 3D scanning methods and virtual modeling, it is possible to use 3D printing to make braces that are specific to a certain treatment plan. A complex algorithm is used to carefully figure out the best amount of force and duration for moving teeth, as well as the exact pressure locations for one or more teeth, while huge amounts of data are being processed. With the help of AI, aligners can shorten treatment times and make it easier to keep appointments. They can also make sure that treatment is done correctly and that progress is tracked well \[[33](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_033)\].

## 7.6 AI in oral and maxillofacial pathology

The area of oral and maxillofacial pathology (OMFP) is very specialized and focuses on carefully looking at and correctly diagnosing diseases and disorders that affect the mouth, face, and jaw. Oral cancer is thought to be the worst sign of oral potentially malignant diseases (OPMDs). Using information from the World Health Organization (WHO), the number of cases of mouth cancer each year exceeds 657,000, with an estimated 330,000 deaths \[[34](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_034)\]. A lot of work has been done in the field of OMFP to figure out how to use AI to find and diagnose tumors and other cancers. The main focus of the study is on using X-ray, microscopic, and ultrasonographic pictures to make diagnoses. In addition, radiographic pictures can be used to find abnormal body parts using AI \[[35](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_035)\]. This includes figuring out where the nerves are in the mouth, the muscles between the teeth on the tongue, and the parotid and salivary glands. Many studies have shown that CNN algorithms are good at automatically finding cancerous growths \[[36](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_036)\]. In the care of people with cleft lip and palate, it is important to know that AI plays a part by helping with risk assessment, pre-surgical orthopedics, diagnosis, speech assessment, and surgery \[[37](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_037)\].

It is very important to quickly find and diagnose different mucosal sores in order to tell the difference between benign and malignant diseases. Malignant growths need to be removed surgically. However, many tumors look alike, so biopsy slides and X-rays are needed to make a correct diagnosis. Pathologists use a technique called microscopic analysis \[[38](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_038)\] to find diseases by carefully looking at the shape of stained samples that are put on glass slides. Pathologists have the difficult job of doing hard work that requires a lot of effort. About 20% of all tests that need to be looked at are found to be cancerous. This means that AI could be a useful tool for helping doctors with their work.

Researchers \[[39](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_039)\] used a CNN method to find OPMDs and oral squamous cell carcinoma (OSCC) in optical images taken inside the mouth. A technique called optical coherence tomography has also been used to find and study both normal and malignant growths in the oral mucosa, along with intraoral optical imaging. In a different study \[[40](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_040)\], researchers used ANN and SVM models to tell the difference between oral tumors that were cancerous and those that were not. Researchers \[[41](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_041)\] used a CNN design called AlexNet (17) in a different study to tell the difference between healthy and unhealthy mucosa in the head and neck area. To find OSCC on their own, researchers in another study \[[42](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_042)\] used a CNN method to look at confocal laser endomicroscopy images. The results of the study showed that the CNN method used in the study was very good at finding squamous cell carcinoma quickly. A CNN method was used by the researchers to successfully separate and classify ameloblastoma and keratocystic odontogenic tumor \[[43](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_043)\]. Radiographic pictures of two oral tumors show features that are similar to each other. By looking at the computer-generated results side by side with the biopsy data, it was found that the CNN algorithm had an 83% success rate and could diagnose in 38 s. The numbers that were seen were similar to those that experts in oral and maxillofacial surgery have written down.

The integration of AI in oral surgery is mostly exemplified by the significant advancements made in robotic surgery, wherein the replication of human body movement and intellect is achieved. In clinical settings, many image-guided cranial surgery techniques have demonstrated success. Among these procedures are tooth implants, biopsies, removal of tumors and foreign objects, and surgery on the temporomandibular joint. Comparative investigations on oral implant surgery have revealed a notable enhancement in precision when utilizing guided implant placement techniques, as opposed to the conventional freehand approach, even when executed by proficient surgeons. Furthermore, a lack of noticeable distinction was observed between skilled surgeons and individuals through training. Previous studies have consistently shown several advantages, including reduced operation time, improved intraoperative accuracy, and enhanced safety during handling near fragile anatomical structures. The utilization of image guidance in surgical procedures has the potential to enhance the extent of surgical resection, hence potentially diminishing the necessity for further correction surgeries \[[44](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_044)\]. The area of surgery has seen a great transformation due to the incorporation of AI. As a result, numerous robotic surgeons have emerged, demonstrating an increasing level of proficiency in performing semi-automated surgical procedures while being closely monitored by experienced surgeons \[[45](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_045)\].

## 7.7 AI in prosthodontics

The dental crown preparation treatment protocol in the field of prosthodontics generally consists of a sequential set of procedures. These processes include tooth preparation, acquisition of impressions, trimming of casts, design of the restoration, manufacturing of the crown, try-in of the crown, and finally, cementation. The integration of AI in the discipline of prosthodontics mostly focuses on the area of restorative design. The use of computer-aided design/computer-aided manufacturing (CAD/CAM) technology has revolutionized the design process in numerous commercial products, including CEREC, Sirona, 3Shape, and other notable examples. This transformation is mostly attributed to the digitization of the workflow facilitated by CAD/CAM technology. Despite the significant enhancement in design process efficiency through the utilization of a tooth library for crown design, the current approach remains incapable of attaining personalized designs tailored to the unique requirements of specific patients \[[46](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_046)\]. Scholars \[[47](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_047), [48](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_048)\] have proposed novel techniques that employ 2D-GAN models to generate crowns by using knowledge derived from designs developed by technicians, in light of the progress made in the domain of AI. The training dataset consisted of 2D depth maps that were derived from 3D tooth models. In their study, researchers \[[49](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_049)\] presented findings on a 3DDCGAN network employed for crown generation. This network incorporated 3D data directly into the crown generation process, resulting in created crowns that exhibited morphological similarities to genuine teeth. The potential for greatly enhancing workflow efficiency exists through the incorporation of AI into CAD/CAM or 3D/4D printing \[[50](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_050)\]. The utilization of AI has also been observed in the domain of shade matching \[[51](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_051)\], as well as in the prognostication of debonding for CAD/CAM restorations \[[52](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_052)\].

In addition to fixed prosthodontics, the design process in detachable prosthodontics presents greater challenges due to the necessity to address a wider range of characteristics and variables. There is currently no ML algorithm specifically developed for the design of removable dentures; however, numerous expert systems based on knowledge have been launched in the field \[[53](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_053), [54](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_054), [55](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_055)\]. Currently, ML algorithms predominantly focus on assisting in the design procedure for removable dentures. This encompasses activities such as the categorization of dental arches \[[56](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_056)\] and the prognostication of facial aesthetics in toothless persons \[[57](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_057)\].

## 7.8 AI in endodontics

The domain of endodontics is experiencing a growing recognition of the significance of AI \[[58](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_058)\]. The paramount importance of CBCT in the domains of endodontic treatment strategy and disease diagnostics is currently on the rise \[[59](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_059)\]. Networks powered by AI can spot changes as small as a single pixel, which humans could miss \[[60](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_060)\]. The following sections provide more detail on the various endodontic applications described in [Figure 7.1](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_fig_001).

![Figure 7.1: Potential applications of artificial intelligence in the field of dentistry.](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-007_fig_001.png)

Figure 7.1: Potential applications of artificial intelligence in the field of dentistry.

### 7.8.1 Periapical lesions detection

It might be challenging for clinicians to diagnose teeth with periapical lesions and/or associated symptoms and come up with an appropriate treatment plan. About three quarters of those with radiolucent lesions in the jaw have apical periodontitis \[[61](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_061)\]. Early diagnosis has the ability to improve the efficacy of treatment measures, which in turn reduces the likelihood of the disease spreading to neighboring tissues and the severity of any consequences that may arise \[[62](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_062)\]. Intraoral periapical radiography (IOPA) and orthopantomography are the two most common 2D diagnostic methods for detecting apical periodontitis in routine clinical practice. Radiolucent periapical lesions are often seen in radiographs. Data acquired from periapical radiographs are thought to be unreliable due to the compression of 3D anatomy into a 2D picture \[[63](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_063)\]. CBCT imaging has advanced to the point that it can accurately identify periapical lesions and evaluate their size, location, and shape in D. Accuracy ratings for periapical lesions were 0.96 for CBCT imaging, 0.73 for traditional IOPA, and 0.72 for digital IOPA, according to a comprehensive review of multiple studies \[[64](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_064)\]. According to a study, CBCT imaging was not as good at diagnosing apical periodontitis in teeth that had root canal treatment.

AI models could help doctors find periapical disease and periodontitis more accurately by being able to identify periapical radiolucency and alveolar bone resorption \[[65](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_065)\]. In their study, researchers came up with two models. The first model tried to find the alveolar bone loss \[[66](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_066)\], and the second model tried to measure how much bone loss there was \[[67](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_067)\]. Researchers created a model using a deep learning neural network to find molars and premolars that have periodontal disease and estimate how these teeth will do in the future \[[68](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_068)\]. The model was built around measuring alveolar bone loss, which is one of the most important ways to measure periodontal health. Researchers have come up with a number of different models to help describe periapical lesions and make diagnoses of periapical pathology \[[69](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_069), [70](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_070)\]. The study \[[71](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_071)\] says that a deep learning algorithm model can find periapical radiolucencies on panoramic X-rays with the same level of accuracy as 24 oral and maxillofacial doctors. Researchers performed a study \[[72](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_072)\] and found that the AI system had a 92.8% detection accuracy rate, correctly identifying 142 out of 153 periapical lesions. Artificial neural networks (ANNs) have been used to find cystic tumors \[[73](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_073)\]. Researchers \[[74](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_074)\] have also come up with a way to tell the difference between granulomas and periapical cysts by looking at CBCT pictures. This method is very important for treatment because it lets periapical granulomas heal with root canal therapy, so surgery is not needed.

### 7.8.2 Root fracture detection

VRFs are a serious problem that make up about 2% – 5% of crown/root fractures and sometimes require root resection or tooth extraction \[[75](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_075)\]. Using CBCT images and radiography has been shown to help find and diagnose VRFs, a condition that can be hard to accurately identify. Uncertainty about the diagnosis can make it more likely that the wrong surgery or tooth extraction will be done \[[76](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_076)\]. Due to the poor sensitivity and clinical manifestation of traditional radiography in finding VRFs, clinicians often have trouble making accurate diagnoses.

Researchers \[[77](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_077)\] did a study that suggests CNN could be a good way to find VRFs on panoramic X-rays. Researchers did a separate study using periapical radiographs and CBCT images to build a neural network that could find VRFs in teeth that were either in their normal state or had root canal therapy \[[78](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_078)\]. The identification of root fractures using CBCT images show superior performance in terms of sensitivity, specificity, and accuracy when compared to pictures acquired from 2D radiography. Researchers in another study used synthetic data to analyze second molar fractures that were produced using wavelet analysis \[[79](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_079)\]. These mathematical approaches help recover weak signals when noise is present, within the framework of ML. Using steerable wavelets to detect fractures in high-resolution CBCT photos was effective, even with the small sample size.

### 7.8.3 Determination of working length

Accurate measurement of working length is important for achieving favourable outcomes in root canal treatment. Radiography is a commonly employed technique for the evaluation of working length. Additional approaches that may be employed encompass electronic apex locators, digital tactile perception, the patient’s response to the insertion of a paper point or file point into the root canal system, and CBCT imaging \[[80](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_080), [81](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_081)\]. Radiography and electronic apex locators are frequently employed in clinical dentistry as standard techniques. The amount of picture resolution acquired through digital radiography is critically important for accurately comprehending the anatomical nature of the root canal system \[[82](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_082)\]. Nevertheless, there are various additional variables that influence the process of radiographic interpretations, perhaps resulting in the occurrence of misdiagnosis \[[83](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_083)\]. Therefore, the utilization of computer-based methodologies becomes imperative in order to continuously get accurate working lengths. According to a study conducted by researchers \[[84](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_084)\], the inclusion of ANNs as an adjunctive method for identifying the radiographic apical foramen has been shown to make the working length calculation more precise. Additionally, researchers \[[85](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_085)\] assessed the efficacy of ANNs for white light assessment in a human cadaver model that mimicked a clinical environment. After conducting a comparative analysis between an ANNs and direct measurement post-extraction, it was revealed that there were no apparent changes in the recorded root length measurements. In addition, it was demonstrated that periapical radiographs were significantly more effective than endodontists in identifying small anatomical constrictions, with the ANN outperforming the endodontist by a wide margin – achieving an accuracy rate of 96%. Consequently, an ANN might be considered a trustworthy method for estimating water level.

### 7.8.4 Morphology of root and root canal system

Maximizing the success of nonsurgical root canal therapy requires a comprehensive grasp of the different root and root canal system classifications. Imaging techniques such as CBCT and periapical radiography have been widely used. In comparison to radiography, CBCT imaging has exhibited enhanced precision in the evaluation of root and root canal geometries. However, it is not advisable in traditional clinical environments because to the potential risks associated with radiation exposure. According to research \[[86](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_086)\], panoramic radiographs can be analyzed using a deep learning algorithm to detect radix entomolaris, which is the separation of the distal roots of the mandibular first molars. In a study conducted by researchers \[[87](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_087)\], an automated method for segmenting teeth in 3D was demonstrated utilizing a CNN technique. In a thorough clinical investigation involving 433 segmentations of teeth using cone-beam computed tomography, the researchers observed that AI exhibited similar levels of accuracy, speed, and efficiency as a human operator.

### 7.8.5 Retreatment predictions

In their study, the researchers \[[88](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_088)\] developed a case-based reasoning paradigm to determine the outcomes of nonsurgical restoration of root canals, taking into account the risks and advantages that come with it. In essence, the system offered guidance on the decision to withdraw or remain in position. The system encompassed data related to statistical likelihood, performance metrics, and recall rates. One clear benefit of the system is how well it can predict the outcome of the retreatment process. The accuracy of the system could only be guaranteed by matching the data exactly. Case-based reasoning is a valuable approach in problem-solving, wherein answers to challenges are derived from past experiences with similar problems. Such method involves incorporating relevant knowledge and information obtained from related instances. The presence of variability and the multitude of diverse techniques may give rise to heterogeneity within the specific system under consideration \[[89](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_089)\]. The specificity, timeliness, and accuracy of future studies could be enhanced by increasing the sample size and taking into account the diversity of human methods.

### 7.8.6 Prediction of the viability of stem cells

Researchers did an academic study and used the neuro-fuzzy inference method to look at how well dental pulp stem cells could be used for different types of regenerative therapies. The method correctly predicted what would happen by checking the viability of stem cells after being exposed to bacterial lipopolysaccharides in a controlled clinical setting. The neuro-fuzzy inference system was used to predict the survival of cells that had been replicated in different ways and were likely to become contaminated with microbes \[[90](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_090)\]. In order to make the cells react with lipopolysaccharide and cause inflammation, the researchers did an experiment to see how long the cells would live after being exposed to it. Researchers tested how accurate their prediction was by using adaptive neuro-fuzzy interferences to guess how long these stem cells would survive after microbes invaded.

## 7.9 Other dental applications of AI

[Figure 7.2](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_fig_002) illustrates other dental applications of AI.

![Figure 7.2: The use of AI in other dental fields.](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-007_fig_002.png)

Figure 7.2: The use of AI in other dental fields.

### 7.9.1 In dental education

There have been big steps forward in the field of intelligent teaching systems since it began. Within the field of dentistry education, AI is often used to create simulated situations that are similar to clinical practice on patients while reducing the risks that come with training on real people. Consequently, there has been a notable enhancement in the feedback provided to students through the utilization of preclinical virtual patients. As a key part of creating high-quality learning settings, the interactive interface lets students evaluate their work and see how it stacks up against a perfect standard. Several research studies have demonstrated that the use of these systems leads to a more rapid development of competency-based skills in students compared to traditional simulator units \[[91](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_091)\].

### 7.9.2 For patient management

Virtual dental assistants that are powered by AI can do various tasks within a dental practice with enhanced precision and reduced errors, while also necessitating fewer human resources compared to their human counterparts. The utilization of such technology can facilitate different parts of healthcare practice, such as medical evaluation, planning of treatment, scheduling appointments, insurance cooperation, documentation administration, and other administrative responsibilities. Providing the dentist with a detailed account of the individual’s medical history and relevant behaviors, like smoking and drinking, is very important. People who are having dental emergencies may choose to use emergency teleassistance as an option \[[92](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_092)\], especially if the dentist is not present.

### 7.9.3 For diagnosis, treatment, and prognosis

AI can help with diagnosing and treating problems in the mouth, as well as finding and labeling changes in the tongue that might be signs of cancer or the start of cancer. The system can see even the tiniest changes at the level of individual pixels, which the human eye might not notice. According to recent research, there is a possibility that AI has the capability to accurately determine genetic predisposition of a significant portion of the population toward oral cancer. An ML system built on AI is a useful tool for figuring out the best way to treat dental problems. It is important to carefully look over a full treatment plan \[[93](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_093)\] in order to figure out how healthy and useful a tooth will be in future years.

### 7.9.4 In dental radiology

The field of dentistry is experiencing a gradual incorporation of AI into radiology, with an increased emphasis on diagnostic techniques such as 3D scans, digital RVGS/IOPA, and CBCT. In order to develop an AI system that could help in expedited treatment planning diagnosis and, a substantial amount of information must be obtained and analyzed \[[94](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_094)\].

### 7.9.5 In prosthetic dentistry

The RaPiD design assistant has been developed for use in the field of prosthodontics, specifically for the creation of aesthetic prostheses. The novel technology incorporates a range of parameters, such as anthropometric measurements, face symmetry, ethnic considerations, and patient preferences, to deliver the most appropriate cosmetic prosthesis to patients. The RaPiD system uses a logic-based description as a unifying framework to connect knowledge-based systems, databases, and CAD \[[95](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_095)\]. The usage of AI in laboratories has been increasingly prevalent due to the breakthroughs in neural networks. The technology lets tooth restorations be made on their own that meet the strictest standards for function, fit, and appearance. The implementation of such innovation is expected to yield positive outcomes in the field of dentistry, while also exerting a substantial impact on craniofacial and orofacial prostheses \[[96](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_096)\].

### 7.9.6 In forensic odontology

AI is a significant scientific breakthrough that has found extensive application within the domain of forensic medicine. The utilization of such method has demonstrated significant utility in the determination of both the biological age and gender of those who are in good health as well as those who are afflicted with illness. Furthermore, such technique is utilized for the examination of bite marks and the anticipation of mandibular morphology \[[97](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_097)\].

The field of dentistry stands to gain significant advantages from the implementation of various intriguing applications of AI. The dentistry chair, which is an important part of dental offices, has changed a lot over the years. Initially, it was characterized by physiologic, hydraulic pressure mechanisms that were manually regulated through pumps. However, it has now advanced to electric chairs that are fitted with a range of sensors. The newest advancement in dental technology is a voice-activated dental chair, which virtually removes the need for direct interaction with the dentist. Voice instructions are utilized for the execution of all actions. In the near future, dental chairs are anticipated to possess the capability to monitor several aspects of a patient’s well-being, including vital signs, anxiety levels, weight, and duration of the procedure. Additionally, these advanced chairs will provide comfort to patients and promptly alert the attending healthcare professionals in the event of any detected deviations or abnormalities. The phenomenon can be attributed to the fact that individuals possessing high cognitive abilities are diligently engaged in the growth and progress of AI \[[98](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_098)\].

One innovative approach AI is used is in “bioprinting,” a method for making live tissues and organs by adding cells one layer at a time. This technology might be able to bring back hard and soft tissues in the mouth that were lost due to poor habits or accidents \[[99](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_099)\].

### 7.9.7 Applications in orthognathic surgery

At this point, there is a clear lack of information about how AI can be used in the field of orthodontic surgery. Various researchers have performed numerous studies to use AI to find out how orthognathic therapy affected the beauty of the face and how old the people were \[[100](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_100)\]. Researchers demonstrated that orthognathic treatment led to enhanced aesthetic outcomes and improved patients’ facial look and attractiveness through the utilization of a computer method grounded in CNN principles. The results of this study are in line with most traditional clinical research. The research demonstrated a proof of concept that emphasized the efficacy of AI as a beneficial instrument for evaluating face alterations subsequent to orthognathic surgery.

Moreover, the usage of AI appears to be highly suitable within the framework of orthognathic surgical protocols. The potential ramifications of such phenomenon in the future are significant.

There are several distinct factors that provide support for this rationale:

1.  Right now, a lot of money is being spent by the industry to study and develop AI for digital orthodontics and robotic surgery. These areas are expected to see big improvements in the economy, especially in the field of braces, which is expected to bring in about 3.6 billion.
    
2.  The growing use of digital pictures and photos in the treatment of orthodontic problems is supported by the fact that big picture databases are becoming more common around the world. The incorporation of intraoral scanners has had a profound impact on the utilization of digital imaging within the domains of orthodontics and orthognathic surgery.
    
3.  In real life, practitioners have many needs, such as the need for reconstruction, automatic treatment planning, 3D morphometrics, and planning for a specific surgical setup.
    

It is crucial to initially ascertain the specific category of AI under consideration. A notable degree of AI in the area of medicine is distinguished by the presence of an autonomous machine that possesses the ability to assess various treatments by considering a foreseeable progression of pathological conditions. The implementation of this method is now not feasible within the context of surgical-orthodontic protocols as well as in the broader field of orthodontics.

A weak level of AI refers to an instance of AI that lacks discernibility and is limited to focusing on a singular task. Digital tools are only used in orthognathic surgery to look at digital models of tooth arches and X-ray data.

There are four different types of changes that technological progress has made to surgical and orthodontic treatments.

1.  The utilization of AI in conjunction with maxillofacial images has resulted in greater diagnostic precision.
    
2.  The implementation of 3D models has facilitated more effective treatment planning.
    
3.  The utilization of CAM and CAD methodologies has facilitated the manufacturing of personalized orthodontic and surgical instruments and apparatus.
    
4.  The implementation of image superimposing allows for more precise interval comparison of outcomes, leading to enhanced therapy follow-up.
    

## 7.10 Application in maxillofacial imagery

AI is found to be very essential in enhancing the efficiency of data collecting, data processing, and pre-analysis of maxillofacial images at various stages. The analysis of different levels of resolution provides the practitioner with a comprehensive and transparent understanding of the patient’s physical characteristics. The provided input is crucial in identifying the appropriate therapy option.

### 7.10.1 Acquisition

Utilization of AI within the software of intraoral scanners enables a more expeditious and effective process of data collecting. Additionally, using AI in 3D imaging raises the signal-to-noise ratio and creates top-notch pictures with less interference \[[101](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_101)\].

### 7.10.2 Interpretation

Utilization of ML methodologies facilitates the generation of 3D reconstructions, which are seeing a notable enhancement in terms of efficiency. The utilization of several diagnostic technologies, such as CBCT, digital photography, and intraoral scanners, enables overlaying of these tools to find out and quantify the measurements of the upper airways \[[102](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_102)\]. The study holds significant relevance in the management of syndromes related to obstructive sleep apnea \[[103](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_103)\].

Furthermore, the examination of a lateral cephalogram yields little benefits for healthcare professionals. Nevertheless, it is important within the framework of an orthodontic surgical regimen. There are several software applications available that integrate AI and ML technology with the purpose of automating cephalometric investigations \[[104](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_104)\]. This allows the practitioner to allocate cognitive resources more effectively. Furthermore, the advantages of AI extend far beyond its ability to do assessments in 2D. Indeed, such technology allows the clinician to attain an accurate and unbiased 3D understanding of a specific patient’s dental and facial attributes. Hence, it has the potential to serve as a highly impactful diagnostic instrument. Based on the study, it was found that at least 100– 200 craniometric points are needed to do biometric analyses of a 3D Cone Beam image of a person’s face \[[105](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_105)\]. The necessity for AI arises from its ability to efficiently analyze and interpret numerous parameters simultaneously \[[106](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_106)\].

Currently, there is a continuous endeavor to construct significant AI systems that aim to produce a literary synthesis through the utilization of biometric data. As an example, an AI system might identify a medical disease called “right hemifacial excess” when there is a right antero-posterior excess, a right vertical excess, and a left mandibular deviation all at the same time.

### 7.10.3 Treatment planning

Surgical-orthodontic planning involves a number of different challenges. This result is due to the positive interaction between the surgeon and the dentist, and is not just based on intuition. The necessity of engaging in thorough and deliberate discussion, as well as effective verbal communication, within the team, should be emphasized:

1.  The planning process should consider orthodontic stage that is characterized by its long-term duration, in contrast to comparatively shorter duration of the surgical phase.
    
2.  In addition to considering several interdependent variables, the design process should also incorporate factors like as bone architecture, occlusion, oral health, orofacial functions (including breathing and swallowing), as well as facial aesthetics. It is imperative to recognize that while numerous criteria can be objectively examined, there are certain aspects that necessitate a subjective evaluation.
    

These scenarios illustrate the imperative nature of employing 3D digital therapy planning software. Such objective could be accomplished by the utilization of a dynamic virtual setup, such as Clin Check, Insignia, Orthoanalyser, and other similar tools. The software in question has been improved by the utilization of ML techniques \[[107](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_107)\]. The hard and complicated process of making plaster stone models and taking lateral cephalograms has been replaced by algorithms.

The utilization of dynamic virtual setups serves as an effective means of facilitating conversation and collaboration between maxillofacial surgeons and orthodontists. The tool enables practitioners to visually represent diagrams depicting individual therapy objectives and the impact of each intervention on the overall outcome. Furthermore, it can be observed that the utilization of such tool proves to be highly efficacious in facilitating communication and strategic planning within the context of orthognathic surgery protocols that require a multidisciplinary approach. This is particularly relevant in cases involving specialists such as otolaryngologists, general dentists, and sleep specialists, who may not possess an extensive familiarity with the intricate technical aspects of orthodontic surgical protocols. Virtual setup software is a valuable resource for facilitating discussions and providing explanations to patients, so enhancing their comprehension and engagement with the orthognathic surgery protocol \[[108](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_108)\].

### 7.10.4 Custom orthodontic and surgical appliances

CAD/CAM technology is used to speed up the production of custom medical tools in the third step, which comes after the planning phase is over. Undoubtedly, the advent of 3D printing is a very important part of the digital revolution, enabling the development of novel and inconspicuous medical interventions such as clear aligner treatments and lingual treatment \[[109](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_109)\]. Furthermore, it aids in the advancement of devices that incorporate the biological mechanisms of alveolar decompression.

The integration of CAD/CAM in orthognathic surgery has emerged as a new development, particularly in the areas of planning for treatment and transfer procedures.

Cephalometric and clinical planning are often employed methods for achieving standard surgical planning. Virtual surgical planning is also seen as an alternative to traditional methods and is generally thought to be a very useful tool for helping doctors with challenging circumstances, particularly when there are uneven facial features \[[110](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_110)\]. In addition, the utilization of such technology enables the development of dematerialized medical data, hence promoting efficient remote communication between healthcare specialists.

In terms of transferability, specifically the clinical implementation of planning, CAD/CAM technology has made substantial advancements in the past decade. Surgical navigation, patient-specific titanium miniplates for osteosynthesis, CAD/CAM splints with additional external bone support, and CAD/CAM splints are all examples of modern transfer technologies \[[111](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_111)\].

### 7.10.5 Treatment follow-up

Utilization of visualization and quantification techniques to assess the effects of a certain therapy on various anatomical components offers significant benefits within the field of orthognathic surgery. This lets the doctor make sure that the best treatment plan was picked and measure the advantages and disadvantages of other treatment options. Furthermore, it provides patients with the chance to visually comprehend the outcomes of the treatment, so enhancing the process of obtaining informed consent. Furthermore, it serves to augment the efforts of maxillofacial surgeon and the orthodontist by providing an impartial assessment of the effects of various treatment stages, devoid of any inherent prejudice. Furthermore, the integration of AI would provide the healthcare professional with enhanced capabilities to effectively address forthcoming clinical complexities that share similar characteristics.

AI is highly suited for therapeutic follow-up due to its capability to overlay several digital pictures.

As an illustration, the utilization of software that incorporates various forms of ML within a single intraoral scanner enables the overlay and presentation of dental arch motions from two digital models belonging to the same patient, placed alongside each other \[[112](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_112), [113](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_113)\].

Various AI-based technologies provide the capability to integrate digital photos and 3D models, commonly referred to as “3D matching,” in order to generate a 3D representation of dental arch models utilizing photographs of the actual teeth. The model undergoes dynamic adjustments in response to the temporal progression of the treatment \[[114](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_114)\].

The utilization of X-ray imaging in orthognathic surgery is inherently advantageous \[[115](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_115)\]. AI has been used in 3D imaging to make geometric morphometric tools, like Procrustes methods, more automated so that practitioners do not have to do as much work by hand. The resulting images can be full Procrustes superimpositions \[[116](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_116)\] or they can only show up in a certain stable area \[[117](#9783111547589_012366.xhtml#b_9783111549729-007_ref_117)\]. In the setting of surgery to move the lower jaw forward, it is important to stress the placement of a certain area, called the “designated area,” which includes the sagittal ramus that is behind the osteotomy site.

## 7.11 Conclusion

The use of AI technologies has found extensive application within the field of dentistry. Based on empirical investigations pertaining to the integration of AI in dentistry, it has been observed that it has exhibited comparable levels of performance to those of dental professionals, while demonstrating superior accuracy and precision. AI should be seen as a tool that makes dentists’ jobs easier and makes them less busy. This frees them up to do more important things, like combining patient data and building better professional relationships. The dentistry industry experiences a quick pace of development and adoption of new technologies. AI is regarded as one of the most promising developments today because of its notable characteristics, including its ability to achieve high levels of accuracy and efficiency. However, it is important to note that these benefits can only be seen when the AI system is educated using unbiased data and the algorithm is appropriately trained. Dental professionals have the ability to recognize AI as an additional tool that can effectively lessen their burden while enhancing the precision and accuracy of several aspects within their practice. Some of these are diagnosing tooth problems, making decisions, planning treatments, predicting how treatments will work, and figuring out how long a disease will last.

# Chapter 8 Managing OPD with AI: implementation and utilization

#### Abstract

Recent years have seen better quality of life due to progress in many areas of technology. Diagnostics have become a lot easier because of recent advances in technology, especially in the healthcare field. Intelligent methods used in medicine have helped find and treat many illnesses. The purpose of this chapter is to look at how using artificial intelligence (AI) might improve the patient experience in the healthcare sector, with a focus on the outpatient department (OPD). People use cell phones, patient monitoring devices, and other tools for health monitoring. This is called “mobile health,” which is also sometimes written as “m-health.” A big success has often been used to describe the big steps forward in technology in the recent years. More and more work is being done in the m-health business to use AI and big data analytics to make healthcare delivery more efficient. Medical study today uses a lot of different and complicated data sources such as electronic health records, medical images, and textual data that is hard to understand. A lot of time, these data sources are hard to understand because they are diverse and not well-organized. A lot of unorganized and unstructured information exist because of the rise of mobile apps and improvements in healthcare systems.

Keywords:  Smart healthcare systems,  security,  data privacy,  internet of things,  blockchain,  wearable devices,  medical devices,  healthcare,  outpatient,  predictive analytics,  

## 8.1 Introduction

Clinicians and health services are currently experiencing significant challenges due to various factors including shifting demographics, administrative obligations, shortages in the workforce, rising morbidity rates, and evolving demands and expectations related to information technology \[[1](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_001), [2](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_002)\]. Recent years have seen remarkable progress in the application of artificial intelligence (AI) to healthcare \[[3](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_003)\]. It is expected that in the future, these techniques will replace certain duties currently performed by clinicians as well as healthcare administrators \[[4](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_004), [5](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_005)\]. However, there has been a notable increase in the prevalence of overstated assertions concerning the capacities of AI, occasionally suggesting that AI will completely substitute human therapists. The approaches do not seem to adequately acknowledge the present limitations of AI systems \[[6](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_006), [7](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_007)\]. By adopting a comprehensive viewpoint regarding the limitations and the potential of AI, it becomes possible to assess the specific sectors within the healthcare business that are expected to have substantial effects from the implementation of these technologies in the immediate future. At now, there is a limited amount of discourse within scientific literature and public policy on the integration of AI methodologies in the delivery of medical treatment.

The healthcare industry has been through a period of tremendous innovation in the past few years, primarily due to the fast advancements in technology, with the goal of improving the quality of patient care. There have been several chances to raise healthcare industry standards since the advent of cutting-edge technologies like blockchain and cloud computing.

The definition of patient experience can be elucidated by examining the various dimensions and components that contribute to the overall perception and satisfaction of those receiving healthcare services. The term “patient experience” refers to a person’s encounter with medical treatment and the way in which they are treated for an illness or injury. The growing emphasis on patient experience is a fundamental aspect of the shift toward patient-centered care. This transformation encompasses the entirety of the healthcare journey, commencing from the initial point of entry and concluding with the interaction with the billing and payment personnel. Hence, it is crucial for healthcare providers to carefully assess all points of contact during every stage of the care process \[[8](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_008)\].

The quality of care a patient receives is heavily dependent on five critical factors.

According to the survey performed among more than 230 healthcare providers, the following are the top five suggestions provided by patients to enhance the patient experience \[[9](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_009)\]:

*   A reduction of 50% in the duration of waiting periods.
    
*   The survey results indicate that 49% of participants possess advanced knowledge on the costs associated with therapy.
    
*   The study found that over half of the participants, namely 47%, reported that they did not have the feeling of being rushed throughout their appointments.
    
*   Approximately 44% of healthcare providers possess the necessary expertise and experience in effectively treating a particular ailment.
    
*   According to the data collected, a significant proportion of individuals, specifically 41%, reported that scheduling appointments was a relatively simple process.
    

The emphasis on enhancing patient experiences within healthcare organizations has prompted a shift in the perspective of chief information officers (CIOs) on their roles. CIOs are no longer solely focused on exploring novel methods for data processing and utilization; rather, they are now examining the manner in which systems affect patients and their treatment. In order to enhance positive patient experiences, it is imperative to consider aspects that extend beyond the realm of robust patient-provider interactions and accommodating facilities, despite the continued significance of these elements \[[10](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_010)\]. In order to cultivate a favorable patient experience, it is imperative to ensure the provision of efficient care that effectively aligns patients with appropriate treatment modalities. This necessitates healthcare practitioners to possess accurate and pertinent patient information.

## 8.2 What is AI?

AI is a relatively recent discipline within the area of engineering, but it has garnered significant scholarly attention since the 1950s \[[11](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_011)\]. According to the renowned pioneer of AI, John McCarthy, the field can be defined as “the discipline and application of scientific principles and engineering techniques to develop machines capable of exhibiting intelligent behaviour” \[[12](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_012)\]. Computers, according to Alan Turing’s definition \[[13](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_013)\], can’t be considered intelligent unless they act exactly like humans. Recent developments in AI have shown promise in mimicking various aspects of human intelligence such as reasoning, vision, making choices, representation of information, language, difficult task analyzing, and communication. Researchers even suggest that AI could soon surpass the Turing test \[[14](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_014)\]. Moreover, it is suggested by several experts that AI would assume a pivotal position in propelling the fourth industrial revolution \[[15](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_015)\]. In previous instances, the complete potential of AI has been obstructed by insufficient resources \[[16](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_016), [17](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_017)\]. When one takes into account the increasing amount of data, better processing power, and financial backing for AI projects, a more positive view of their potential applications becomes apparent \[[18](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_018), [19](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_019)\].

## 8.3 Incorporation of AI in healthcare delivery

The increasing prevalence of AI methodologies in the field of medical services and medical research is shedding light on the possible roles they can play \[[20](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_020)\]. Numerous studies demonstrate the effectiveness and promise of AI-enabled health solutions. Governments and technology companies are currently making substantial investments with the use of AI in healthcare operations, which aligns with the ongoing advancements in technology \[[21](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_021), [22](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_022)\]. In addition, the United States Food and Drug Administration plays an active role in enabling the market entry of medical devices equipped with AI \[[23](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_023)\]. This viewpoint makes it clear that healthcare management, surveillance of patients, clinical decision support, and healthcare therapeutics are four main areas where the incorporation of AI into healthcare delivery is expected to make a significant impact.

### 8.3.1 Healthcare administration

The delivery of healthcare services has become increasingly intricate due to the stress on healthcare infrastructure in numerous countries, caused by administrative hurdles and limitations in resources \[[24](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_024), [25](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_025)\]. Researchers have found that health facilities can handle more patients when they use IT tools. Among the various approaches to support healthcare administration, AI and the techniques for data mining have been recognized as particularly promising. These techniques have the capability to improve clinical care and reduce the administrative workload on clinicians \[[26](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_026)\]. AI has the ability to reduce physicians’ workload by performing repetitive and standardized tasks such as inputting patient data and automatically evaluating laboratory results and imaging outcomes. This would allow physicians to allocate more time to providing personalized care for patients \[[27](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_027)\]. Adding machine learning (ML) algorithms to an electronic health record (EHR) could make it easier for doctors and managers to find accurate and useful information about patients \[[28](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_028)\]. ML and a concept-based data retrieval system could make searches more accurate and speed up retrievals. At this point, though, there are still a number of problems that need to be solved. Concerns consist of having access to information as well as labels making sure that the ML model is easy to understand and that these models work well with current EHR systems \[[29](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_029)\]. Many different algorithmic approaches have been put forward as possible solutions to these problems. Transfer learning, knowledge incorporation, environmental analysis, and information extraction are some of these methods. Optimized ML algorithms can be used by healthcare workers to make clinic schedules and decide which patients are most important. This can result in improved service utilization and reduced waiting times \[[30](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_030)\]. In the pre-admission phase, AI methods might help healthcare centers guess how long patients will need to stay in the hospital. This capability has the potential to assist in the efficient and optimal allocation of restricted hospital resources. Voice recording and transcription in natural language processing systems has been shown to be useful for putting together EHR data and taking clinical notes, which gives doctors to spend more time caring for patients \[[31](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_031)\].

### 8.3.2 Clinical decision support system (CDSS)

Clinical decision support systems (CDSS) are computer programs which utilize medical information and data to provide assistance in the decision-making process for healthcare professionals \[[32](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_032)\]. CDSS has been widely recognized as a helpful instrument in the minimization of medical mistakes and the improvement of healthcare quality and effectiveness. The endeavor of integrating CDSS into regular clinical practice has gained momentum. AI methodologies have been utilized in the field of CDSS investigation since the inception of the 1970s, specifically within the domain of expert systems. Predicting when septic shock will occur, helping with the diagnosis and treatment of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease patients, and other specialized medical evaluations are all examples of current uses for ML algorithms \[[33](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_033)\]. It has been difficult to incorporate large databases of previous cases into clinical decision-making procedures, but new technologies have the potential to make treatment options more personalized for patients. As a demonstration, a research investigation successfully found the utilization of an AI framework that utilizes sequential decision-making to suggest alternative treatment approaches, deduce the health condition of patients even in the absence of measures, and enhance treatment and management strategies based on newly acquired information \[[34](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_034)\].

Artificial neural network (ANN), a sophisticated variant of ML, is currently undergoing trials in the field of medical diagnostics and demonstrates potential in surpassing clinicians’ abilities in predicting and diagnosing medical disorders \[[35](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_035)\]. A number of medical illnesses, such as the risk of cardiovascular disease, cancer, and diabetes, are expected to be better predicted by ANN than by traditional CDSS, which rely on standard software engineering concepts. Furthermore, ANN could be effectively employed for histopathological and radiological diagnoses. Previous research has indicated that AI programs had the capability to achieve performance levels comparable to those of radiologists and pathologists. Furthermore, the incorporation of DL techniques into EHR has the potential to discover deficiencies in treatment protocols and mitigate the occurrence of any errors in medical \[[36](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_036)\]. While ANN can handle some data noise, it’s yet worth noting that this noise could reduce the precision of the network’s predictions. Therefore, the incorporation of clinical expertise and practical knowledge alongside the selective ability of ANN carries considerable significance.

### 8.3.3 Patient monitoring

The widespread incorporation of EHR and increasing prevalence of smart mobile phones and fitness tracking gadgets (m-health) have resulted in unparalleled availability of digital data, hence opening new possibilities for utilizing AI methods in patient monitoring ([Figure 8.1](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_fig_001)). Researchers were able to collect abundant information about patients’ sleep habits, heart rates, blood pressure, and other metrics that were previously unavailable. Furthermore, apart from these developments, there have been notable increases in various other contexts. Medical equipment such as electrocardiographs, electroencephalographs, electromyographs, and Doppler ultrasounds can be better monitored and analyzed with the use of waveform pattern learning, as demonstrated in a documented case \[[37](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_037)\]. The utilization of AI software offers the chance to be employed within intensive care units (ICUs) to facilitate respiratory and cardiovascular surveillance by means of analyzing vital signs. Following a visit to hospital, healthcare providers have the option to utilize virtual assistants equipped with natural language processing capabilities to effectively communicate pertinent health and medication details to patients as well as facilitate the arrangement of subsequent appointments. Research has demonstrated that the utilization of virtual health assistants has been associated with a notable improvement in medication adherence and consistent follow-up \[[38](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_038)\]. [Figure 8.2](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_fig_002) shows a smartphone-based m-health model that uses AI and big data analytics.

![Figure 8.1: The diagram illustrates the m-health scenario. (Created with BioRender.com)](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-008_fig_001.png)
