### 8.3.4 Healthcare interventions

The advent of AI has facilitated the customization of health interventions that respond to the specific needs of a person or subgroups within populations. ML algorithms, when coupled with EHR, have the capability to evaluate biometrics and other medical information pertaining to patients. Subsequently, these algorithms can provide treatment recommendations that align with prevailing clinical norms. The utilization of AI programs that are connected to hospital servers and possess the ability to analyze recorded patient data and generate patient inquiries has the potential to mitigate extended waiting periods in overburdened emergency departments \[[39](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_039)\]. Medication administration could be aided by AI programs that use fuzzy logic, a kind of many-valued logic. One area where fuzzy controllers have found use is in the dosing of vasodilators for patients recovering from surgery.

Recent advancements in the fields of computer vision and robotics have shown great potential in enhancing the efficiency and cost-effectiveness of treatment and diagnosis services. Automated analysis of three-dimensional medical images using computer vision has been a widespread practice for a number of years \[[40](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_040)\]. Nevertheless, the present application of this technique involves the assessment of a patient’s state by examining facial characteristics \[[41](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_041)\]. The utilization of robots in healthcare is most promising in the realm of elderly care. A significant portion of the elderly population resides within the community, although they may encounter limited assistance from their family or carers, so exposing them to potential health hazards. Robotic assistants have been identified as a viable solution for addressing certain deficiencies in elderly care, such as providing reminders for routine tasks and assisting in navigating unfamiliar surroundings \[[42](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_042)\].

## 8.4 Application of artificial intelligence for patients

The conventional procedure observed in a routine of the patient involves several sequential steps including scheduling an appointment, attending the clinic or facility, checking in, validating insurance, recording vital signs, engaging in a consultation with a physician, maybe visiting the pharmacy, and utilizing services provided by the laboratory or radiology department.

The conventional method of routing is often accomplished via a queue management system. A significant number of healthcare providers utilize queue management solutions to handle the influx of patients. The overall experience of the patient could be made much better by combining data from queue management tools and electronic medical records (EMRs).

The information is made up of various components that came from various sources, like managing lines, pharmacy systems, EMR, labs, and more. Other components are wait time at the service delivery site, care time, travel time, and movement time inside the service delivery site. Patterns can be found by using AI algorithms to look at this dataset. This can lead to the finding of datasets that could make the patient experience much better \[[43](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_043)\].

Predictive modeling in the healthcare field is seen as a proactive measure aimed at the identification of patients who are at a heightened risk of developing diseases or experiencing poor consequences. One commonly encountered predictive model in the domain of AI involves estimating the number of patients, entering emergency departments, predicting the probability of re-admissions to these departments, forecasting disease or other relevant outcomes, and anticipating mortality rates among in-patients \[[44](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_044)\].

*   The concept of predictive wait-time involves providing the patients with advance notice of the expected duration they would need to wait before accessing a particular service. Patients can use this information to make better decisions regarding whenever to visit the healthcare facility.
    
*   Analysis of trends in the average length of patient treatments in different service settings (e.g., consulting, pharmacy, and laboratory) could lead to the development of predicted wait-time algorithms. The achievement of predictive timeframes can be facilitated by joining the overall wait-time and care-time at various facilities providing points and specific seasonal statistics.
    
*   Various datasets could be processed simultaneously, leading to enhanced accuracy in predicting wait times. These datasets encompass specific healthcare professionals such as doctors, nurses, and clerks working at the welcome desk as well as specific pharmacies supervised by pharmacists. The projected wait-time accuracy is improved by considering the overall service data. The dataset has the potential to integrate seasonal oscillations, such as differences in the day of the week, in conjunction with specific seasons, such as the period immediately following the reopening of schools, weekends and holidays, flu seasons, and the period before and after vacation seasons.
    

The utilization of diverse datasets enhances the significance of forecast wait-time. This phrase can be directly translated as “appointments.”

The utilization of patient flow prediction holds potential benefits for healthcare facilities, as it enables the optimization of hospital resources, enhances operational planning, and improves healthcare quality. Additionally, it facilitates the efficient planning and allocation of hospital resources, ultimately leading to increased satisfaction of the patient \[[45](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_045), [46](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_046)\].

### 8.4.1 AI in predicting patient flow

The utilization of AI has the potential in facilitating the prediction of patient flow patterns, hence mitigating the occurrence of avoidable visits to the emergency department. In emergency department activities, patient categorization and outcome prediction would be made easier with the ability to rapidly assess clinical data. Thus, AI has an effect on many things, including how much things cost, how resources are used, how efficiently time is used, and how well treatment is provided to patients. Upon arriving at the emergency department, AI has the capability to categorize patients based on their level of risk, thereby enabling the effective distribution of resources. This, in turn, leads to enhanced patient outcomes. The advent of AI holds great promise for expediting patient treatment plans in the emergency room by utilizing radiography to make accurate diagnoses. Upon patient discharge, the AI system has the capability to forecast potential outcomes, particularly bad occurrences, and offer an individualized postdischarge plan \[[47](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_047)\].

Applications of AI in the field of patient flow management within healthcare facilities involve the application of intelligent systems to forecast patient flow patterns and optimize the effective management of patients. The primary goals of AI in the context of patient flow management are as follows:

*   To predict the frequency of admissions to emergency rooms
    
*   To minimize unnecessary telephone inquiries and in-person visits to the healthcare facility
    
*   To customize a treatment plan to an individual’s specific needs and circumstances
    
*   To enhance the efficiency of patient flow management
    

A pilot research was conducted in Finland to assess how an intelligent patient flow management system (IFPM) could enhance hospital patient flow. A sophisticated medical diagnostics engine that utilized AI and ML algorithms enabled the web-based solution to function based on basic health issue information and symptom assessment. The main goal of integrated patient flow management (IPFM) initiative was to facilitate the reduction of unnecessary calls and visits to healthcare facilities by patients, while simultaneously enhancing treatment planning and optimizing patient flow management. The integrated performance and financial management exerted a substantial influence on the expenses associated with healthcare. The measure significantly lowered the average cost of care for a group of 17,943 patients, saving a total of EUR 552,000. A decrease in unit cost by EUR 31 results in cost savings for various healthcare services. The price drop means that it will cost EUR 34 less for a nurse to go to the emergency room, EUR10 less for three phone calls to an emergency room nurse, EUR12 less for almost three phone calls to a primary care nurse, and the same amount less for two letters or emails with a primary care nurse. Furthermore, this cost reduction is equivalent to approximately EUR 35 for a single consultation in an emergency department physician, EUR 23 for several telephone calls to an emergency department doctor, EUR 26 for numerous phone calls to a primary care doctor, as well as an equivalent cost savings for a single letter or electronic correspondence with a primary care doctor. According to the researchers, it was determined that the implementation of an integrated practise and financial model has the potential to decrease service expenses within a primary care environment \[[48](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_048)\].

### 8.4.2 Patient flow prediction models

The utilization of patient flow prediction can contribute to the optimization of hospital resources, enhancement of operational planning, improvement of healthcare quality, allocation and planning of hospital resources, and augmentation of patient happiness \[[44](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_044)\]. In order to anticipate patient flow, several forecasting methods have been used including exponential smoothing, linear regression, SARIMA, time series regression, and ANNs \[[49](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_049)\]. Numerous studies have sought to employ AI techniques in an attempt to predict the volume of patients presenting to either the emergency room or outpatient center.

Anticipating the variability in patient arrival number in advance might alleviate the burden experienced by healthcare personnel.

Researchers employed a genetic algorithm (GA)-based feature selection technique to evaluate its performance against conventional forecasting models such as the ARIMA (auto regressive integrated moving average), generalized linear model, and ANN. However, the models demonstrate satisfactory performance while making predictions using a 1-week dataset of previous daily patient arrivals. The predictive accuracy of these models diminishes when the prediction horizon extends beyond 1 week, hence falling short in meeting the requirements for long-term decision-making. In order to predict the daily and hourly admission of patients, researchers used a deep learning method called DNN \[[45](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_045)\].

### 8.4.3 AI for improving operational efficiency

The matter concerning the optimization of resources and the occurrence of patient crowding within the emergency department poses a substantial difficulty. The forecasting of resource requirements is a crucial aspect in mitigating the increasing healthcare expenditures through the optimization of healthcare resource utilization and availability.

Researchers found that the GA and ML methods were the best ways to make use of resources in emergency rooms. The experts came up with the idea of a meta-model that uses three powerful ML methods: the feed-forward neural network, the adaptive neuro-fuzzy inference system, and the recurrent neural network. To improve the performance of the meta-model, researchers used adaptive boosting (AdaBoost) ensemble techniques and bootstrap aggregating (bagging) approach. The utilization of the GA in the context of an emergency department yielded a noteworthy decrease in the mean duration of hospitalization by 15% \[[39](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_039)\].

The consideration of waiting times and appointment delays can play a role in the effective utilization of hospital facilities and the improvement of patient satisfaction. The researchers used a variety of ML techniques to forecast CT, ultrasound, magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), and radiography wait times as well as scheduled radiology facility delays. The radiology information system yielded a number of traits. To get the best possible fit with the training data, nine different ML algorithms were used, each with its own set of parameters that were optimized. The following algorithms were included in this set: neural network, support vector machine (SVM), random forest, elastic net, k-nearest neighbor, bagging, gradient boosting machine, classification and regression tree, and multivariate adaptive regression splines. The root mean square error was used as a parameter to evaluate the algorithms’ anticipated accuracy. Based on the performed investigation, it was found that out of the nine ML algorithms investigated, the approach exhibited superior performance in accurately and efficiently forecasting both waiting time as well as delay time \[[50](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_050)\].

Automated diagnostic decision support technologies possess the capacity to enhance the efficiency of diagnostic decision-making procedures in various healthcare environments, such as the emergency room, and inside healthcare facilities and wards \[[51](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_051)\]. The study revealed that AI models with a multitude of predictor factors demonstrated a high level of functionality in identifying patients who were susceptible to an unplanned transfer to the critical care unit \[[52](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_052)\]. AI systems possess the capability to forecast hospital readmissions within a designated timeframe, hence potentially mitigating costs within the healthcare system \[[53](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_053)\].

In comparison to other commonly employed prediction models, the DNN-I (integrated)-GA model demonstrated superior accuracy and minimal variation, indicating its notable robustness \[[50](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_050)\]. The feature selection technique that relies on GAs was enhanced by adding a fitness-based crossover mechanism. When put up against a standard GA and four other modern feature selection algorithms, the upgraded GA-based feature selection approach demonstrated significantly better performance. The algorithms encompassed consist of a selection algorithm that utilizes a framework of minimal redundancy and maximal relevance, a wrapper feature selection technique that uses simulated annealing, a feature ranking strategy that relies on variable importance, and a feature ranking algorithm that is based on correlation.

In terms of predictive accuracy, as measured by root mean square error and mean absolute percentage error metrics, the study found that the integrated DNN-I-GA framework performed better than both traditional statistical models and modern ML models (e.g., SVM-RBF, random forest, SVM-linear, and R-LASSO) \[[54](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_054)\].

## 8.5 Modern technologies and frameworks in smart healthcare systems

Conventional healthcare systems often encompass various components including medical practitioners, individuals seeking medical care, clinical monitoring facilities, diagnostic laboratories, healthcare facilities, EHR systems, and patient data systems. Additionally, many healthcare services pertaining to medical condition monitoring, diagnosis, and treatment, medical testing, decision-making, and hospital administration can be integrated \[[55](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_055)\]. The utilization of key-enabler technology is essential for controlling and administration of the healthcare services. Automated, intelligent, secure, and cost-effective computational ecosystems are within reach with the integration of numerous contemporary technologies including blockchain, AI, Internet of Things (IoT), edge computing, and cloud computing, among many others. This setting improves the effectiveness of the traditional healthcare system and is the foundation of the healthcare industry \[[56](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_056)\]. These technologies have been implemented by intelligent healthcare systems. [Figure 8.3](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_fig_003) illustrates the essential technologies that facilitate the functioning of the intelligent medical system, together with its inherent characteristics.

![Figure 8.3: Smart healthcare system architecture and emerging technology including essential characteristics.](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-008_fig_003.png)

Figure 8.3: Smart healthcare system architecture and emerging technology including essential characteristics.

### 8.5.1 Internet of things-based smart healthcare systems

In past, due to lack of interconnectivity among the many components of the medical care, the system exhibited an inability to consistently provide medical services to patients. Therefore, medical professionals faced difficulties in consistently monitoring the health of their patients and providing appropriate advice. Healthcare providers may now offer their patients top-notch treatment because of the IoT that allows doctors to remotely diagnose and treat patients. This is achieved by the utilization of convenient IoT devices, specifically sensors, that are strategically installed inside the healthcare setting \[[57](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_057)\]. The sensors can gather data from the environment, including the body of the patient, and transfer it to other different healthcare apps through the internet. The IoT facilitates the transmission of patients’ actual medical information, encompassing vital signs such as blood pressure, oxygen saturation, and caloric expenditure, through the utilization of wearable sensors affixed to the human body. By utilizing body-worn sensors, medical professionals can comprehensively monitor patients (through continuous monitoring) and provide recommendations based on real-time medical data. Instead of going to the hospital, patients can stay in the comfort of their respective homes and have a live video chat with their doctor. Ultimately, the available bed becomes accessible to additional patients requiring urgent intensive care \[[58](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_058)\].

In addition, the healthcare system possesses numerous physical resources including oxygen cylinders, wheelchairs, nebulizers, oxygen meters, personal protective equipment, heart monitors, and surgical instruments. It is imperative to effectively manage these assets to facilitate efficient tracking of their quantities and whereabouts during emergency scenarios \[[59](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_059), [60](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_060)\]. The utilization of IoT technology in medical care for asset management facilitates the expeditious tracking of those assets. In a study \[[61](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_061)\], the authors examine the global COVID-19 epidemic, which necessitates the rapid establishment of sophisticated medical infrastructure. In pursuit of this objective, the researchers put forth a healthcare monitoring architecture that leverages the IoT. The architecture plan incorporates cloud-based data centers, hybrid communication, and extensive medical screening capabilities. The intended recipients of this initiative encompass medical facilities, emergency response teams, and primary healthcare units. Additionally, researchers introduced a healthcare management system for the IoT that incorporates edge computing capabilities. Researchers aimed to enhance the efficiency of monitoring, diagnosing, and managing patient data \[[62](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_062)\]. The researchers put out a proposal for a system that utilizes database systems to monitor a range of medical chores and oversee diverse healthcare activities. However, because the system’s data is centralized, an attacker can break into it and make it less safe.

### 8.5.2 Artificial intelligence in smart healthcare systems

In order to provide accurate and effective patient care and administrative operations, the healthcare business needs intelligent and predictive services \[[63](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_063)\]. In addition, the healthcare industry generates and transmits a massive amount of data from a variety of sources as a result of the incorporation of IoT devices. Healthcare organizations must prioritize the incorporation of AI technology into their data management strategies to guarantee efficient administration and data optimization. The conventional healthcare system is based on laborious data analysis and decision-making processes; nevertheless, there are several advantages to using AI technology in healthcare. The system engages with medical information, namely training information, in order to offer significant insights pertaining to medical treatment, diagnosis, and clinical decision support. In the study conducted by the researchers \[[64](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_064)\], the investigation focused on the examination of osteoporosis, a medical condition characterized by the presence of porous bones. Common X-rays, scans, and MRI are usually sufficient to discover this issue. The main goal of the study was to improve the ultrasound-based osteoporosis patient classification using an AI-based feature selection method. This led to a 71% success rate in putting osteoporosis patients into groups based on their risk of breaking a bone. Researchers have also performed studies \[[65](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_065), [66](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_066)\] that include a remote patient tracking system that uses AI technology. The system was designed to help patients in ICUs. It can do many things like predicting when a patient will need to be readmitted, checking vital signs like heart rate, body temperature, and breathing rate, and noticing any changes from the patient’s normal treatment plan. Specifically, the model achieved a return accuracy rate of 67.53% and an abnormality accuracy rate of 67.40%, which is better than the previous models in terms of accuracy.

Researchers \[[67](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_067)\] delineated the essential characteristics of AI within the medical field. The researchers employed AI models to identify intelligence patterns in healthcare data and predict the likelihood of heart attacks and cancer, among other applications. The researchers presented a communication approach for the healthcare ecosystem that employs AI technology, emphasizing lightweight and secure features. The approach was evaluated by examining various performance metrics, including end-to-end latency and throughput, across multiple simulated scenarios. The researchers concluded that the decision tree technique demonstrates superior computing efficiency relative to competing techniques. In contrast, the SVM demonstrates superior accuracy relative to existing approaches. Researchers \[[68](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_068)\] performed a study on the incorporation of AI algorithms into the paradigm of sustainable development. The objective of the research was to examine prospective user demographics for an AI-driven question suggestion system across various contexts. The study’s primary purpose was to improve the AI-based question recommendation system, emphasizing its application in safety screening and healthcare integration. The system proposed various applications in healthcare. The difficulties of data dissemination while safeguarding privacy have recently heightened interest in federated learning as a viable option within the healthcare sector \[[69](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_069)\]. The researchers \[[70](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_070)\] have created a method for monitoring healthcare data that employs federated learning. The framework was designed to address the problem of local data collection. The methodology utilized by healthcare professionals also enables the effective identification of dermatological issues. The empirical findings indicate that the methodology efficiently safeguards patient data privacy while also reducing operational costs for medical providers. Similarly, the topic of heterogeneous healthcare data was investigated in research \[[71](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_071)\], underscoring the difficulties encountered by ML models in effectively training on this data. Consequently, the researchers chose to employ a decentralized methodology known as federated learning to create a distributed network infrastructure for brain tumor segmentation. The foundation is based on the use of the Message Queuing Telemetry Transport protocol. The study’s findings indicate that their approach enhances the accuracy and latency efficiency of healthcare systems in typical procedures.

### 8.5.3 Blockchain-enabled healthcare systems

In the healthcare sector, ensuring the security of medical data and effectively managing diverse supply chains – comprising pharmaceuticals, human organ donation, and medical devices or infrastructure – are paramount. Blockchain technology is widely acknowledged for its ability to improve security, reliability, privacy, and interoperability, resulting in substantial changes in the healthcare sector \[[72](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_072)\]. Blockchain technology, utilizing an immutable decentralized ledger, can securely store medical records and safeguard them against data integrity breaches. The ledger is protected by cryptographic methods, including asymmetric keys, hashing algorithms, and digital signatures, thereby ensuring data integrity and immutability \[[73](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_073)\]. Furthermore, the ledger functions in a decentralized mode, guaranteeing that every little modification in the data transaction is swiftly identified by all participants in the blockchain network. Thus, this improves the transparency of the entire system. The healthcare sector’s inherent susceptibility to harmful attacks makes the implementation of blockchain technology a feasible solution for securing patient data exchange, reducing potential disruptions, and enhancing the administration of medical resources. The researchers of the study \[[74](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_074)\] introduced an intelligent healthcare system that safeguards the privacy of individuals’ data within the healthcare framework through the utilization of blockchain technology. A number of smart contract implementations have been developed with the goals of streamlining access control, easing decision-making over an open network, and facilitating the verification of data transfers. Furthermore, differential privacy methods were employed to safeguard the privacy of users. The study’s results indicate that the proposed strategy displays enhanced performance in relation to reliability, security, and at the system level transparency when compared with alternative approaches \[[75](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_075)\].

Researchers in a study \[[76](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_076)\] came up with a fresh strategy to fix security holes in EHR systems by using blockchain technology. The major objective was to make these systems more reliable and secure. The study utilized private blockchain and swarm intelligence techniques to establish a reliable system for enhancing safe data sharing within the IoT network. Furthermore, a study was carried out by researchers \[[77](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_077)\] to examine how blockchain technology could be used to tackle the difficulties associated with diabetes, specifically in light of the COVID-19 epidemic. The incorporation of blockchain technology facilitated the allocation of healthcare resources including medical beds, oxygen supplies, telemedicine services, insulin pumps, and continuous surveillance systems for those with diabetes, who were disproportionately impacted by the pandemic. Researchers built a proof-of-concept model by securing and verifying patient data using blockchain-dependent technologies including smart contracts, interplanetary file systems, and the new economy movement. There are clear improvements in transaction speed, transaction fees, and energy consumption using the suggested methods.

### 8.5.4 Cloud computing technology for healthcare

Several sectors within the healthcare industry can benefit from cloud computing and its associated domains such as fog and edge computing, which provide an integrated and interactive system \[[78](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_078)\]. The smart healthcare ecosystem encompasses a wide range of interconnected components and encounters numerous significant obstacles such as elevated operational expenses, concerns regarding security and privacy, and reliance on centralized storage of data. Cloud computing improves healthcare operational efficiency by making services more accessible, decreasing response times, increasing personalization of therapy, and solving balance of workload issues. Moreover, fog and edge computing are highly adaptable cloud computing methodologies which effectively mitigate the delay experienced in the communication between wearable devices and medical professionals. The essential nature of this situation arises from the fact that an ICU patient needs prompt critical care, with no tolerance for delays in data transmission. In the event that the patient’s wearable device fails to expeditiously convey the necessary data to medical professionals, there exists a potential risk to the patient’s life. Fog and edge computing enable medical equipment to locally analyze their data, as opposed to transferring it to a global model. This localization reduces latency, leading to improved response times and enhanced performance in remote monitoring. Additionally, it enhances the patient engagement process.

Researchers have utilized on-demand computing services to implement a variety of methodologies \[[79](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_079)\]. The problem of prolonged reaction time in a safety-critical healthcare sector was initially introduced by researchers \[[80](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_080), [81](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_081)\]. In order to address the problem at hand, the researchers employed fog computing as a means of ensuring dependable data interchange and expeditious data processing. However, it has been noticed that fog devices produce a substantial volume of data that poses challenges in terms of management. In order to tackle such matter, the researchers utilized a methodology referred to as neighborhood optimization, which enhances the data transfer and enhances the capacity for failure of intelligent devices inside intelligent healthcare systems. Researchers \[[82](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_082)\] introduced a worldwide vaccination system that combines fog computing with blockchain technology. Within the context of the COVID-19 pandemic, the goal was to provide a quick and effective approach for determining vaccination decisions. As part of the worldwide vaccination drive, the deployed model was tested and evaluated using several performance indicators including response time, performance, and data phases. In a previous study, the author examined the issues related to task scheduling in healthcare services, specifically focusing on the issue of time delay. The study highlighted the direct impact of delayed data transmission on the quality-of-service delivery and the wellbeing of patients. In order to solve the problem of delayed communication, the investigators integrated cloud computing into an IoT-based healthcare system. Swarm intelligence techniques were employed to enhance the optimization of data flow between healthcare practitioners and patients. In terms of makespan, resource utilization, and waiting times, the suggested model outperforms the baseline models. In their study, researchers \[[83](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_083)\] examine the privacy and security concerns related to the conventional healthcare system, which relies on unreliable communication channels among various entities within the healthcare system. In addition, the researchers looked into blockchain technology’s weaknesses namely its processing complexity and capacity to manage massive data sets. To make medical data storage easier, cloud computing was used, and to boost security and privacy, blockchain technology was used. Furthermore, the researchers conducted a validation of their research by employing the AVISPA tool to assess its effectiveness in mitigating various security vulnerabilities such as replay assaults. Researchers \[[84](#9783111547589_014329.xhtml#b_9783111549729-008_ref_084)\] conducted a study on the vulnerability of medical data to potential attacks. Academics have suggested a secure communication framework for smart healthcare systems that is privacy-enhanced in order to address this specific challenge. Anyone seeking medical treatment, as well as healthcare providers, patients, and the communication channels they use, can be very anonymous when using cloud-based authentication.

## 8.6 Conclusion

The ever-improving capabilities of AI are leading to remarkable developments in the healthcare sector. AI is poised to further enhance the patient experience and improve healthcare competence through advancements such as predictive medical care, more accurate diagnoses, and more patient motivation to engage in self-care. The general patient experience is anticipated to be greatly affected by the use of predictive algorithms for calculating wait times. Additionally, accurately estimating care and service times will improve the efficiency of healthcare professionals, leading to better resource management and the ability to optimize patient loads within healthcare facilities. In the future, cloud computing, the IoT, AI, blockchain technology, and other big trends that drive innovation will change the way healthcare is done. There are, unfortunately, security risks that come with putting these new technologies into healthcare processes. For instance, someone could break into systems that keep an eye on patients, use healthcare data repositories for their own gain, or listen on conversations between patients and healthcare workers. Because of these security holes, healthcare processes might not work as well as they should. Because of this, it is important to look into the different security issues that come up with smart healthcare systems and the safety measures that have been put in place to deal with them.
