# Chapter 9 Elder patient care and monitoring through AI

#### Abstract

With the escalating need for medical practitioners, there is a growing imperative to deliver individualized healthcare services to senior individuals and those afflicted with chronic ailments, while also ensuring prompt interventions in emergency scenarios. Healthcare in the digital era is currently exploring the integration of robotics as a means of delivering personalized healthcare to patients requiring a. This chapter aims to identify the specific needs of elderly patients and propose potential solutions through the utilization of a personalized robot. The utilization of Internet of things (IoT) devices can expedite the prediction of emergency situations, enabling prompt recommendations for critical actions through the application of artificial intelligence (AI). IoT-enabled wearable devices are utilized for the acquisition of essential health data from individuals. The data undergoes processing and subsequent decision-making is executed by AI, while the designated robot performs the necessary actions.

Keywords:  Internet of things,  robotics,  healthcare,  artificial intelligence,  aging,  health monitoring,  older patient,  

## 9.1 Introduction

Telemedicine and mobile health (mHealth) have become prevalent in contemporary society. Telemedicine refers to the utilization of video conferencing technology to facilitate remote consultations between patients and healthcare professionals. Internet of things (IoT) sensors and devices, accessed via desktop or mobile apps, can be used to track patients’ vital signs and symptoms more effectively. During times of emergency, the healthcare provider has the option to either directly deliver the prescribed medication to the patient or transmit it to a nearby drugstore. Mobile health, commonly referred to as mHealth, primarily aims to offer a comprehensive range of mobile applications pertaining to healthcare. These applications encompass various aspects such as healthcare provider tools, wearable sensors, patient education, decision support, healthcare data management, electronic health records (EHRs), disease registries, and other related functionalities. According to the data from 2016, the number of mHealth applications available for the purpose of assisting healthcare providers and patients exceeded 250,000 \[[1](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_001)\]. The implementation of blockchain technology in the healthcare sector is now in its developing phase. The implementation of blockchain technology has the potential to address various challenges related to transparency, unity, cohesion, and security within the EHR network.

The medical sector is anticipated to witness the integration of robotics and artificial intelligence (AI) as prominent advancements in future technology. Robotic nurses have the capability to measure vital signs, while robots might be designed to provide aid in surgical procedures, administrative duties, and senior care. These advancements have the potential to facilitate the advancement of individualized healthcare interventions with enhanced precision \[[2](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_002)\].

## 9.2 Introduction to artificial intelligence

AI has been employed within the medical sector for analyzing intricate medical and healthcare data, enabling the creation of suitable solutions only through algorithmic processes, without human involvement. The acquired data undergoes processing to create the required output for the patients. ML algorithms are employed to facilitate acquisition of knowledge by a system through the analysis of provided data and scenarios. This enables the system to make accurate decisions when confronted with novel situations or to anticipate potential issues before they arise. The analysis focuses on the correlation between the treatment program and the patient’s outcome. Treatment plan preparation, diagnosis procedures, real-time patient monitoring, and the development of individualized medicines and pharmaceuticals are only some of the healthcare applications that have benefited from the advent of AI algorithms and programs \[[3](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_003)\]. The utilization of AI offers several advantages within the healthcare sector. These advantages include increased patient satisfaction, meeting demand for staff, optimizing staff resources, decreasing total costs, increasing healthcare facility utilization, and decreasing unplanned hospitalizations \[[4](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_004)\].

## 9.3 AI for care of older people

Both deep learning (DL) and machine learning (ML) rely on the identification of picture patterns and necessitate a substantial amount of data points, ranging from thousands to millions, in order to develop an AI learning model that may effectively and efficiently be employed in medical applications such as computer-aided diagnostics. The research undertaken on older persons utilizing ML and DL modelling has been limited in sample size and lack the necessary reproducibility required for broader clinical implementation across diverse clinical settings and larger populations. Although these techniques are highly accurate, it is important to note that their sensitivity and specificity in detecting specific medical disorders, such as idiopathic normal pressure hydrocephalus (iNPH) and Alzheimer’s disease (AD), can differ and possibly fall short of the level of specificity and sensitivity shown by a qualified clinician \[[5](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_005)\].

The AI-based algorithms demonstrate the maximum level of accuracy when considering the amount of factors involved. In one case, the algorithms attained a 95% accuracy rate in predicting short-term adverse effects after syncope in the elderly population. This precision was attained through the examination of 10 factors obtained from syncope cases’ histories, ECG data, and surrounding conditions. A total of 1,844 patients participated in the study \[[6](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_006)\]. When working with a smaller sample size \[*n* = 49, including 18 people with a documented history of traumatic brain injury (TBI) exposure and 31 people without\], including a greater number of variables (*n* = 27) contributes to better accuracy of algorithms (98%). This improved accuracy facilitates the establishment of a connection between the autonomous nervous system and TBI as well as its subsequent progression to AD \[[7](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_007)\].

The superior precision of algorithms was also validated in a study conducted in Finland that focused on predicting late-onset dementia. The study had 709 individuals who were initially cognitively normal and underwent a second test up to 10 years later. Out of these participants, 39 developed incident dementia. The approach underwent reevaluation within a larger sample size of 1,009 individuals, with 151 cases of incident dementia. For the core population, the developed model achieved an accuracy of 79%, while the extended population achieved 75%. Dementia risk factors include age, vascular risk factors, subjective memory problems, and the APOE gene \[[8](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_008)\]. The method holds potential utility in the identification of persons who exhibit a twofold susceptibility to the onset of dementia and stand to derive significant advantages from therapies aimed at preventing its manifestation.

The identification of frailty and assessment of fall risk in older individuals after an emergency department visit have also been facilitated through the application of a comparable approach in the development of an AI algorithm. Therefore, a prediction model was developed with an accuracy rate of 78% \[[9](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_009)\] after evaluating multiple ML algorithms for automated risk identification and referral action. The success rate (75%) of an AI algorithm used to diagnose frailty in people aged 75 and up is very similar to the result \[[10](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_010)\]. A subsequent investigation was conducted utilizing demographic and psychometric data obtained from a sample of 284 individuals. The research utilized a ML approach to predict the possibility of older persons developing persistent depressive symptoms over a 12-month period. The utilization of this methodology yielded a more advanced level of predictive efficacy with the implementation of ML in contrast to logistic regression (with a mean accuracy of 72% as opposed to 67%, *p* < 0.0001) \[[11](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_011)\]. When accelerometer data were used to model fall risk, the study found that DL neural networks were about 75% accurate. This accuracy was especially clear in multitask learning, which used data from wearable sensors \[[12](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_012)\] to correctly figure out the risk of falling. The next step is to look into possible ways to use these results as a preventative approach, which will then be put into practice in a normal clinical setting.

The study used a special residual extraction method that used an anomaly detection method to shrink the picture and make it again \[[13](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_013)\]. The special DL method works especially well with small datasets and is very good at figuring out if someone has dementia. For their study, the researchers used a clinical sample that included 69 people in their 70s. The control group had 23 people, and the AD and unexplained iNPH groups had 23 people each. All participants underwent 3T MRI scans of their brains. The T1-weighted, whole-brain 3D images that were created were then used to train a 3D DL algorithm that does everything by itself. Its main goal was to tell the difference between older people who had dementia and those who still had their cognitive skills. The researchers found that this method was 90% more accurate than the standard neuroradiological diagnosis of AD, but it was not more accurate than the standard method for finding iNPH.

Additionally, in a corresponding study \[[14](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_014)\], the DL-based method was utilized to examine DTI and MRI data for neurological issues. Thirty people with amyloid-β42-negative MCI, 35 people with amyloid-β42-positive MCI, and 25 healthy control subjects were part of the study. It was found that DTI data were better at predicting early-stage AD dementia than gray matter volume, with 77% accuracy compared to 68% for gray matter volume. ML analysis has also been used to look at the words of people with dementia, showing that it can tell the difference between people with AD and people with mixed dementia, which is a mix of vascular and AD dementia \[[15](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_015)\].

Artificial neural network (ANN) techniques, using large sample sizes and datasets, have shown that they are capable of identifying risk variables related to AD, in addition to their remarkable precision in detecting dementia. Therefore, it may be posited that advanced age, poorer educational attainment, and lower monthly family income are potential factors that may heighten an individual’s susceptibility to acquiring AD. Additionally, a familial history of dementia and a sedentary lifestyle have also been identified as contributing factors to the onset of this neurodegenerative disorder. It is interesting to note that among the well-established peripheral biomarkers for AD, urine AD7c-NTP, and not blood Aβ42, has proven clinical value in the early diagnosis of AD \[[16](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_016)\]. The ultimate developed ANN incorporates various data, encompassing epidemiological parameters, neuropsychological functions, and biomarkers. The comprehensive ANN demonstrates a notable level of diagnostic accuracy and effectiveness, suggesting its potential as a cost-effective screening and diagnostic tool for dementia in both general cases and specific subtypes of dementia.

ML has demonstrated superior accuracy compared to statistical methods in predicting various adverse health outcomes in older individuals. The risk of functional falls \[[17](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_017)\], the risk of delirium \[[18](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_018)\], and overall the risk of emergency hospitalization \[[19](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_019)\] in older adults are among these outcomes. So is the risk of hospital-acquired pneumonia \[[20](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_020)\] in high-risk populations like the mentally ill on neuroleptic medication. It is possible that the presented algorithms will provide a thorough and accurate assessment of the effects of aging and the possibility of physical problems and falls in the elderly. Further, patients with a higher risk of developing such illnesses may benefit from these algorithms’ predictive potential for timely management.

AI has made a positive impact in the field of pharmacology by facilitating the creation of innovative pharmacological interventions through the utilization of virtual screening and de novo drug design techniques employing DL \[[21](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_021)\]. It is anticipated that novel pharmacological advancements will mitigate the undesirable effects associated with existing medicines administered to the elderly population, while concurrently diminishing the financial burden and hazards associated with preclinical and clinical trials. In addition, DL models may be useful in managing cerebrovascular disease, loss of hearing, and dementia in middle-aged and older people by identifying potential preventive strategies such as support from family members, economic status, and relationship activity \[[22](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_022)\].

## 9.4 AI based monitoring for older adults

Wellness information, which includes physical activity and nutritional data, is being gathered by a wide range of commercial gadgets and systems, which include those incorporated into widely available smartphones. This data can offer a comprehensive overview of the whole lifestyle of older people. Recreational items typically do not undergo the same level of regulatory oversight as medical devices or applications designed for the purpose of collecting health data, such as heart rate or ECG reports. The potential of artificial intelligence-driven health monitoring solutions goes beyond the simple collection and tracking of various indicators. AI health monitoring possesses capabilities that closely resemble human intelligence including the ability to recognize, learn, evaluate, evolve, predict, and make decisions. As a result, it has the potential to revolutionize the care of older adults by supplementing existing care services, alleviating the responsibilities of family carers, and enhancing the overall quality of their treatment \[[23](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_023), [24](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_024)\].

ML is a field of AI that uses statistical methods to teach computers how to learn from data and get better at making predictions and accepting decisions on their own. One way to do this is to use data from the past. This allows the programs to improve over time through iterative learning processes. Consistently collecting and analyzing a large amount of data gathered through longitudinal observation is possible with optimization algorithms. With the help of predictive analytics, these algorithms can spot and group trends, access the amount of risk, and suggest ways to help with behavior or care. AI could help predict a number of health problems, such as high blood pressure and atrial fibrillation, for example, by being used in blood pressure or EKG monitoring. Some types of AI home health monitoring systems, like those that use computer vision statistics, can differentiate between movements like standing and walking. Through a process of repeated learning, the system can determine what moves and activities are normal for a certain older person in a specific setting. This includes looking at things like how easy it is for a person to get out of bed and how long it takes \[[25](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_025), [26](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_026), [27](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_027), [28](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_028), [29](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_029)\]. Sensing devices placed in different parts of a person’s home can keep track of their daily activities including how long they spend outside the home, how fast they walk, and where they are in their own home. Also, these monitors can help keep track of the exact kinds of activities that person does as well as the order in which they happen and how long they last \[[27](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_027)\]. These people are able to notice when someone is moving or acting in a way that is not normal, which could mean that their cognitive and functional skills are getting worse. For instance, AI monitoring systems that consistently analyze input data have the potential to identify subtle changes in an elderly individual’s ability to regain balance while standing up. This capability surpasses that of human observers, who may overlook such slow alterations. After analyzing the gathered data, the automated analytical system may make predictions about the deterioration of health. Subsequently, it can determine whether intervention is necessary by comparing the results to a predetermined risk threshold. In such cases, the system can send advance notifications or even provide recommendations for modifying behavior to the elderly individual or their carer \[[28](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_028)\]. By offering both descriptive real-time data and automated alerts, these systems have the ability to prevent acute deteriorations or major injuries, leading to a delay or avoidance of expensive institutional care \[[29](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_029), [30](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_030)\]. Multiple research have indicated that community-dwelling older individuals and their family carers consider automated monitoring systems at home as a more secure alternative \[[31](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_031), [32](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_032), [36](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_036)\]. The discovery suggests that these technologies have the potential to be effective substitutes or additions to in-person supervision, thus promoting self-sufficiency \[[33](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_033)\].

At the level of the healthcare system, the provision of clinically significant and practical AI monitoring data pertaining to the risk levels of older persons for adverse events has the potential to enhance medical decision-making processes and revolutionize the delivery of healthcare services. Healthcare practitioners can receive assistance from these tools in the process of triaging cases, thusensuring that patients who require timely access to suitable care are prioritized \[[34](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_034)\]. This, in turn, could make clinical workflow better and boost output. AI technologies could improve the effectiveness of human resources and make it easier for older people to stay safe in the places they choose. Then, this can help eliminate barriers to entry and simplify things for caregivers \[[35](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_035)\]. The incorporation of monitoring technology into the EHRs of elderly people has the potential to improve therapeutic interactions and offer significant assistance to patients who have difficulties articulating their symptoms or requirements, particularly those with cognitive or memory impairments. The application of continuous data can also enhance the understanding of the dynamic nature of disease development or functioning in older persons. The reason for this is that health evaluation does not exclusively depend on a static collection of indicators at a singular moment, but rather on the ongoing fluctuation of pertinent markers over a period of time, within discernible circumstances that are observed using tracking devices \[[36](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_036)\]. The possible benefits of the output produced by these systems of monitoring, which utilize synthesized data gathered by algorithms, include improving virtual assistance, preventive care, and personal consultation. As an illustration, in the context of clinic visits, physicians who are able to utilize synthesized outputs generated by these automated systems might transition from engaging in demoralizing paperwork and data-entry responsibilities to engaging in activities that are centered around building relationships, providing therapy, and focusing on the well-being of the patient \[[37](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_037)\]. Healthcare personnel possess the ability to review and evaluate previously collected data, thereby enabling them to formulate further questions that can augment their ability to diagnose and/or treat the patient’s medical condition.

## 9.5 Using AI to improve patient care and health outcomes

The elderly population expresses a strong preference for aging in their current residences and maintaining social connections. Hence, it is crucial to take into account the potential effects of AI health surveillance technologies on the psychological, emotional, and relational dimensions of elderly individuals, alongside their clinical health results \[[38](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_038)\]. The ethical importance of health technologies, including AI health monitoring devices, lies partially in their ability to enhance familial and professional care relations, therefore facilitating the ability of older adults to stay in their homes with appropriate help \[[39](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_039)\]. The principle of independent living asserts that individuals, including older adults with potential limitations or declining functional abilities, should possess equal entitlement to reside autonomously within the community and exercise self-determination, without encountering prejudiced paternalistic attitudes from others \[[40](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_040)\]. In some settings, liberty, or living on one’s own, is defined by the way it interacts with other people. This pertains to individuals’ capacity to engage, reside, and interact with their family and peers according to their own preferences, as opposed to a refusal of external support or a preference for solitary living.

The investigation of various viewpoints from diverse stakeholders, such as older adults, family carers, medical professionals, AI technicians, businesses, and ethicists, through multidisciplinary research, can establish a crucial feedback mechanism to guarantee the responsible and adaptable development and utilization of these technologies. Empirical exploratory study has the potential to offer useful insights on the implementation or reconciliation of the notions of relationships, independence, and autonomy within the context of AI health monitoring. The research is crucial in identifying ethical approaches that maximize the possible advantages offered by innovative AI health monitoring technologies. By informing practical solutions to potential ethical problems associated with the utilization of these technologies, such research contributes to the development of new approaches. Understanding the perspectives of older adults regarding the comparison between human and AI monitoring, identifying the crucial privacy and care relationships that contribute to improved health outcomes, and determining the information that professionals deem valuable for promoting care management and workflow can assist AI health developers in prioritizing privacy and clinical features that enhance carers’ capacity to deliver personalized monitoring and care management plans \[[41](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_041)\]. For example, an analysis of the preferences of elderly individuals regarding the specific health and activity data they wish to have collected or omitted, their preferred level and mode of interaction with technology and the accompanying data for self-management purposes, and their preferences concerning the disclosure or nondisclosure of information to family members, healthcare professionals, and external commercial entities can offer valuable insights into how older adults negotiate the decisions between privacy, security, user-friendliness, and autonomy in relation to artificial intelligence-driven home health monitoring. The possibility of generating innovative views on the usage of automated platforms by older adults to facilitate living in place can be enhanced by collaboration with these key variables in the procedure of creating health monitoring technology. This collaboration can shed light on the various considerations that older adults may contemplate, such as the balance between privacy and safety advantages, as well as the impact of these technologies on therapeutic interactions and interpersonal connections \[[42](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_042)\]. Given that a primary objective of advancing AI health monitoring technologies is to offer uninterrupted and practical data, it is relevant to investigate the perspectives of both family and professional carers on the types of information they deem valuable. It has the potential to enhance the provision of care at the bedside, facilitate long-term management of healthcare, and foster relationships, ultimately resulting in improved health outcomes.

## 9.6 IoT and IoT-based technologies

Sensors and gadgets have the capacity to acquire data, engage in data processing, and transmit the acquired data via the internet. In the field of healthcare, the collection of patient data by sensors or wearable devices facilitates the real-time transmission of such data to healthcare providers, enabling them to actively monitor the patient’s health \[[43](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_043)\]. IoT-connected gadgets offer assistance in the surveillance of elderly individuals and individuals with chronic ailments as well as in self-monitoring. Telemedicine and telehealth services frequently utilize this technology. The data obtained through IoT devices has the potential to be transmitted to robotic systems, which can then analyze the data and respond appropriately based on the patient’s state \[[44](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_044)\]. An illustration of an IoT gadget may be observed in the form of a wireless glucometer. This device is designed to consistently monitor the glucose levels of a patient. If the level of glucose drops, the patient is notified right away so they can administer insulin. Real-time continuous monitoring of health, improved reaction time, avoiding unnecessary hospitalization, and lower costs are only some of the benefits of the IoT \[[45](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_045)\].

Within the realm of elder healthcare, the IoT is a pioneering technological advancement that facilitates the interconnection and information interchange among several commonplace pieces of equipment and systems. These devices include sensors, actuators, machines, computers, and smartphones. By utilizing the IoT, highly distributed intelligent systems can be created to connect multiple devices and facilitate the exchange of information with individuals. This represents a viable solution for designing smart homes that integrate e-health and assisted living technology. The incorporation of IoT technologies and frameworks holds the potential to have a substantial impact on the transformation of the medical system for the senior demographic.

### 9.6.1 Activity recognition

Typically, an activity identification architecture consists of two subframeworks:

1.  A sensor architecture capable of detecting environmental occurrences and phenomena.
    
2.  An intelligent model capable of recognizing activities based on sensor data.
    

The acquisition of complete knowledge serves the objective of enhancing the environment by utilizing modern sensor devices that are interconnected through a communication system, resulting in the creation of a digital assistant. The aforementioned entity is capable of perceiving changes in the surrounding environment, subsequently inferring the underlying reasons behind these changes, and ultimately deciding on the most suitable course of action to optimize the well-being of the individuals utilizing such environment.

A detailed research review was undertaken by researchers \[[46](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_046)\] to examine improvements in sensor-based motion recognition systems. A survey was undertaken to investigate the fundamental attributes of video-based as well as sensor-based motion detection systems, in order to emphasize their respective merits and drawbacks, and to draw comparisons with data-driven and vision-driven movement detection systems. Direct detection refers to factors that are intrinsically linked to the individual, while indirect detection depends on the identification of ambient conditions. Both immediate and delayed frames are utilized in research and practical applications to capture and analyse human behavior. Direct detection involves the utilization of auditory capture, video recording devices, motion sensors, and wearable body sensors. The sensors transmit raw data or signals to the database. The process of data identification is commonly clarified and frequently integrated to determine behavioral patterns in the working phase of analysis, whereby health-related ambient-assisted living (AAL) systems can be categorized into six main classifications.

*   Physiological assessment: The comprehensive set of indicators includes heart rate, respiration rate, body temperature, blood pressure, blood glucose levels, gastrointestinal outputs, and urine outputs, among other factors.
    
*   Utilitarian assessment: The utilitarian evaluation includes several factors such as overall activity level measurements, movement patterns, walking identification, meal intake, and other relevant variables.
    
*   Well-being monitoring: The notion of good health monitoring encompasses the examination of data in order to detect and ascertain potential environmental dangers such as the leakage of gases. Security assistance encompasses various elements, one of which is the automatic turning on of restroom/hall lights. This particular function serves the purpose of mitigating the possibility of trips and falls.
    
*   Security monitoring: Security monitoring comprises a variety of approaches and technologies that are designed to identify and address potential human hazards. These measures include the use of theft alarm systems and the implementation of suitable actions in response to identified risks.
    
*   Social association: Social affiliations encompass the utilization of video-based communication systems to promote mediated relationships with family members, as well as virtual help in a multitude of tasks, among other functionalities.
    
*   Intellectual checking frameworks: Intellectual verification frameworks encompass subjective assistance technologies, such as automated updating and other cognitive aids like automated prescription systems and key locators. Furthermore, the incorporation of vocal activity guiding systems for machine operations and the implementation of sensor-assisted advancements are employed to aid individuals with impairments, including but not limited to visual, aural, and sensory deficits \[[47](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_047)\].
    

The utilization of distributed processing enables the incorporation of a wider range of technological advancements into daily life. Smart gadgets, sensors, and actuators have experienced a notable decrease in cost, an increase in durability, and enhanced ease of use for installation. Rapid advancements in embedded frameworks, particularly in the field of low-power processor engineering, such as ARM, have facilitated the integration of intelligence into everyday devices and equipment. Consequently, individuals now have the capability to be monitored and assisted within the confines of their own homes rather than being required to gather at medical facilities. This shift has resulted in the provision of cost-effective and secure care supervision \[[48](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_048)\]. Furthermore, PDAs with enhanced features can establish two-way communication with cloud infrastructure in order to delegate computationally intensive tasks, hence creating opportunities for the implementation of advanced functions. They have the potential to effectively engage older adults in their daily activities, capturing their attention and conveying specific tasks, requirements, or guidance. Additionally, they can serve as a means of communication for sharing important information with followers and family members in typical scenarios. Therefore, these technological breakthroughs possess the capacity to substantially decrease healthcare costs and mitigate the strain on healthcare professionals and individuals within familial groups \[[49](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_049)\].

The experts conducted a study to investigate the problems with AAL frameworks and how they affect clients. In their study, the researchers found that how people feel about changes in their personal space depends on their own needs and preferences for living. Improvements to AAL systems were categorized into three separate groups in the study: real space, intelligent space (AmI-S), and virtual space (VS). These groups can be combined to help people live independently. In addition, their model takes into account changes in clients’ lifestyles that occur when their mobility habits change. There is a growing desire to conduct more in-depth research on how AAL systems affect the lifestyles of their users \[[50](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_050)\]. In some situations, the system is also linked to families and emergency services so that people can receive instant help. It is generally known that sensors that output binary signals are easier to place and do not need to be aligned as strongly as sensors that output continuous signals.

### 9.6.2 Wearable systems

In their study, the experts suggested a methodical way to create a sensor system that can effectively monitor activities. The proposed system is designed to include a graphical user interface that can operate in three dimensions to facilitate virtual spatial projection. To achieve this, devices would be simulated in a virtual reality world. For clients, this approach provides simulation data that they can use to better understand patterns and trends in a specific subject. It was shown how a 3D testing device called Persim can be used in virtual reality by the researchers. The researchers showed that combining the necessary information for real-time activity identification applications is effective. The framework is built around a computer interface that records information about what a virtual figure does in a VS using Persim 3D’s user-friendly graphical user interface \[[51](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_051)\].

A system called Centinela \[[52](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_052)\] was under a thorough examination. The suggested framework includes measuring the subject’s vital signs and how fast their body is moving. This is done so that a very accurate activity identification system can be made. The framework put a lot of weight on four main actions: sitting down, walking, swimming, and climbing stairs. The suggested plan includes a cell phone and flexible identification equipment. The tests that were done using three different temporal window sizes and eight different classifiers show that the Centinela stage is pretty accurate (95% of the time). It works better than other methods that were tested under the same experimental conditions. The study’s results also show that checking vital signs is a very important part of distinguishing the difference between different types of physical exercise. These results support the claim that using both basic signals and movement data structures is a better way to recognize human actions than just using motion data alone. Researchers found that attaching the movement sensor to the chest of the elderly person prevented any disputes that could have arisen from its attachment to the wrist, making the positioning of the sensors a crucial part of the analysis. In addition to the recognition of actions, the system exhibited a continuous interface for monitoring vital signs, which integrates fundamental health condition tracking with the aim of detecting motions.

## 9.7 Incorporating robots for elder care

Robotic technology has been implemented across different sectors with the aim of reducing human labour and enhancing the efficacy of patient care, resulting in significant implications for the whole healthcare sector. In addition, robots contribute to enhancing safety and reducing costs in healthcare and medical procedures. Robots have the ability to conduct surgical procedures with heightened precision even in confined anatomical spaces within the human body \[[53](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_053), [54](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_054)\]. Moreover, it should be noted that a robotic system possesses the ability to function in the capacity of a nurse, hence enabling the monitoring of patients’ health and vital signs. In the event of an emergency, the robot promptly notifies relevant personnel \[[55](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_055)\]. In addition, robots have the capability to function as a transportation mechanism within the hospital, facilitating the movement of essential supplies and medications. In the event of the emergence of a novel infectious disease, such as the coronavirus, the utilization of a customized robotic system holds potential for the purpose of monitoring and analysing patients on an individual basis. Thus the approach serves to mitigate the risk of disease transmission to healthcare professionals, hence safeguarding their well-being. Despite the higher cost associated with robots, there exists a significant potential for their substitution of health workers and subsequent transformation of the healthcare industry within a relatively short timeframe.

### 9.7.1 Solutions for physical and functional challenges

To ensure the safety and comfort of the elderly in their own homes, robotic technology’s primary focus is on assisting the elderly with their everyday tasks. Robotic Hoover cleaners \[[56](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_056)\], automatic kettles and spoons \[[57](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_057)\], laundry sorters, lawnmowers, and food heating equipment are all examples of robotic gadgets. As of now, robotics research is focused on improving mobility aids including prosthetics, exoskeletons, and intelligent wheelchairs \[[58](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_058)\]. Furthermore, the field of robotics has made significant advancements in the development of devices designed to assist individuals in carrying or lifting heavy loads. In addition, researchers have made notable progress in the creation of robotic walkers, specifically tailored to accommodate individuals who rely on canes for mobility. One notable example is the personal mobility assistance that the researchers created; it works in a manner similar to a smart walker \[[59](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_059)\]. The goal of the 2004 study project that made the prototype robotic walker, Guido, was to help people who have trouble seeing. Guido is meant to make it easier for older people to find their way around by giving them useful information about their surroundings and things present in the surroundings.

Care-O-bot \[[60](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_060)\], Pearl \[[61](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_061)\], and AILISA \[[62](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_062)\] are just a few of the robots that are being studied and worked on right now. Pearl can check on people’s health, communicate with other devices, and remind patients of their upcoming appointments. The Care-O-bot can do many things such as keeping an eye on health, controlling home security, using the oven, getting things from the home, and talking to family and healthcare providers. The AILISA system is responsible for monitoring both the occurrence of falls and the physiological symptoms exhibited by the patient \[[63](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_063)\].

Robotic systems have been designed and implemented to perform routine tasks, including bathing, for elderly individuals in need of assistance. An example of a robot created to help the elderly with showering is the Harmony in Roll-lo showering machine. The user reclines in a chair that slides into a space, which encloses the user’s body but leaves their head and shoulders exposed \[[64](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_064)\]. Subsequently, the automated machine is programmed to dispense a combination of soap and water within the enclosure, with the intention of cleansing the individual. The robot is currently undergoing testing in Japan, and the feedback gathered thus far has been predominantly positive. According to the existing literature, these robotic systems are purported to safeguard the dignity and privacy of their users, while simultaneously offering enhanced ease of operation and reduced time requirements. The act of bathing poses challenges for the older population, thereby rendering robots a valuable resource in this regard. In addition, the shower can be substituted, thus reducing the potential for accidents related to slipping within the bathroom.

### 9.7.2 Solutions for cognitive challenges

The brain can be stimulated by cognitive training exercises made easier with the help of robotic systems created with integrated software. Cafero is a service robot that can help with tracking health, making calls, and mental workouts \[[65](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_065)\]. A new study \[[66](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_066)\] looked at how well this robot works in memory care settings. Robots have been designed to meet the needs of people with dementia by providing a variety of activities, including music therapy, games that stimulate the brain, and memory therapy \[[67](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_067)\]. It is very important to keep patients in a stimulating and interesting setting so that they do not act up and their quality of life is improved. The method aims to lower the stress of caregivers and improve health in general. Robotic systems like Ifbot \[[68](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_068)\] and IRobiQ \[[69](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_069)\] were made to provide cognitive games and many other services. According to expert recommendations, the utilization of robots equipped with integrated software specifically designed for individuals with dementia is advised. Therefore, these robotic devices have the potential to facilitate cognitive brain training activities and simultaneously assess and monitor their physical well-being.

## 9.8 Other applications of AI in healthcare management of elderly people

### 9.8.1 IoT for data acquisition

Patients are outfitted with wearable or biometric bracelets that are equipped with a range of IoT devices or sensors for the purpose of consistently monitoring their vital signs \[[70](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_070)\]. Sensors built into IoT devices can measure a wide variety of biometrics including but not limited to cardiac rate, blood pressure, body temperature, glucose levels, oxygen saturation in the blood, the heart electrical impulses, lung volume, brain activity, and movement \[[71](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_071)\]. [Figure 9.1](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_fig_001) depicts a visual representation of a selection of sensors employed for the purpose of gathering health-related data.

![Figure 9.1: Sensors implanted in patients to record their vital signs.](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-009_fig_001.png)

Figure 9.1: Sensors implanted in patients to record their vital signs.

Cognitive behavioral data relating to the patient, including factors such as social activity, stress levels, physical activity, dietary patterns, and other relevant variables, are also gathered. The data under surveillance and analysis by the robot encompass several software components including navigation, user localization, environmental monitoring, calendar management, voice processing, and database operations. The process of robot navigation relies on the utilization of a map that is maintained within a database. The purpose of user localization is to determine the precise location of the patient who requires assistance from a robot. The domestic setting is subject to surveillance by a range of sensors including those designed to measure humidity, light levels, water leakage, temperature, and other relevant factors. This feature has the capability to notify the user of urgent circumstances. The natural language user interface employed for patient interaction comprises vocal synthesis and speech recognition techniques. An integrated calendar system is utilized to effectively manage medication schedules and medical appointments. The primary function of the database is to serve as a repository for the comprehensive collection of patient information as well as a repository of knowledge pertaining to appropriate actions to be taken during emergency scenarios. The database also contains maps for localization \[[72](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_072)\]. [Figure 9.2](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_fig_002) shows the various parts of the healthcare management system described herein.

![Figure 9.2: Structural diagram of the healthcare management system’s components.](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-009_fig_002.png)

Figure 9.2: Structural diagram of the healthcare management system’s components.

**Healthcare Monitoring Algorithm:**

Inputs:

Sensor 1 = Heartbeat Sensor;

Sensor 2 = Body Temperature Sensor;

Sensor 3 = Blood Saturation Sensor;

Sensor 4 = Glucometer Sensor;

Sensor 5 = ECG Sensor;

Sensor 6 = Spirometer Sensor;

Sensor 7 = SpO2 Sensor;

Sensor 8 = Brain Wave Sensor;

Sensor 9 = Blood Pressure Sensor;

Sensor 10 = Electrocardiogram Sensor;

Sensor 11 = Motion Sensor;

Begin

Collect User Data and Store in Database;

Function Patient Health Monitor (Sensor 1, Sensor 2, Sensor 3, Sensor 4, Sensor 5, Sensor 6, Sensor 7, Sensor 8, Sensor 9, Sensor 10, Sensor 11);

Send Data to Healthcare Provider;

Call Ambulance if Anything Found to be Unusual;

**End**

**Home Environment Control Algorithm:**

Inputs:

Sensor 1 = Humidity Sensor;

Sensor 2 = Temperature Sensor;

Sensor 3 = Light Sensor;

Sensor 4 = Water Leak Sensor;

Begin

Collect Environmental Data and Store in Database;

Function Home Environment Control (Sensor 1, Sensor 2, Sensor 3, Sensor 4);

Send Data to User’s Mobile Phone;

Alert User if Anything Found to be Unusual;

**End**

**Medication Management Algorithm:**

Inputs:

Prescription;

Calendar;

Begin

Get Tablet Name and Dosage Allotment from Prescription;

Allot Concern Tablets According to Its Dosage Allotment in the Calendar;

Function Medication\_Management (Prescription, Calender);

Alert User When It Is Time to Take the Medicine;

**End** \[[73](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_073)\]

### 9.8.2 Medication and healthcare assistance robots

Health monitoring is a prevalent characteristic found in healthcare robots. Robotic systems provide the capability to assess essential physiological indicators using a diverse array of sensory mechanisms, hence enabling the transmission of collected data to medical professionals for consultation. The robots can also be equipped with telecommunication capabilities, enabling remote consultations with medical professionals via video conferencing. Robots possess the capacity to integrate several extant technologies within their systems, hence facilitating a straightforward and user-friendly interface. Several robots have been developed for health monitoring purposes including Hector, Pearl \[[74](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_074)\], and Cafero \[[75](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_075)\]. These robots offer a range of services in addition to health monitoring. However, robots like Hopis \[[76](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_076)\] were developed with the distinct purpose of tracking physiological parameters like the core temperature of the body, heart rate, and glucose levels. However, production of the Hopis robot has been discontinued due to its low sales and limited utilization. The primary issue with this particular robot is in its physical manifestation since it bears a resemblance to a soft and cuddly creature despite its intended purpose of performing activities of a severe nature. The developers intentionally crafted the robot’s appearance with the intention of enhancing its approachability and likability. However, it appears that older individuals exhibit a reluctance to engage in interaction or communication with a toy-like entity. Another robot, known as Ifbot \[[77](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_077)\], which possesses capabilities for health monitoring, entertainment, and cognitive training, is presently not being manufactured due to its toy-like esthetic design. The evaluation of the Ifbot robot revealed that senior individuals exhibited a gradual affinity toward the robot throughout the beginning stages, but their interest decreased after a period of 5 weeks \[[78](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_078)\]. To date, diminutive robotic devices have only elicited favorable and beneficial responses from the older population. The development of these robots, namely Teddy \[[79](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_079)\], Wakamaru \[[68](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_068)\], and Robobear \[[80](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_080)\] is characterized by inquiry and exploration. According to a study conducted by \[[81](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_081)\], smaller robots have the capability to engage in health monitoring, provide medicine reminders, and serve as companions. In the event of any atypical incidents among the older population, Wakamaru promptly transmits an emergency alert to the attending physician, thereby facilitating the implementation of appropriate measures. Robobear possesses telepresence functionalities and is equipped with interactive gaming features to mitigate the experience of monotony.

### 9.8.3 Robotics for psychological issues

In forthcoming times, it is imperative for ongoing efforts in healthcare robotics to encompass not only the healthcare and functional requirements of the senior population but also to extend their scope to include the distribution of information and provision of support to the wider community. This is particularly significant since robotics is poised to assume a pivotal role in the advancement of the intelligent digital landscape. Robotic systems equipped with telecommunication capabilities possess the ability to establish communication not only with medical professionals and healthcare providers but also with others inside one’s own network, including family members and friends. The development of robots such as social companions is aimed at mitigating the experience of loneliness for patients or users. Robotic companions, as shown by Paro, AIBO, and similar models, are primarily designed to offer companionship to the elderly population, serving as interactive pets that engage with patients and provide caregiving support. Previous research conducted on Paro, a seal robot \[[82](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_082)\], a dog robot \[[83](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_083)\], and AIBO, exclusively yielded favorable outcomes. Engagement with these robotic companions was found to effectively alleviate symptoms of depression and mitigate feelings of loneliness among older individuals \[[84](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_084), [85](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_085), [91](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_091)\]. Since 2005, consumers have had easy access to the Paro robot, with sales of over a thousand units expected. The majority (around 65%) of these purchases were made by individual consumers \[[86](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_086)\]. Researchers \[[87](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_087)\] performed a study in order to ascertain the underlying motivations behind the acquisition of these particular types of robots. According to the findings of the poll, individuals who are unable to keep pets resort to purchasing robots as an alternative. Furthermore, unlike dogs, these robots do not experience illness. Instead, they fulfill the role of caretakers for patients and possess endearing qualities that make them amenable to being embraced. Additionally, certain participants expressed a preference for robotic companions as opposed to live pets due to concerns regarding potential emotional distress upon the death of a pet as well as uncertainties surrounding the future care of the pet in the event of their own demise. Furthermore, a study was conducted with elderly individuals who owned AIBO robots to investigate the extent to which these patients developed emotional connections with their robotic pets and the potential psychological advantages associated with ownership \[[88](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_088), [89](#9783111547589_015791.xhtml#b_9783111549729-009_ref_089)\]. The survey findings indicate that the ease of engagement with these robots makes them very ideal for domestic applications. Additionally, it was observed that minimal learning is required to effectively operate these robots.

## 9.9 Conclusion

The issue of falls and injuries becomes increasingly significant as individuals advance in age, particularly in instances when they reside independently. As individuals progress in age, they often experience a decline in memory function and may necessitate professional assistance. The senior population is susceptible to a variety of health conditions and cognitive impairments. Consequently, it is crucial to closely monitor their physical and mental well-being and effectively address their medical concerns. This support is essential in enabling them to maintain an independent lifestyle. Despite the elderly possessing the capacity to effectively navigate the difficulties associated with the aging process, they may experience feelings of depression, isolation, and loneliness. The current surge in demand for medical professionals may be attributed mostly to the prevalence of contagious infections such as the coronavirus. Consequently, there is a growing imperative to deliver tailored healthcare services to aged individuals and those with chronic ailments, while also implementing appropriate measures in emergency scenarios. IoT wearable devices are employed for the purpose of acquiring essential health data from individuals. The data undergoes processing and subsequent decision-making is executed by artificial intelligence. Subsequently, the designated robot is responsible for executing the necessary action. Robotic technology is now being advanced with the objective of mitigating the need for human intervention, enabling multifunctionality, facilitating motivational interactions, including a substantial volume of instructional data, and promptly notifying emergency medical services in the event of an urgent situation.
