# Chapter 10 AI and pregnancy: an unexpected alliance

#### Abstract

In the field of mother care, artificial intelligence (AI) is making big strides. AI is changing the health of mothers by making it easier to find problems early and give each woman individualized pregnancy care. Predictive algorithms use big datasets to see what risks will come up in the future, like preeclampsia (PE) or gestational diabetes, so that they can be dealt with quickly. Virtual health aids are available 24 h a day, 7 days a week to help with questions and give information about pregnancy. Also, imaging tools that use AI to improve the accuracy of ultrasounds give doctors a better picture of how a fetus is growing. Using AI in pregnancy care will help make sure that all pregnant women get the care and attention they need, which will be good for both the mother and the baby’s health. AI has the potential to make birth safer and more comfortable for women all over the world. This chapter explains how AI can be used in the area of pregnancy. When doctors supervise pregnant women with AI, it may improve their health. This could lead to better pregnancy results and an overall better experience. This method might make long-lasting problems and their effects less likely to happen. AI has been used to improve mother’s health in a number of ways throughout the whole pregnancy process, from conception to care after giving birth. This includes the stages of protection, pregnancy, giving birth, and recovery after giving birth.

Keywords:  Pregnancy,  artificial intelligence,  machine learning,  decision support systems,  privacy,  security,  

## 10.1 Introduction

In the last few decades, people from all walks of life and businesses have become much more interested in and excited about artificial intelligence (AI). People who work in the health sciences often use the word “prediction” to refer to figuring out how likely something is to happen, even if it hasn’t happened yet. AI has been the subject of a lot of studies that have helped doctors find diseases. Some of these conditions are heart disease \[[1](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_001), [2](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_002)\], diabetes \[[2](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_002), [3](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_003)\], cancers \[[4](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_004), [5](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_005)\], and problems that can happen during pregnancy \[[6](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_006)\]. There are a lot of different types of prediction studies. At first, studies on risk factors were looked at. The goal of these studies was to find biomarkers, age, and disease stage as well as other factors that can be used to predict results. The next study looks at prediction models and tries to build, test, or improve these models. These models are meant to make sure that every patient gets the best care possible. In the end, using prediction models has a big effect on studies that try to find out how well these models work at helping doctors treat patients when they’re not around \[[6](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_006)\].

A lot of AI techniques and methods have been used to predict or lessen problems like PE and maternal diabetes during pregnancy. Over the past decade, the prevailing AI methodology deployed in this field has been machine learning (ML) \[[7](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_007)\]. The majority of the studies that have been evaluated operate under the assumption of a conventional medical diagnostic model, wherein the predictive indicators primarily rely on the biological aspects of patients such as their medical history, anatomical characteristics, and physiological functioning. Nevertheless, the biopsychosocial diagnosis paradigm \[[8](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_008)\] is becoming the prevailing method, emphasizing the integration of biological and behavioral information at psychological and social levels \[[9](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_009)\]. Research that primarily examines biological aspects often relies on data derived from a singular source, commonly medical records or medical instruments, to construct predictive models. In the context of the biopsychosocial diagnosis model, it is anticipated that the predictive models will proficiently integrate diverse data obtained from several sources. Data will be collected from several sources, such as health data, medical equipment, mobile devices, remote sensing gadgets, patient inputs, and medical professionals, among other potential sources. Additionally, it is possible to infer from the biopsychosocial diagnostic paradigm that the individual’s psychological data is an essential source of information. AI algorithms that can recognize emotional states can be improved with the help of this data. The wide range of data sources presents opportunities for a comprehensive assessment of healthcare, yet also raises inquiries regarding data collection and utilization. These inquiries encompass the types and frequency of data collection, the selection of data sources, and the integration of diverse data sets to enhance health predictions.

The discipline of AI encompasses the examination of “agents” which acquire knowledge from their surroundings and execute activity in reaction to these surroundings \[[10](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_010)\]. They are sometimes designated as “intelligent agents.” The term “AI” is used in a wide sense to describe the techniques that computer systems use to perform jobs that typically need human participation. Among these responsibilities are the following: making decisions, automatically recognizing people from images, and translating words into many languages.

In healthcare, the early deployment of AI mostly focused on the application of rule-based decision-making methodologies. Rule-based AI systems demonstrate significant compatibility with the clinical environment due to their ability to accurately replicate physicians’ decision-making processes. One of the initial algorithms for decision-making based on rules was MYCIN. The development of MYCIN, which occurred in 1974, aimed to forecast the most suitable treatment for various bacterial infections \[[11](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_011)\]. The system was initially conceptualized as an “expert system” with the purpose of providing guidance to physicians in making suitable decisions. This was achieved through the implementation of a set of conditional statements. In 1994, professionals pioneered the application of rule-based systems in women’s health by developing a rule-based predictor for preterm birth risk. The aforementioned system, which operated on a set of rules, made predictions regarding the likelihood of preterm birth in women. These predictions were based on diagnostic codes recorded during the course of the pregnancy. The system incorporated the most advanced AI techniques available at the time of its development \[[12](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_012)\]. Enhanced performance may solely be attained by means of program restructuring. ML is a subset of AI that enables computers to autonomously acquire new knowledge without human interaction or programming. The ANN is a commonly employed ML methodology that seeks to replicate the information processing methods present in biological brain systems.

## 10.2 AI and pregnancy

A small number of studies have examined the application of AI for the health and wellbeing of pregnant women, especially in utilizing psychological data to develop predictive models. But there is a lot of evidence that suggests pregnant women’s mental health affects the likelihood that they will have unfavorable pregnancy symptoms or complications like PE. Numerous risk factors have been associated with PE. The factors contributing to the development of PE encompass mental stress and high blood pressure, exposure to distressing life events such as demise or trauma, sleep disorders, disturbances in the body’s natural sleep-wake cycle, excessive body weight, presence of hyperinsulinemia, occurrence of recent miscarriages, and personal or familial predisposition to PE. Multiple studies have shown a correlation between mental health disorders during pregnancy and a number of negative outcomes such as an increased risk of PE, cesarean section delivery, low birth weight babies, and premature birth. Consequently, it is advisable for pregnant women experiencing anxiety and depression to have their symptoms assessed while evaluating the risk of problems. In an investigation carried out by researchers \[[13](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_013)\], it was shown that the precise identification of women who are prone to experience pregnant-related anxiety and depression would facilitate the selection of individuals who could benefit from preventative and supportive interventions. The phenomenon of pregnancy holds considerable importance and complexity within a woman’s life, potentially impacting her psychological state. There are many ways in which pregnant women’s decision-making could be improved by using AI and ML ([Figure 10.1](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_fig_001)).

![Figure 10.1: The possible uses for AI and ML techniques in processing diverse data modalities within clinical data, aiming to generate robust and trustworthy findings about maternal and fetal outcomes. (Created with BioRender.com)](graphic/converted/b_9783111549729-010_fig_001.png)

Figure 10.1: The possible uses for AI and ML techniques in processing diverse data modalities within clinical data, aiming to generate robust and trustworthy findings about maternal and fetal outcomes. (Created with BioRender.com)

On the one hand, there are notable physiological transformations that require an adaptive procedure, potentially posing difficulties in terms of both physical and psychological acclimation. However, the pursuit of both the mother’s personal wellbeing and the wellbeing of her fetus is a fundamental aspect that might impact her psychological health. This is because it may necessitate acquiring knowledge and adopting lifestyle modifications such as nutrition, physical activity, sleep, and work adjustments, among various other factors. Additionally, it involves receiving appropriate medical care and monitoring. Stress during pregnancy can adversely influence a woman’s mental health, particularly if she is concerned about acquiring a serious illness that may impair her unborn child. Issues with mental health may increase the chance of getting sick during pregnancy and may also be a sign of other problems.

Studies have shown that mental stress during pregnancy, trouble sleeping, and stressful events in life are all linked to a greater likelihood of high blood pressure disorders such as PE \[[14](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_014)\]. Researchers have found a strong link between anxiety and depression during pregnancy and a higher risk of giving birth early or using a way other than vaginal delivery \[[15](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_015), [16](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_016)\]. Researchers looked at 623 pregnant women as a whole group and found a strong link between anxiety and depression in the early stages of pregnancy and a higher risk of getting PE. Similarly, a study \[[17](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_017)\] that looked at 1,371 pregnant women found a link between hypertensive illnesses and the mental health of the mothers.

The study included 150 pregnant women who had been diagnosed with PE and similar number of healthy pregnant women. The main purpose was to test the idea that women who are very anxious are more likely to get PE. The study found that 26.7% of the women who were labeled with PE also showed signs of anxiety. In contrast, only 10.7% of the women in the control group, who did not have PE, said they were feeling anxious. Findings show that pregnant women who are stressed may have a higher chance of getting PE \[[18](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_018)\].

Concerns about possible health problems in the early stages of pregnancy were looked at by researchers \[[19](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_019)\] to see how they affected the overall health of pregnant people. The research, while not directly addressing depression or anxiety, examines the impact that stress has on the physical condition of pregnant women. Researchers found that feeling worried in the first three months of pregnancy can directly affect the likelihood of bad pregnancy results, such as giving birth early. Experts noted that programs assisting pregnant women with health difficulties during pregnancy benefit both mothers and their infants.

## 10.3 Utilization of AI to understand maternal and fetal health

One of the primary obstacles faced by methodologies aiming to comprehend the fetal and maternal implications of pharmaceuticals used during pregnancy is the scarcity of randomized controlled trial data available in human subjects. The focus is commonly placed on populations that are considered vulnerable such as those who are having opioid substitution therapy \[[20](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_020)\], those who are getting HIV preventative therapy \[[21](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_021)\], and women who are at risk of experiencing premature delivery \[[22](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_022)\]. Regarding the overall pregnant demographic, there exists a shortage of studies pertaining to the utilization of medications during the gestational period. Researchers are required to employ data collected during regular clinical practice, resulting in studies that frequently adopt a retrospective design to examine outcomes. Furthermore, it should be noted that although data can be obtained from animal models, there exist significant limitations in the application of this information to the human environment \[[23](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_023)\]. Hence, the presence of data necessitates their utilization for addressing significant clinical inquiries. AI approaches can exhibit significant efficacy in this particular context.

### 10.3.1 Assisted reproductive technologies

The domain of assisted reproductive technology (ART) is effectively represented in research, primarily because pharmacological intervention is a crucial component in various commonly employed ART procedures. AI methods have been used in a number of medical settings to inform and advise doctors, figure out the best ways to treat patients, predict the chances of a successful pregnancy, and measure the risk of miscarriage.

The process of managing infertility and ART treatment is often financially costly and requires multiple attempts. As a result, a lot of work is going into coming up with ways to improve treatment outcomes and predict pregnancy. Neural networks were used to predict how likely it was that couples who were getting in vitro fertilization (IVF) treatments would become pregnant \[[24](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_024)\]. The growth of neural networks is impacted by four things: the mother’s age, the number of eggs she retrieves, the number of embryos she implants, and the cryopreservation state of the embryos (fresh or frozen). The neural networks were able to make predictions that were 60% accurate at most. This outcome might have happened because the input data wasn’t complete; important predictive factors might not have been included in the dataset. To make it easier to see, process, and get knowledge in the field of IVF, researchers made a case-based reasoning system that uses context-based relevance assessment \[[25](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_025)\]. It helps doctors make better decisions so that more treatments work. This is called the clinical decision support system (CDSS). The CDSS looks at 39 different factors to find the best amount of hormonal stimulation for treatment and the best time to start ovulation.

The researchers used a retrospective method and created a Bayesian network (BN) model to predict pregnancy and implantation outcomes (no implantation, single implantation, or twin implantation) \[[26](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_026)\]. The model takes into account and sorts the following variables: the mother’s age, previous IVF attempts, cycles using intracytoplasmic sperm injection, embryo evaluation, insemination method used, maternal and uterine receptivity, embryo viability, and the occurrence of pregnancy. Even though there weren’t enough observations of maternal receptivity and embryonic viability, the researchers used the expectation-maximization method to estimate parameters. The model correctly predicted the rate of pregnancy, with an AUC value of 0.72. It is important to note that the model needs to be tested in the future. Furthermore, it is plausible that the model in question may be considered rudimentary in capturing the intricacies associated with implantation, as it fails to incorporate a comprehensive set of pertinent features. Researchers in their study presented a novel approach for predicting IVF pregnancy outcomes. Using a number of different supervised ML classifiers, the researchers use automated classification approaches in conjunction with a hill climbing feature selection strategy \[[27](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_027)\]. Multiple classifiers have detected numerous noteworthy characteristics within the set of 25 variables, including antral follicle counts, maternal age, signs of infertility component, and the number of mature eggs.

Using a ranking-based algorithm, researchers developed a new way to assess the success of IVF treatments \[[28](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_028)\]. The dataset comprised 64 traits, with 52 pertaining to female qualities and 12 to masculine aspects. The treatment modality is not regarded as a predictive factor for assessing efficacy; yet, this approach entails preliminary assessments of luteinizing hormone, follicle-stimulating hormone, and estradiol levels. The methodologies employed by the researchers are specifically designed to assess and evaluate prospective clients of IVF in order to ascertain the likelihood of success based on clinical characteristics. Researchers introduced a web-based approach designed to aid doctors in delivering individualized treatment for couples experiencing subfertility, with the aim of enhancing the outcomes of ART procedures \[[29](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_029)\]. The system uses an IVF database and an ANN model to link several databases inside the health information system. There are four ways to set up the ANN model’s parameters: cycle factors, female evaluation, couple’s evaluation, and male evaluation. There are 24 parameters in the cycle characteristics set and 12 parameters in the couple’s evaluation set. In addition, the evaluation set for women has 30 factors, while the evaluation set for men only has 12. The use of albumin, gonadotrophin, and metformin, as well as the co-administration of cortisolone and the stimulation routine, are all important factors. The system that is being proposed is meant to help at different steps of care during IVF treatment. It additionally seeks to improve the model’s accuracy by using the information gathered from each record.

Researchers came up with a way to test human leukocyte antigen haplotypes in couples who have had multiple miscarriages and couples who have had good pregnancies in the past \[[30](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_030)\]. A support vector machine (SVM) classifier with a linear kernel was used in the study to figure out which pregnancies were normal and which ones had a history of miscarriages. One possible outcome of the study was that an exact assessment of the risk of recurring miscarriage linked to a certain combination of gametes could lead to better selection of gamete donors, which would increase the chances of a successful pregnancy.

Experts studying ARTs are still trying to figure out how to make accurate predictions about how a baby will turn out. People have noticed that many of the elements needed for the most accurate predictions are missing from current models. Pharmaceutical treatments are sometimes used as a predictor of pregnancy viability in some models, but they are rarely seen as an important factor. Doctors may use one of several methods to start ovulation when addressing infertility. This treatment plan includes gonadotrophins, GnRH mimics, estrogen antagonists, and medicines that make insulin work better \[[31](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_031)\]. Researchers in the area of ART may use ML to compare the efficacy of various ovulation induction methods in future studies.

### 10.3.2 Developmental toxicology

An approach was developed by researchers to obtain a set of criteria for the identification of developmental toxicologic hazards from a panel of specialists. These rules were intended to be utilized in an AI application \[[32](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_032)\]. The researchers obtained a collection of rules as a result of the interviewing procedure. The next step was for experts to go through the whole set of rules and assess if each one was a confidence rule or an important rule. It is worth noting that although an expert may possess confidence in the validity of a rule, its significance may be very insignificant. Experts held differing opinions on the matter, indicating a notable level of disagreement. The classification of rules necessitated more clarification from the experts, suggesting that the limits of classification were indistinct. The study conducted by the researchers aimed to identify six characteristics that exert influence on an expert’s assessment of whether a substance or agent presents a developmental toxicologic threat. The variables encompassed consist of the following: (1) empirical observations derived from investigations conducted on human subjects, (2) empirical observations derived from investigations conducted on animal subjects, (3) identification of the presence of an active compound within the human body, (4) assessment of the degree of resemblance in physical structure to a known substance that has been established as toxic to human development, (5) assessment of how closely the mechanism of action resembles that of a recognized toxic chemical, and (6) classifying the substance as an established carcinogen or an immediate cytotoxic agent. Issues such as dosage, administration technique, absorption, timing of administration, mechanism of action, and interactions among drugs and diseases must be addressed in a comprehensive set of rules that combine pharmacologic principles.

## 10.4 AI-based chronic disease management in pregnancy

In the United States, the incidence of chronic diseases affecting mothers has been on the rise. The frequency of women with one or more chronic diseases who presented to hospitals for delivery increased from 66.9 to 91.8 per 1,000 delivery hospitalizations during the years 2005–2006 and 2013–2014, as reported. A number of chronic disorders, including those involving the lungs, blood pressure, substance abuse, and a history of diabetes, have seen significant increases in their frequency over the years \[[33](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_033)\]. A single scholarly article was discovered relevant to the correlation between chronic illness and the outcome of pregnancy. There is a wide range of clinical manifestations, diagnostic markers, and prognostic outcomes associated with systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE), a severe immune disorder whose cause is unknown. A substantial risk of severe obstetric consequences, including fetal loss due to an abrupt abortion or intrauterine death, is associated with pregnancy in women with SLE \[[34](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_034)\]. The goal of creating a CDSS was to help forecast how a pregnancy with SLE will turn out for women who are expecting a child. The main outcomes that were of concern were either live births or spontaneous abortions \[[35](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_035)\]. Two artificial neural networks (ANNs) were trained with characteristics extracted from a binary logistic regression (LR) model. There were two ANN models: one used radial basis functions (RBFs), and the other used multilayer perceptrons (MLPs). Laboratory test parameters (hematuria, proteinuria, antibody levels) and pharmacological therapies (azathioprine, hydroxychloroquine, aspirin) evaluated before and throughout pregnancy were the features that were determined to be important. Pregnancies affected by SLE were found to have the highest level of accuracy (91%), specifically in terms of spontaneous abortion or live birth, after a 10-fold cross-validation procedure. The network with the most predictive power was the MLP network.

Chronic disease has been observed to exert an impact on various aspects of reproductive health including fertility, pregnancy outcomes, maternal wellbeing, and fetal wellbeing. Preconception care should ideally include screening for possible poor outcomes and providing evidence-based treatment and individualized management plans for chronic diseases during pregnancy in order to reduce the likelihood of such events.

## 10.5 Pregnancy-induced disease

Pregnancy induces substantial physiological transformations within the body, and regrettably, several maternal problems may arise during this period. The search results yielded academic studies that primarily examined gestational hypertension problems \[[36](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_036)\], gestational diabetes mellitus (GDM) \[[37](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_037)\], and bacteriuria \[[38](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_038)\]. The primary objectives of these studies are to forecast and categorize diseases during the initial stages of pregnancy, enhance screening procedures, and offer therapeutic guidance to aid in illness management. Some studies have found a prevalence of 9.2% for GDM in the United States \[[39](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_039)\]. Cromosomia (excessive birth weight) \[[40](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_040)\], impaired glucose intolerance \[[41](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_041), [42](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_042)\], an increased risk for long-term neuropsychiatric problems \[[43](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_043)\], and early onset heart diseases \[[44](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_044)\] are some of the negative consequences linked to maternal GDM. There are defining characteristics of GDM that set it apart from T1D and T2D. Firstly, individuals with GDM exhibit insufficient endogenous insulin secretion. Secondly, the metabolic state of GDM patients undergoes continuous fluctuations, necessitating frequent modifications in treatment. In addition, it should be mentioned that the brief illness could potentially have significant ramifications for patient education and their comprehension of insulin self-management \[[45](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_045)\].

Worldwide, approximately 10% of pregnant women experience hypertension during their pregnancies \[[46](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_046)\]. The term “gestational hypertension” or “pregnancy-induced hypertension” (PIH) is used when there is an increase in blood pressure during pregnancy but no protein in the urine. In certain instances, PIH has the potential to progress into PE, a vascular condition associated with pregnancy. PE affects approximately 2–8% of pregnancies and is the second leading cause of maternal mortality worldwide, behind obstetrical hemorrhage \[[47](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_047), [48](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_048)\]. The mother’s and the baby’s health are negatively impacted by HELLP syndrome, which is characterized by hemolysis, increased liver enzymes, and low platelets, according to multiple studies \[[49](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_049)\]. It is expected that between 0.2% and 0.8% of pregnant women will experience HELLP syndrome \[[50](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_050)\]. This consequence is linked to heightened maternal risks including pulmonary edema, heart failure, hemorrhage, kidney damage, liver bleeding, rupture or failure, and mortality. Similar to PE, the one effective approach for addressing the problem is to terminate the process of gestation. Due to the lack of knowledge about the causes of HELLP syndrome and PE and the lack of established methods for preventing them, gestational hypertension disorders are notoriously difficult to manage. A woman is significantly more likely to experience HELLP (14–24%) and PE (22–28%) in her subsequent pregnancies if she has a history of HELLP during her pregnancy, which is likely due to interrelated pathogenetic pathways \[[51](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_051), [52](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_052)\].

### 10.5.1 Disease screening

Researchers have developed a screening tool for GDM utilizing ANNs to effectively model the associations between demographic parameters and the likelihood of developing GDM \[[53](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_053)\]. A 70% success rate in accurately predicting actual positive diagnoses was demonstrated by the ANN model, in contrast to a 56% success rate demonstrated by the LR model, when a comparison of their respective performances was made. Scientists have proposed the radial basis function network (RBFNetwork), an ANN method, as a means of identifying pregnant women who may be suffering from GDM. The study achieved a 79% accuracy rate and an F-measure of 0.79 \[[54](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_054)\]. The RBFNetwork is designed to help hospital administration and is rooted in the utilization of ANN within the field of AI. The researchers urged administrators, healthcare providers, and pregnant women to work together to reduce the occurrence of GDM.

In situations where conventional wisdom indicates that an oral glucose tolerance test is not appropriate, there have been attempts to improve the methods for the early diagnosis of GDM. The researchers proposed a hybrid method to enhance GDM initial detection \[[55](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_055)\]. The proposed methodology includes elements borrowed from multicriteria analysis, BNs, and expert systems. Using Expert SINTA software, the collected data was organized methodically within the database of a rule-based expert system. Using illness codes and a large database maintained by a health insurance firm that provides coverage in more than one state, the prediction engine analyzes medical records of pregnant individuals.

Researchers undertook the development of a model for categorizing women into three groups: those with normal pregnancies, those with hypertensive pregnancies, and those with preeclamptic pregnancies. This model utilized maternal heart rate variability measures in conjunction with ANNs \[[56](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_056)\]. The resulting model demonstrated an approximate sensitivity of 80% for pulmonary embolism (PE), with a comparatively larger percentage for the normal and hypertensive categories. The study employed a multiomics methodology to establish reliable biomarkers for PE, by analyzing and comparing blood proteomes of individuals with PE and a control group. The researchers employed a genetic algorithm (specifically, the R genalg package) to develop a biomarker panel that is both sensitive and specific while minimizing the number of protein analytes required. Several indicators have been identified, which indicate the intricate nature of the condition known as PE.

In order to forecast the likelihood of HELLP syndrome in high-risk pregnancies, the researchers proposed a theoretical framework that makes use of ANNs and fuzzy logic \[[57](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_057)\]. The method combines the power of fuzzy logic with the learning potential of ANNs. The design of this model is specifically tailored for mobile cloud computing, hence mitigating the need for resource-intensive diffuse inference. The model under consideration has similar performance to other ML techniques. When comparing the performance of MLP and the suggested neuro-fuzzy technique, it can be observed that MLP outperforms the neuro-fuzzy method. However, it is worth noting that the proposed model exhibits superior computing performance.

The occurrence of asymptomatic bacteriuria (ASB) during pregnancy is well-documented, with prevalence rates ranging from 2% to 10%. This condition has been found to be linked to adverse outcomes such as premature birth, decreased birth weight, as well as perinatal mortality \[[58](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_058)\]. In order to reduce the diagnostic workload related to UTIs while keeping the detection rate constant, the researchers in the study tested three ML algorithms, namely RF neural networks and extreme gradient boosting. Given the identification of the classification sensitivity to this particular category, the model took the presence of pregnant patients into account. The pregnant patients, children, and other individuals in the dataset underwent distinct training protocols. The pregnant subgroup in the study demonstrated enhanced performance. Nevertheless, several studies suggest that the likelihood of developing pyelonephritis in untreated ASB is minimal \[[59](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_059)\], or find no evidence of heightened negative outcomes for both mothers and neonates in females with untreated ASB \[[60](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_060)\]. The implications of such results raise concerns regarding efficacy and appropriateness of employing screening practices in the context of prenatal care.

### 10.5.2 Disease management with clinical decision support systems

Due to the increased need for monitoring in patients diagnosed with GDM, there is a natural compatibility for the incorporation of CDSS to effectively manage this condition. The applications primarily concentrate on enhancing the monitoring of individuals within their homes, devising therapeutic plans, providing nutritional suggestions, and promoting patient participation. Researchers developed a computerized decision support system to help people with gestational diabetes evaluate their home monitoring data and plan their therapy \[[45](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_045)\]. The DIABNET system employs both qualitative and quantitative reasoning techniques to successfully address the issues presented by data uncertainty. The major objective in developing the system was to assist doctors during patient visits by providing quantitative improvements to insulin administration and qualitative recommendations for dietary adjustments. In 92% of instances, the DIABNET system accurately identifies the need for therapeutic intervention, according to the study. Furthermore, a considerable majority of the recommended changes, ranging from 74% to 86%, were assessed by competent experts and found to be valid \[[61](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_061)\].

Researchers devised Sinedie, a web-based telemedicine system that allows them to efficiently monitor GDM remotely. Users can immediately enter their glucose levels from their monitor as well as other monitoring-related data on the website \[[62](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_062)\]. Treatment planning is made easier with the advent of automated systems that supply and send dietary ideas to patients and relay insulin therapy recommendations to doctors. The C4.5 decision tree method and EM clustering approach are used to classify the glycemic level. The Sinedie system was designed primarily to be highly sensitive when making insulin therapy recommendations. This sensitivity is intended to reduce the occurrence of false negatives while simultaneously ensuring that false positives are reduced. Consequently, the system efficiently decreased the number of personal visits needed for each patient by about 89% and the time needed for physician evaluation by around 27%.

The researchers successfully demonstrated the practicality of the features and layout of a CDSS known as MobiGuide. This CDSS aims to provide patient-centered treatment and increase patient participation \[[63](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_063)\]. At a Spanish healthcare facility, the functional prototype was tested on GDM patients with and without hypertension. The study discovered that healthcare practitioners and patients with GDM were happier because GDM patients were more likely to adhere to computer-interpretable guidelines (CIG). The financial outcomes could be improved with the help of a mobile health management system by reducing the number of hospitalizations and complications and the number of clinic visits needed for disease monitoring. A pilot study was carried out by researchers to evaluate the practicality and acceptability of a mobile CDSS that offers personalized CIG for the management of GDM \[[64](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_064)\]. The integration of a mobile CDSS resulted in a higher level of adherence to blood glucose monitoring compared to standard treatment. The researchers observed a significant level of acceptability based on the outcomes of a questionnaire regarding the utilization of the system.

## 10.6 Labor analgesia

The utilization of neuraxial labor analgesia is prevalent in the United States, with a reported range of 37–80% \[[65](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_065)\], for the purpose of alleviating pain throughout the process of birthing. An example of a neuraxial labor analgesia technique is epidural anesthesia. The researchers developed an independent image classification algorithm to recognize the bone/interspinous region in lumbar spine transverse plane ultrasound images of pregnant women \[[66](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_066)\]. In order to extract features, methods for midline detection and template matching were used. The goal was to offer a concise depiction of the ultrasound image. The SVM model was employed for bone and interspinous image classification in an effort to optimize the margins. Thus, the approach yielded a test set success rate of 93%. Upon conducting additional tests on ultrasound video streams, it was seen that the method under consideration accurately recognized the location in 45 out of the total 46 cases. The primary goal of the study was to identify the optimal site for epidural anesthesia administration. However, it is worth noting that no existing research has been identified regarding the dosage of anesthesia.

## 10.7 Mode of delivery

A study was carried out by researchers to determine the elements that put women at risk of having cesarean sections \[[67](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_067)\]. A total of 488 participants were selected in the classification analysis, utilizing a set of 23 variables. These variables encompassed a range of factors such as blood pressure, maternal age, hemoglobin levels, mode of last birth, history of miscarriages and abortions, presence of hypertension, diabetes status, medication usage, symptoms of heavy breathing, folic acid intake, headache, and bodily discomfort, among others. Among the classifiers tested – which included k-NN, linear discriminant analysis, naive Bayes, SVM, and RF – 10-fold cross-validation revealed that RF was the best fit due to its superior accuracy, precision, and recall. Research shows that maternal age has a significant impact on the expected delivery method, which in turn is influenced by the prior birth mode. The research incorporated medicines as a variable, examining risk variables associated with cesarean sections. The medication did not demonstrate a meaningful impact in their model.

### 10.7.1 Predict postpartum disease

Maternal impacts of GDM encompass subsequent postpartum occurrences of a seven-fold amplified susceptibility ultimately leading to the onset of type 2 diabetes (T2D) as well as an elevated likelihood of experiencing metabolic syndrome and cardiovascular disease \[[68](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_068)\]. Several studies have utilized ML techniques to enhance the prediction of hypoglycemia or hyperglycemia in females diagnosed with GDM after childbirth. These studies have introduced feature selection techniques and unique ML algorithms as potential approaches to achieve such improvement. The application of a supervised learning algorithm was utilized by researchers to ascertain the presence or potential development of DM in pregnant women \[[69](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_069)\]. Examining the potential use of the artificial immune recognition system (AIRS) in predicting the commencement of DM subsequent to GDM was the primary objective of this study. The AIRS algorithm draws inspiration from both natural and artificial immune system operations, incorporating elements such as competition for resources, clone selection, mutation, maturation, and the development of memory cells \[[70](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_070)\]. An AIRS works by viewing training and test data as antigens; this triggers a reaction from the system’s B-cells, which in turn generate artificial recognition balls (ARBs). The ARBs engage in a competitive interaction to acquire the specified resource quantity. ARBs possessing greater resources will be allowed increased opportunities to generate mutant progeny, hence enhancing the mechanism. In order to classify the test data, memory cells are produced after all training antigens have been shown. Researchers developed a novel feature selection method by combining the EM algorithm with the nearest neighbor classifier \[[71](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_071)\]. For Taiwanese women whose pregnancies have been complicated by GDM, the researchers showed that the proposed methodology is useful for predicting the onset of T2D. The researchers conducted an evaluation on an ML system called CNN. The objective was to assess the predictive efficacy of the CNN in determining the probability of women diagnosed with GDM developing T2D in subsequent periods \[[72](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_072)\]. Although the CNN model demonstrates great performance with 1,000 neurons, it does not offer any interpretable evidence for predicting T2D.

## 10.8 Pharmacologics and pregnancy

There have been a number of efforts to use expert systems to improve the delivery of pharmaceutical information and patient counseling support. In 1994 the researchers introduced a prototype knowledge system with the aim of assisting patients during their interactions with healthcare professionals in community pharmacies \[[73](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_073)\]. Within this method, pieces of informations are categorized and organized based on their level of significance. These categories include important items, potentially important items, and the remaining objects. In a research, scholars have put forth alternative approaches to address the challenges associated with the tedious task of creating and managing rule-based systems.

Medicinal medications administered in the first, second, or third trimesters of pregnancy can have their potential fetal toxicity predicted using a method devised by researchers using ML techniques. The ML approach utilized a methodology known as “random forests” to acquire knowledge from previous outcome studies on drugs that have been identified as posing risks to the fetus. The US-FDA has already recognized several medications as contraindicated in pregnancy, categorizing them as either category D or X. The model additionally incorporated data regarding pharmaceuticals that have been previously deemed safe for the fetus based on outcome studies as well as their categorization by the FDA as either category A or B. The AI methodology successfully predicted the relative likelihood of fetal toxicity for medications falling within the intermediate or uncertain fetal toxicity category (i.e., FDA Category C pharmaceuticals). The researchers employed a technique that integrated data on the drug’s chemical composition with information regarding the medicine’s demonstrated ability to target a vitamin gene or a known Mendelian disease gene. The goal was to increase the strategy’s effectiveness \[[74](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_074)\]. This study employed a comprehensive manual review approach to determine the importance of understanding the Mendelian disease genes and the pharmacological chemicals that impact them, including any unintentional targets or “off-targets,” with respect to the effects for the fetus \[[75](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_075)\]. Therefore, the process was vital in shaping the methodology that followed in terms of ML.

The researchers of the study put up a proposed recommendation system called PARS for the purpose of antibiotic prescribing \[[76](#9783111547589_017408.xhtml#b_9783111549729-010_ref_076)\]. While the primary intention of such recommendation system does not revolve around pregnancy-related matters, it does incorporate a specific use case scenario involving an individual who is pregnant. The system’s functionality is dependent on the use of three separate ontologies: an ontology focused to antibiotics, an ontology concentrating on infections, and an ontology assigned to patients. The data is then used in two different levels of logical analysis, resulting in a conclusive compilation of antibiotic medicines adapted to the person’s individual needs. To give treatment choices based on procedures that are based on knowledge, the CDSS combines ontologies with the multicriteria decision analysis (MCDA) method. The choice to use an ontology-focused method instead of ML was made because ML doesn’t do well at giving thorough explanations yet, which is very important in medicine. Using ontologies and MCDA gives a complete explanation along with a treatment suggestion that doesn’t rely on a training set. The PARS is primarily designed to check if antibiotic prescriptions for bacterial illnesses are suitable, and it can only be used in this setting. It might not be the right way to look at antibiotic use in other situations. Additionally, PARS thinks about how harmful antibiotics can be, but it fails to consider about how these treatments might combine with other medicines when they are given at the same time as the patient’s other ongoing treatment. To fix the problems with the CDSS, the experts want to add more ontologies.

## 10.9 Conclusion

In conclusion, AI is changing the way pregnancy care is provided by finding complications earlier, providing more personalized help, and making diagnoses more accurate. By allowing for timely measures and ongoing support, AI in maternal health ensures that pregnancies are safer and more comfortable. It is very important that ethical problems are given the most attention as technological progress is made to ensure that privacy and consent stay at the forefront of these breakthroughs. The future of pregnancy care appears increasingly hopeful with AI, targeting improved health outcomes for both mothers and infants.
